# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## What this is

A fan remake of Pokémon Platinum in C# (.NET 10) on Raylib-cs 8.1 (raylib 6.0), built toward the full game over many sessions. The roadmap lives in `docs/plans/` (start with `docs/plans/README.md`; `docs/plans/00-overview.md` is the order of what is left, in waves of sessions that run side by side); the art rules live in `docs/art/style-guide.md`. Read the relevant plan before continuing roadmap work, and tick its status checklist and record decisions in it when a session ends.

The git repository is this folder. The parent folder has the same name and is not a repository; sessions often start there.

## Commands

Run from this folder.

```bash
dotnet build PokemonPlatinum.sln
dotnet test PokemonPlatinumTests
dotnet test PokemonPlatinumTests --filter "FullyQualifiedName~TestCatchRateFormula"   # one test
dotnet run --project PokemonPlatinumEngine                                             # play
dotnet run --project PokemonPlatinumEngine -- --region Kanto                          # new games start in Kanto (FireRed's Pallet Town, plan 19) instead of Sinnoh
dotnet run --project PokemonPlatinumEngine -- --help                                  # every switch: --windowed, --fullscreen, --safe-mode, --verify, --log <path>, --dev
dotnet run --project PokemonPlatinumEngine -- --where                                 # where the saves, the options and the log are kept, and why: nothing is opened, made or moved
dotnet run --project PokemonPlatinumEngine -- --save <folder>                         # keep them in this folder (--portable: in the folder the game is started in)
dotnet run --project tools/DataImporter                                               # regenerate species/moves/abilities/items/trainers JSON
dotnet run --project tools/DataImporter -- --check-entries                            # the Pokédex's written entries against the games' own (PokeAPI's flavour texts, in the cache): every run of six words shared, by species; ends with 1 on a finding and writes nothing
dotnet run --project tools/MapImporter -- [--quick] [--out <dir>]                     # Sinnoh's maps from the decompilation: pictures, world files, reports
dotnet run --project tools/MapImporter -- --data                                      # rewrite the world files the game loads (Data/world/sinnoh), after opening an area
dotnet run --project tools/MapImporter -- --room <key>                                # a room of the original (contest_hall_lobby): its grid, props, people, warps and triggers, to build a room file from
dotnet run --project tools/MapImporter -- --game firered|emerald [--quick]             # a GBA game's maps (plan 18 · W6, W7) into out/kanto or out/hoenn: mosaics by cover, behaviour and height, every map, world files with events, wild Pokémon, trainers and spawns, report.md
dotnet run --project tools/MapImporter -- --game firered|emerald --data                # what Data/world/<region>/world.json opens, written beside it (nothing until a region has one)
dotnet run --project tools/MapImporter -- --game firered|emerald --unknown <Map>       # a GBA map's metatiles no look names yet, each with its open and blocked tiles and where the first stands: what GbaCover's rows are judged by (plan 19 · K6, part b)
dotnet run --project tools/MapImporter -- --game heartgold [--quick] [--data]           # HeartGold's Johto and Kanto (plan 20 · J1): out/johto's mosaics, area pictures, world files and report; --data what Data/world/johto/world.json opens, with Johto's trainers, phone book and habitats
dotnet run --project tools/MapImporter -- --game heartgold --unknown <area>             # the textures no rule of HgCover sorts on an area's chunks, with how many tiles each covers (plan 20 · J8, part b): what a new area's cover rules are written from
dotnet run --project tools/MapImporter -- --game black2 [--quick]                      # Black 2's Unova (plan 22 · U1), from fuddlesworth/pokebw2 (GPL v3.0), into out/unova: mosaics by cover, behaviour and height, a picture per zone, report.md with the tile classes and the textures no rule sorts by the tiles they lie over; each model.glb's binary chunk is read as numbers only to find which texture covers each tile (plan 22 · U2)
dotnet run --project tools/MapImporter -- --game black2 --data                         # what Data/world/unova/world.json opens, written beside it: the areas' events, wild Pokémon by season, trainers, habitats and the rooms (plan 22 · U2)
dotnet run --project tools/MapImporter -- --game black2 --chunk <folder>               # a Black 2 chunk's looks and the textures on and over each tile, with how Bw2Cover sorts them (overworld_2_map12_13)
dotnet run --project tools/MapImporter -- --chunk <id>                                # a chunk's textures tile by tile, ground and above, and how Cover.cs sorts each name
dotnet run --project tools/ContentCheck                                               # the hand-written files held to their rules: every overlay, room and script, a line to a finding (the file, the thing, what is wrong); ends with 1 on a finding
dotnet run --project tools/ContentCheck -- --plan <out dir> <Room or area key> ...    # a room (PlayerHouse) or an open area (twinleaf_town) from above, its props, warps, people, signs and triggers tagged and named in a key: PNGs to open with the Read tool
dotnet run --project tools/ContentCheck -- --plan <out dir> Sinnoh@96,864,20,12       # a rectangle of a map's tiles alone (x, y, width, height); --tile 64 draws a tile that many pixels wide
dotnet run --project tools/SaveTool -- dump <save> [--all]                           # a save written out: who, where, the team, the boxes, the bag, the story's size (plan 12 · Q3; the tool's README)
dotnet run --project tools/SaveTool -- validate <save>... [--errors]                  # a save held to the data (species, moves, items, levels, IVs, places, versions, the file's checksum); ends with 1 on an error
dotnet run --project tools/SaveTool -- migrate <save> [--out <file>]                 # what the game's migrations (SaveMigration, SaveData.PlaceOn, StoryMigration) do to it, line by line; --out writes the copy, never over the save
dotnet run --project tools/SaveTool -- diff <save> <other save>                      # every place two saves differ; ends with 1 when they do
dotnet run --project tools/ContentCheck -- --rows "<line>"                            # a line as the text box breaks it (\n for a break), row by row with each row's width
dotnet run --project tools/TextTool -- extract <tag> [--update]                       # a language's scripts/, world/ and maps/ translation files: every English line beside an empty text (plan 17 · N4)
dotnet run --project tools/TextTool -- report [tag] [--lines] [--check]               # what a language has left untranslated, stale or unknown, per file; rewrites docs/localisation.md
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> [all|field|lineup|battle|doubles|demo|arenas|flow|menus|intro|evolution|look|title|terrain|buildings|lab|life|world|times|sheets|pokemon|story|opening|jubilife|windworks|eterna|veilstone|celestic|rooms|distortion|boutique] [before dir]
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> area <key>                        # one shot of an area of the imported world (twinleaf_town, route_201…)
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> area <region>:<key>               # an area of a GBA import (kanto:pallet_town, from tools/MapImporter/out/<region>/world or SHOTS_IMPORT), and inside each room its doors lead into
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> cities [key ...]                  # every town of Sinnoh from the importer's last full run: a shot per building and prop, a sheet per town
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> cities johto [key ...]            # the same for Johto, from the importer's last full `--game heartgold` run (tools/MapImporter/out/johto/world)
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> dex [--back] [species ...]        # boards of every species' 3D model (or the species and forms named), sixty to a page; --back from behind; --prints one board of every species' footprint, 94_prints (plan 09 · L9)
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> export [species ...]              # species' models as .glb files, to refine in Blender and drop into overrides/models
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> pokemon [species ...]             # the named species' turntables on one board (91_turntables), for sculpting
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> versus <mine> <foe> [...]         # a wild battle for each pair (species or forms), the first leading the player's party
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> moves [move ...]                  # boards of Platinum's 467 moves at their landing, sixty to a board (mv_NN), each with its quads and whose look it is (a batch's, G8's or T, its type's template); given moves, four moments of each (mv_<key>_1 to _4; MOVES_AGES=0.2,0.3 asks for others)
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> cast [look ...]                  # every look of characters.json turning, twenty to a board (56_cast_NN), and the named cast in battle (57_cast_battle_<look>)
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> conditions                        # a battle's passing states on screen: a flight, the platform empty and the Pokémon back; a move of several hits; a Ditto transformed; the weathers and the Substitute's doll; hazards, screens, veils, the rooms and what is sent ahead; the turn's moments (poison, a burn, Leech Seed, a bind, a curse, an item going off, the marsh's bait) and an attack sent ahead landing; and a condition that lasts on each Pokémon that has it (asleep, poisoned, burned, paralysed, frozen, confused, in love)
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> profile                           # where a frame goes: the heavy scenes timed and taken apart pass by pass
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> fieldmoves                        # the field moves at work: a tree cut, a boulder pushed, a cave lit, Fly, Surf, the Pokétch, a rod, and last Fly between Sinnoh and Kanto (plan 18 · W9)
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> encounters                        # the wild Pokémon beyond the tables: the Poké Radar, a honey tree, poison, a swarm, a roamer, Feebas
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> gyms                              # the Gyms of plan 01 · M9 and their puzzles: the flower clock turning, the bags kicked, the dark rooms and their doors
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> daycare                           # the Day Care: two Pokémon left, an Egg found, carried and hatched, and Cynthia's Egg
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> poffins                           # Poffins: the fan club's case, the Poffin House's pot stirred through its stages, the case, a Poffin fed, the condition page
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> contests                          # a Super Contest: the Contest Hall's counter, the line-up, the dressing-up, the dance, the acting, the results and the ribbon; a practice round
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> capsules                          # Ball Capsules, Seals and ribbons: the Seal Case's givers, the capsules' screen, a burst in battle, the ribbons' page, the Fashion Case
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> league [room ...]                 # the League's rooms: the guard, a lift rising, the Elite Four's doors and battles, Cynthia, the Hall of Fame
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> lakes                             # the seventh chapter: Riley and Iron Island, Canalave's library and the explosion, Saturn at Lake Valor, Mars at Lake Verity, the way north
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> battlezone [Map,x,y[,night] ...]   # the Battle Zone and the rest of the post-game, Stark Mountain to the Battle Frontier (the end of `world`); given places, a shot of each instead
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> snowpoint                         # the eighth chapter's first half: the rival at Acuity Lakefront, Snowpoint City, Maylene, Candice's Badge, Rock Climb, Jupiter at Lake Acuity
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> headquarters                      # the eighth chapter's second half: the Storage Key and Looker, the warehouse's door, Team Galactic's headquarters floor by floor, its warp panels, Cyrus, Saturn and the button
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> underground                       # the Underground: the way down, a wall dug through, every trap, a Sphere buried, the hikers, the secret base and its decorating, a flag brought home
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> pillar                            # the ninth chapter: Team Galactic up Mt. Coronet, Spear Pillar and the tag battle, Cyrus taken, the Distortion World floor by floor, its boulders, Giratina, Sendoff Spring
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> pastimes [part ...]               # fossils, the Game Corner, the Villa and Pal Park (museum, corner, villa, park): a Fossil revived, a slot machine through a win and the bonus game, the Villa furnished and visited, a Catching Show
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> sunyshore                         # the tenth chapter: the professor's lab, the lakes' three caverns and their guardians, Flint in Sunyshore City, the Vista Lighthouse and its lift, Volkner, the Beacon Badge, the rival and Jasmine on the beach
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> tower                             # the Battle Tower: its lobby and counters, a team entered and turned away, the lift, a streak of seven, the Tower Tycoon, a partner out of the Battle Salon, the Exchange Service Corner
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> factory                           # the Battle Factory: its lobby and counter, the staff's hint, the six rentals and a summary, the three chosen, a battle, a trade, the round's Battle Points, a challenge put down, the Factory Head
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> champion                          # the eleventh chapter: Victory Road, the rival before the League's door, the Hall of Fame's scene, the team's entry, the credits, home and Mom's news
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> postgame                          # after the Hall of Fame, part a: the lab's rating, Professor Oak's upgrade and the Poké Radar's lesson, Pal Park's lobby, the ship, the Fight Area's tag battle, Route 227, the Survival Area, Marley in Victory Road
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> hall                              # the Battle Hall: its lobby and counter, the record keeper, one Pokémon entered, the stage, the board of types, a battle, the round's Battle Points, a challenge put down, two of a kind, the Hall Matron
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> castle                            # the Battle Castle: its lobby and counter, three entered, the valet's room and his two counters, the throne room, a battle and its Castle Points, the round's Battle Points, a challenge put down, a battle passed, the Castle Valet
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> interiors [town ...] [work] [workb] [workc] [workd] [worke]   # the rooms of plan 01 · M11: every room of the towns named from its door, a sheet per town, and a few of them at work
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> legends                           # after the Hall of Fame, part b: Stark Mountain from the grunts at its foot to Charon's arrest and the parting, Heatran, a rematch at the League, Snowpoint Temple and Regigigas, the sailor's son, Fullmoon Island and Cresselia
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> arcade                            # the Battle Arcade: its lobby and counter, three entered, the corridor, the game room, the game board counting, running and stopped, an event and the battle under it, a round's events and its Battle Points, a challenge put down, a fifth round's bonuses, the Arcade Star
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> townmap                           # the Town Map: out of the bag, the cursor on a town, a route, the sea and a landmark, the way come, zoomed in, a hidden island before and after, Fly's choice, a wall's map, the picture, the Pokédex's area page
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> epilogue                          # after the Hall of Fame, part c: Rotom in the Old Chateau's television, the elder's book, Dialga's and Palkia's pillars, the Battleground, its guests and their rematches, Cynthia before the ruins' painting
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> amity                             # Amity Square's walk: the gate, the counter's check for a team that may not and one that may, a Pokémon let out and the walk with it through every turn, its mood, a find, the ruins' huts, the man of the park, the goodbye, every species behind the player, a game saved on a walk taken up again
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> saves                             # no shots: saves of today's game (a new game, past the first chapter, surfing, on a bridge, a Safari Game, the girl) into <out dir>/saves, for the tests' corpus of every version
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> fulldex                           # the game with every species in it: the start's steps, the National Pokédex walked whole, the boxes full, a save of it all, forty battles, each with the memory and the models, sprites and cries held
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> crash [thread]                    # the crash handler at work: an exception thrown on purpose in the game's own guarded loop, the report, the crash save and the log it leaves in <out dir>, the next start's notice
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> formats                           # the formats of the later games (plan 14): an inverse battle from the Fight Area's scientist, his offer refused and taken, the battle's opening line and hints, a resisted hit, an immunity and a weakness turned round, a win, the next day, a loss
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> skips                             # no shots: four of the story's scenes played and then skipped by the hold (plan 12 · Q20), and what each way left told side by side
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> ways                              # the ways to play (plan 13), each in a new game begun under its run: a capped Turtwig held at Roark's level and the line that says so (wy_level_cap_menu, wy_level_cap_line), Roark under Normal and under Challenge (wy_roark_normal, wy_roark_challenge)
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> kanto                             # Kanto as FireRed's maps make it (plan 19): a new game begun there, Pallet Town, Route 1, Viridian City, Route 2, Viridian Forest, Route 22, Pewter City, Route 3, Route 4, Mt. Moon, Cerulean City, Nugget Bridge and Route 25 by day and after dark, their rooms furnished, the walk from the player's door to Viridian's Pokémon Center timed, and the chapters' scenes (ks01 to ks17: Oak in the grass, the three Poké Balls, the first battle, the Parcel, the Pokédex, the rival on Route 22, the Gym's guide, Brock and the Boulder Badge, the Running Shoes; ks18 to ks29: Miguel and the fossils, the rival at Nugget Bridge, the bridge's prize, Bill and the S.S. Ticket, Misty and the Cascade Badge, Kanto's badges on the Trainer Card; plan 19 · K7's kj0 to kl1 and ksb0 to kse1: Viridian's Gym, Route 23, Victory Road, the League's rooms and One Island, Bill and the Seagallop, Giovanni, the rival, the guards, the Four, the Champion and the Hall of Fame; plan 19 · K9, part a's Kindle Road, Mt. dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> johto                             # Johto as HeartGold's maps make it (plan 20 · J2): a new game begun there, New Bark Town to Violet City by day and after dark (jw*), every room furnished (jr*), and the walk from the player's door to Cherrygrove's Pokémon Center timed; then the first chapter played by its own scripts (plan 20 · J4: jst00 the bedroom to jst24 Route 32, Mom, Elm's machine, the rival in Cherrygrove, Sprout Tower, Falkner and the Zephyr Badge), then the Pokégear (plan 20 · J3: pg01 the phone to pg05 Oak's talk on the radio), the centre's places (J5), the west's, the north's and the way to the League (J8, J10; `JOHTO_FROM=west`, `north` or `league` shoots one alone), and last the second chapter in a new game of its own (plan 20 · J7: jst30 the grunt by the Slowpoke Well to jst59 the Kimono Girls, through Proton, Bugsy, the Farfetch'd, the strange tree, the Burned Tower and Morty; JOHTO_FROM=well plays it alone), and on into the third (plan 20 · J9: jst60 the rival at Olivine's Gym to jst79 Fly over Johto, through Jasmine and Amphy, the lighthouse's lift, the pharmacy, Chuck's winch and Badge, Fly, Suicune and Eusine, the Mineral Badge and the Moomoo Farm; JOHTO_FROM=sea plays it alone), and on into the fourth's part a (plan 20 · J11: jst80 the toll on Route 43 to jst8m the Glacier Badge, through the red Gyarados, Lance, the souvenir shop's stairs, the Team Rocket Hideout, Petrel, the password, Ariana's tag battle beside Lance, the generator, a block of ice and Pryce; JOHTO_FROM=rage plays it alone), and its part b (plan 20 · J11, part b: jst90 Elm's call to jst9q Goldenrod free, through the city taken, the uniform under the city, the rival at the tower, Petrel, the Basement Key, the rival under the city, the switches and gates, the real Director, the shutters, the lift, Archer and the Rainbow Wing; JOHTO_FROM=takeover plays it alone)
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> hoenn                             # Hoenn as Emerald's maps make it (plan 21 · H1): a new game begun there, Littleroot Town, Route 101 and Oldale Town by day and after dark (h01 to h06), Routes 102 and 103 and Petalburg City (h07 to h10), the three towns' rooms furnished (h11 to h18), Route 104, Petalburg Woods, Rustboro City, Route 116, Rusturf Tunnel and their rooms (h19 to h32), the walk from the family's door to Petalburg's Pokémon Center timed, and last the first chapter played by its own scripts in a new game of its own (plan 21 · H2, hs01 to hs23: the van, Mom, the clock, the rival next door, Birch on Route 101 and his bag, the first battle, the rival on Route 103, the Pokédex, the Running Shoes, Norman and Wally's catch; and on, plan 21 · H2, part b, hs24 to hs48: the grunt in the woods, Roxanne and the Stone Badge, the Devon Goods snatched, Mr. Briney, the grunt in the tunnel, Mr. Stone's PokéNav, Match Call and its pages, the rival in Rustboro, the Cutter, Wanda's young man)
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> johto                             # Johto as HeartGold's maps make it (plan 20 · J2): a new game begun there, New Bark Town to Violet City by day and after dark (jw*), every room furnished (jr*), and the walk from the player's door to Cherrygrove's Pokémon Center timed; then the first chapter played by its own scripts (plan 20 · J4: jst00 the bedroom to jst24 Route 32, Mom, Elm's machine, the rival in Cherrygrove, Sprout Tower, Falkner and the Zephyr Badge), and last the Pokégear (plan 20 · J3: pg01 the phone to pg05 Oak's talk on the radio)
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> hoenn                             # Hoenn as Emerald's maps make it (plan 21 · H1): a new game begun there, Littleroot Town, Route 101 and Oldale Town by day and after dark (h01 to h06), Routes 102 and 103 and Petalburg City (h07 to h10), the three towns' rooms furnished (h11 to h18), Route 104, Petalburg Woods, Rustboro City, Route 116, Rusturf Tunnel and their rooms (h19 to h32), Mr. Briney's jetties, Dewford Town, Granite Cave, Route 109's beach, Slateport City and their rooms (h33 to h50, Dewford and Slateport after dark h51, h52), Route 110, the Trick House, the Cycling Road's gate, Mauville City, Route 117, the Day Care and Verdanturf Town with their rooms (h53 to h67, Mauville and Verdanturf after dark h68, h69), the walk from the family's door to Petalburg's Pokémon Center timed, and last the first chapter played by its own scripts in a new game of its own (plan 21 · H2, hs01 to hs23: the van, Mom, the clock, the rival next door, Birch on Route 101 and his bag, the first battle, the rival on Route 103, the Pokédex, the Running Shoes, Norman and Wally's catch; and on, plan 21 · H2, part b, hs24 to hs48: the grunt in the woods, Roxanne and the Stone Badge, the Devon Goods snatched, Mr. Briney, the grunt in the tunnel, Mr. Stone's PokéNav, Match Call and its pages, the rival in Rustboro, the Cutter, Wanda's young man; and on, plan 21 · H3, part a, hs49 to hs72: Mr. Briney's boat and the sea, Dewford's jetty, the hiker's Flash, Steven, Brawly's dark Gym lit trainer by trainer, the Knuckle Badge, the sail to Route 109, Team Aqua at the museum, Dock, the fee, Captain Stern, the grunts, Archie, Scott; and on, plan 21 · H3, part b, hs73 to hs98: the rival on Route 110 and the Itemfinder, the Trick House's first puzzle and its reward, Wally at Mauville's Gym and Scott, Rydel's Mach Bike, Wattson's barriers flipped, his battle and the Dynamo Badge, Rusturf Tunnel cleared and Wanda's house, the Mach Bike on the Cycling Road and the Acro Bike on Route 117; and on, plan 21 · H4, part a, hs99 to hs113: the Winstrates one after another and the Macho Brace, the desert's storm turning the player back and seen from inside, Team Magma's grunts on Route 112, Route 113's ash and the Soot Sack, Lanette in Fallarbor, Team Magma and Archie at Meteor Falls, Mauville's Game Corner; with the places of H3, part c and H4, part a, h70 to h91: Routes 111 to 114, the desert in its storm, Fiery Path, Fallarbor Town, Meteor Falls and their rooms, the Game Corner, the desert, Route 113 and Fallarbor after dark; and last Dive, plan 21 · H5, h92 to h99: Route 124's dark water, the question, the sea floor by day and after dark, a battle in its kelp on the sea floor's own stage, a breath's bubbles rising and bursting and the waves' light moved on; and Route 118 to Fortree, plan 21 · H6, part a: Routes 118 to 120, the Weather Institute, Fortree City in its treetops and its Gym's gates, h100 to h119 with Fortree and Route 119's rain after dark, and its story, hs114 to hs139: Steven on Route 118, Shelly at the Weather Institute and the Castform, the rival on Route 119 and HM02, the Kecleon nobody sees, the Devon Scope, Winona and the Feather Badge; and Mossdeep City, plan 21 · H8, part a: its streets, Gym, Space Center, rooms and the sea round it, h165 to h179, and its story, hs200 to hs226: Scott, a switch of the Gym turning its arrows, Tate and Liza and the Mind Badge, Team Magma filing into the Space Center, the grunts and the battle beside Steven against Maxie and Tabitha, HM09, Dive, the Wingull's errand and the treasure hunter)
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> pokegear                          # the Pokégear by itself (plan 20 · J3), on a game of Johto past its opening: the phone, Joey ringing on his day for a rematch, the map card and the map it opens, the radio's March and Oak's talk, the March given up for the area's theme
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> contest                           # the Bug-Catching Contest (plan 15 · E5): a Tuesday in Johto, Route 36's gate counter, the park's corner, a Sport Ball battle and its catch, the swap's question, the minutes run out, the board of results and the prize (ct01 to ct07)
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> athlon                            # the Pokéathlon (plan 15 · E6): Route 35's gate before the dome's door, the clerk's question, the course choice, the team, each event's briefing, count, play and points (Hurdle Dash, Block Smash, Ring Drop), the scoreboard and the clerk after it (at01 to at07b)
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> unova                             # Unova as Black 2's maps make it (plan 22 · U2): a new game begun there, Aspertia City, Route 19, Floccesy Town and Floccesy Ranch by day and after dark (u01 to u08), Route 20 and a few rooms (u10 to u16), and the walk from the player's door to Floccesy Town's Pokémon Center timed, and last the first chapter in a new game of its own (plan 22 · U5, part a: us01 Mom's questions to us30 Cheren's advice on Route 20)
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <out dir> seasons                           # Unova's seasons (plan 22 · U3): Route 19 and Floccesy Town in each of the four (season_<name>_route19, _floccesy), the sign's second line, and Twinleaf Town in spring and with winter fixed, which must be the same picture
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <dir> diff <other dir>                      # two runs' shots compared pixel by pixel: what a change did to the picture
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <dir> contact [prefix]                      # every shot of a run on sheets of twenty
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <dir> crop <shot> <x> <y> <w> <h> <scale> [other dir ...]   # a rectangle of a shot enlarged, beside the same of other runs
dotnet run --project tools/MusicRender -- <out dir> [song id or folder ...] [--night] [--stems]                    # songs to WAV, with level, loudness and clash checks (--sounds, --cries, --ambience for the rest)
dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <dir> ab <before> <after> <before again>  # three runs of `profile` compared, scene by scene

bash tools/dev/session-start.sh                                   # a machine with no .NET 10 SDK gets one (the cloud's SessionStart hook runs it)
tools/dev/shots.sh <out dir> <mode> [filter] [more...]            # the harness in Release; under xvfb with software GL on Linux
tools/dev/baseline.sh <commit> [--only <path>...] <mode>...       # a commit's shots against the working tree's, each mode diffed
tools/dev/ab.sh <commit> [words]                                  # profile in the commit, the working tree and the commit again (Windows only)
tools/dev/bisect.sh <mode> <shot> <good> <bad>                    # the first commit that moved a shot
```

- `tools/DataImporter` and `tools/MapImporter` are in the solution; `tools/ShotHarness` and `tools/MusicRender` are not, so `dotnet build PokemonPlatinum.sln` doesn't compile them. Build them too after renaming or removing engine members.
- Both importers fetch a pinned commit of the decompilation (set once, in `tools/DataImporter/Sources.cs`) into their own git-ignored `.cache` folder the first time they run; the map importer's `--game` fetches pinned commits of pret/pokefirered and pret/pokeemerald the same way (plan 18 · W6: their maps, layouts, tilesets' attributes and two headers, never their graphics). `tools/MapImporter` writes to its git-ignored `out/` folder and takes about twelve seconds; open its PNGs with the Read tool. The test project references it, so its readers are tested without the decompilation.
- `tools/SaveTool` (plan 12 · Q3) is in the solution and referenced by the test project too: its classes (`SaveChecks`, `SaveDump`, `SaveMigrate`, `SaveDiff`) are what `SaveToolTests` drives; it opens no window and never writes over a save.
- `tools/ContentCheck` (plan 16 · T8) is in the solution too and referenced by the test project, and reaches the engine's internal classes as the harness does (`InternalsVisibleTo`). It opens no window, reads the `Data` folder beside its executable (or `--data <dir>`) in about two seconds, and writes nothing but the plans it is asked for, into the folder it is given. Its checks are classes (`ContentCheck.Checks`), which `ContentTests` runs over the game's own files and over a small world it breaks on purpose; "The content check", under "Data", says what they hold.
- **Where the game keeps its files** (plan 12 · Q2, `Core/SavePaths.cs`): the three slots' saves (`save1.json` to `save3.json`, each with the save before it as `save1.bak`, and `save1.json.tmp` while it is written), `settings.json`, the log and a crash's files are in one folder, which `SavePaths.Resolve` chooses, the first of these that holds: `--save <folder>`; `--portable` (the folder the game is started in); a `portable.txt`, or a file the game itself wrote (an old `savegame.json` or its backup, a slot's save or backup, `autosave.json`, `settings.json`), in the folder it is started in, which is how `dotnet run --project PokemonPlatinumEngine` from the repository keeps finding the save it had there; a `portable.txt` beside the executable; otherwise the user's own folder (`%APPDATA%\PokemonPlatinum`, `$XDG_DATA_HOME/pokemon-platinum` or `~/.local/share/pokemon-platinum`, `~/Library/Application Support/PokemonPlatinum`). `--where` says which and why and touches nothing. The harness names its `<out dir>` outright (and writes a `portable.txt` there, and stops before any game is made if the game's own rules would keep the saves anywhere else), so it never touches a real save. **A session or a tool that runs the game itself gives it `--save <scratch folder>` (or `--portable` from a scratch folder) every time, after `--where` with the same switches has shown where it would write**: started with neither, from a folder that holds none of the game's files, it writes in the user's own folder, where their saves are. And no test finds that folder: a test hands `SavePaths.Resolve` a made-up machine (`SaveEnvironment`) whose folders are its own temporary ones.
- No formatter is configured. CLAUDE.md's bans of single calls are build errors (plan 24 · X1): `Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.BannedApiAnalyzers` on the engine, its list in `PokemonPlatinumEngine/BannedSymbols.txt` (an unseeded `Random`, `Random.Shared`, `Raylib.GetTime`, a string's `GetHashCode`, `HashCode`, `DateTime.Now`, `Today` and `UtcNow`, `Raylib.GetShaderLocation`, `ToggleBorderlessWindowed`, `UnloadMaterial`), RS0030 an error. The places allowed are four, each a `#pragma warning disable RS0030` with its reason: `Core/Dice.cs` (chance), `Graphics/FrameClock.cs` (raylib's clock), `Core/WallClock.cs` (the computer's clock: `WallClock.Local` and `UtcNow`, which a tool fixes with `WallClock.Fixed`; the hour and the date stay `GameClock`'s, read through it) and `FieldShaders.Location` (every uniform's location, which `UiShapes`, `UiFonts` and `SkinnedModel` ask too). The tools are outside it. `ArchitectureTests` reads the engine's IL for the rules of where things may be used (each engine file's namespace is its folder; Battle.Sim, Battle.Effects, Story, Models and Data use no raylib type but a `Color`, and none of `InputManager`, `AudioManager`, `BattleAnimator`, `BattleEngine`, `BattleHUD`, `BattleRenderer`; Overworld no raylib type outside `DialogueManager`; a battle makes no `Random` but a `BattleRandom` and reads `Dice` only in `BattleCore`'s constructor; the sculptors use no `System.Random`; Graphics asks `Map.HeightAt` only in `Relief` and the two readers `ArchitectureTests.WalkedHeights` names; Battle reads `Pokemon.Ability` only in `Battler.Ability`), and runs each rule over probes of its own (`ArchitectureProbes.cs`) to show it finds a break.
- **The console is the log's** (plan 16 · T3): `System.Console` is on the banned list too, and a fifth place is allowed beside the four above, `Core/Diagnostics/Log.cs`. The engine says what it has to say with `Log.Info`, `Log.Warn` or `Log.Error` (a warning and an error are still shown on the console; every line is kept for `log.txt` and a crash report), and what a switch prints for the console alone (`--help`, `--verify`) with `Log.Say`. `ArchitectureTests` also holds `Core.Diagnostics` and the command line (`CommandLine`, `LaunchOptions`, `Switch`) free of raylib.
- Beside the save the game also writes `log.txt` (and `log.1.txt`, the log before it, once the first has reached two megabytes), and after a crash `logs/crash-<date>.txt`, `logs/crash.pending` and `savegame.crash.json` (with `savegame.crash.bak`): "The command line, the log and the crash handler", below. All are git-ignored. The harness and the tests open no log file, so their runs write none of these; only the harness's `crash` mode does, on purpose, in its out dir.
- **Two kinds of machine** (plan 16 · T16). The Windows machine (a GTX 970, Git Bash, no Python, no `gh` CLI) is where timings are read; Git Bash heredocs containing apostrophes or non-ASCII text tend to fail there, so write such content with the Write tool. A cloud session runs on Linux: `.claude/settings.json`'s SessionStart hook runs `tools/dev/session-start.sh`, which installs the .NET 10 SDK where there is none (the distribution's package as root, else Microsoft's installer into `~/.dotnet`) and restores the solution and the harness; `.devcontainer/devcontainer.json` does the same for Codespaces. There the harness draws with Mesa's llvmpipe under `xvfb` (`tools/dev/shots.sh`), slowly at 4K, so shoot what changed with a filter; its milliseconds mean nothing (`ab.sh` refuses), a picture is compared only with one from the same kind of machine, and the decompilation's and the models' caches fill on the first run. The scripts in `tools/dev` are bash, never Python, and keep LF endings (`.gitattributes`).
- **One cache for several trees**: `POKEMON_CACHE` names a folder that holds the meshed models and baked sprites (`<cache>/models`, `<cache>/sprites`; `Graphics/CacheFolders.cs`) in place of `cache/` beside the executable, and in it nothing is deleted for being older, so trees at different commits share it. The `tools/dev` scripts set it to `~/.cache/pokemon-platinum` unless it is set, and keep the commits' worktrees and shots under it (`dev/`, or `DEV_SCRATCH`); the folder can be removed whole at any time.

## Checking visual work

Claude can't see the game window. `tools/ShotHarness` runs the engine in a hidden 1920×1080 window, drives it into known states and saves PNGs of the virtual screen (halved to 1920×1080; set `SHOTS_4K=1` for the full 3840×2160, or use `ShotCrop` for a native-resolution crop). Write its output to a scratch directory, open the PNGs with the Read tool before calling graphics work done, and show the user before/after pairs. It also prints frame timings (the budget is 8 ms per frame). `SHOTS_FILTER=text` saves only the shots whose name contains the text and skips the timings (add `SHOTS_TIMING=1` to keep them), which makes a quick look at one thing take seconds. The `lineup` mode also renders every character's 3D turntable (`37_turntable_*`), sprite sheets of each strip (`38_sheet_*`) and the faces in every expression (`39_faces_*`), and ends with a room of people on their furniture and a pond of swimmers with the player fishing at its edge (`36d` to `36h`, plan 11 · C7, C8); the `pokemon` mode renders every hand-built Pokémon model's turntable (`91_turntable_*`; given species names, only theirs, one under another on `91_turntables`), one generated species of each body kind (`91_turntable_gen_*`), a model read back from a glTF file with its own clips (`91_turntable_imported_*`, `92_clips_imported_*`), boards of every clip for one species of each body plan (`92_clips_*`) and the eye states up close (`93_eyes_*`), and `sheets` the contact sheet of the menu sprites (`90_pokemon_sheet`; run on its own it goes on through every species, sixty to a page, `90_pokemon_sheet_NN`). Given species names, `pokemon` also writes their field sprites (`97_field_<species>`: four facings, two frames each). `dex` (not part of `all`) renders boards of every species' 3D model (`94_dex_NN`), or only the species and forms named (`Charizard-Mega-X`), and with `--back` from behind, as the player's own Pokémon is seen in battle (`94_dex_back_NN`); the first run meshes all 1025 (cached afterwards). `versus` (not part of `all`) shoots a wild battle for each pair of species or forms named, the first leading the player's party (`95_versus_<mine>_<foe>`), to see a model in battle from the front and from behind. `conditions` (not part of `all`) shows a battle's passing states: a Pokémon that has flown up is off its platform with its HP box left in place, and back for its strike (`96_flight_*`), a Fury Attack's hits landing and their count told (`96_hits_*`), and Ditto transformed into the foe (`96_transformed`), and Roark speaking in battle at each of the three moments, back on his platform beside his Pokémon with the camera on them, on his way off again, and once with the option off (`98_speaks_*`, plan 11 · C12 and C13; `CONDITIONS_FROM=speaks` runs this part alone); between them the five weathers at a menu and at their turn's line and the Substitute's doll up, aside and faded (`96_weather_*`, `96_substitute_*`, plan 25 · Y1), and last what lies on the field (plan 25 · Y2, `Modes/ConditionsField.cs`): Spikes falling, every hazard on the foe's side and on the player's, blown away by Defog (`96_hazards*`), Reflect and Light Screen on both sides (`96_screens*`), Safeguard, Mist and Lucky Chant (`96_veils*`), Tailwind, Trick Room rising and standing, the stage before and under Gravity, a Wish and a Future Sight waiting (`96_wish*`, `96_future_sight_close`) and a double battle in a sandstorm with everything on both sides (`96_field_double`), with the platforms close up at the screen's own resolution (`*_close`); it prints what each left on the field as the screen has it and whether that is what the rules have. After those come the turn's moments (plan 25 · Y3, `Modes/ConditionsMoments.cs`), each in a battle of its own read on a line a second, as a player reads, to the line that tells of it (`96_turn_<moment>`, each with its platform close up, `_close`): poison biting and its HP landed (`96_turn_poison`, `_poison_landed`), a burn, a Pokémon fast asleep and one confused, Leech Seed's sap leaving the foe and arriving at the seeder (`96_turn_leech_seed`, `_arriving`), Fire Spin, a curse, a berry eaten, Leftovers, a Potion out of the bag, a level under its panel, the Great Marsh's bait as it lands and as it is eaten and its mud (`96_turn_bait`, `_bait_eating`, `96_turn_mud`), and Future Sight and Doom Desire falling and landed (`96_future_sight`, `96_doom_desire`, each with `_falling`; the landing's close-up is `_landed_close`, since `96_future_sight_close` is the attack waiting), and two poisoned foes of a double battle, each bitten in its turn (`96_turn_double_poison`, `_second`); it prints at each which moment plays and how far in, whether the body flinched or the camera shook (neither should), the HP as the Pokémon and the bar have it, and how many quads the effects drew. Last, every one of the twenty-five moments by itself on the foe at two ages and a few on the player's own Pokémon, cropped at the screen's own resolution (`96_moment_<name>_a` and `_b`, `96_moment_mine_<name>_*`, `96_moment_leech_seed_to_mine` and `_to_foe`; `Modes/ConditionsMomentBoards.cs`, which starts each cue itself: `contact 96_moment` puts them on sheets). And at the very end the conditions that last (plan 09 · L2, `Modes/ConditionsStatus.cs`): a battle at its menu for each of the six, the foe and the player's own Pokémon both having it from before the battle (`96_status_asleep`, `_poisoned`, `_badly_poisoned`, `_burned`, `_paralysed`, `_frozen`), a confusion and a love each given to both by its own move (`96_status_confused`, `_infatuated`), each with both platforms at the screen's own resolution (`_close`, `_mine_close`); the marks from the director's other shots, over the attacker's shoulder and on the target close up (`96_status_shot_attacker`, `_shot_target`); a foe poisoned, confused and in love at once (`96_status_all`); a sleeper before and as it wakes (`96_status_asleep_before_waking_close`, `96_status_woken`); and a double battle with four conditions on the field (`96_status_double`). It prints what the screen shows of each Pokémon beside what the rules have of it, how much of its marks shows and the quads drawn; a paralysed Pokémon's mark is drawn only in its fits, so the mode waits for one (`IntoAFit`). `CONDITIONS_FROM=status` runs this part alone, for a quick look (its pictures are then not the whole mode's, since the clock and the dice stand elsewhere). After it come each kind of ball's release and a recall (plan 25 · Y4, `Modes/ConditionsBalls.cs`, `CONDITIONS_FROM=balls`), and last the bodies that answer (plan 25 · Y5, `Modes/ConditionsBodies.cs`, `CONDITIONS_FROM=bodies`, battles of their own): a miss dodged from the front and from behind (`96_dodge_a_coming`, `96_dodge`, `_close`, `96_dodge_c_back`, `96_dodge_mine`), both Pokémon tired at the menu (`96_tired`, `_close`, `_mine_close`, `_b`), a cheer for a win and for the player's loss (`96_cheer`, `_b`, `_close`, `96_cheer_foe`, `_close`) and Skull Bash's charge as its line is read and held through the menu (`96_charge`, `96_charge_menu`), printing each view's ages and the bar. And at its very end the field of the later games (plan 06 · R19b, `Modes/ConditionsLater.cs`, `CONDITIONS_FROM=later`, battles of their own under the modern rules, each laid through the core's entry points and settled as a menu settles a field): the four later weathers (`96_later_snow`, `_harsh_sun`, `_heavy_rain`, `_strong_winds`), the four terrains each with the foe's platform close up (`96_later_<terrain>`, `_close`), Aurora Veil and Sticky Web on both sides (`96_later_veil`, `96_later_web`, each with both platforms close up) and an Aegislash's blade drawn against a Mimikyu's busted disguise (`96_later_shapes`), printing what the screen has of each beside the rules. `demo` plays a scripted battle (send-outs, a dozen moves of different types, a critical hit, a faint, a Poké Ball thrown and caught; `d*`) and `arenas` every battle stage (`a*`). `evolution` plays the evolution scene phase by phase, the move choice, a stopped evolution, the bag's choice of Pokémon, and a stone and a trade through the game's own states (`e*`). `menus` shows every menu screen in its states: the bag's pockets, its action menu and its choice of Pokémon, the shop and its "how many?", the Pokédex on a species caught, one seen and one unseen, an entry's area and size pages, the search and its results by weight, the National Pokédex and a diploma (`26*`), the Trainer Card front and back in two colours (`28*`), the PC's party, box and box name, its menus, two wallpapers, a Pokémon carried, its marks, the release question and the box's keyboard (`30*`), the Hall of Fame with two teams (`30n` to `30q`), the Journal (`22j`, `22k`), the starters and their question, and saving before and after. `intro` plays the new-game introduction step by step (`i*`); run by itself it also lets the introduction end and shows the game it starts as the girl (the field, her Trainer Card, a battle, the assistant). Battle modes advance time with `Skip(seconds)`, which draws only every sixth frame: battle presentation is a function of time, so the frames between only cost time under software rendering. `lab` builds a map in the harness itself that has every terrain feature in one place (`BuildTerrainLab`: a mountain with stairs, a rock face and a waterfall, a gorge with a bridge, a boardwalk, ice, snow of every depth, a marsh, a beach, ledges facing three ways, the three obstacles) and shows each, on foot and surfing (`lab*`): the areas open so far are flat, so this is where relief is seen. `life` shows what moves in the field (`l*`): prints in sand and snow, dust behind a run and under a hop, leaves out of tall grass, rings behind a swimmer, the splash of riding out and the player on the back of the Pokémon whose Surf it was (`l06e`), a house door and a Pokémon Center's glass doors opening, standing open behind the player and shutting (by day and after dark), every bubble, every weather over Twinleaf Town (rain also at night and on the pond, a thunderstorm caught in its lightning), the camera sent to look elsewhere, each way into a battle caught in its flash, closing and opening, a waterfall twice an eighth of a second apart, and a fountain and a turbine in their four frames. It walks the player with the game's own steps (`GameEngine.Steering`), because a step is what leaves a print. It ends with the camera kit's lens (`l50` to `l56`) and, last of all, the ways into a battle by place and by whom (plan 09 · L13): each of the twelve in its flash, its lead where it has one, three times as its dark closes and as it opens (`l30_<kind>_1_flash` to `_6_opening`: the water's from the pond, the caves' in Oreburgh Gate, the Frontier's in the Battle Tower's lobby, the boss's on Team Galactic's fourth floor), a cave's ring closing on a player who stands off the picture's middle (`l30_wild_cave_7_*`), a wild Pokémon met surfing from the closing to the battle (`l34_*`), and a battle with a grunt (`l35_*`); it prints the way the game picked for each of its own battles begun there (`way in: Commander Mars in the Valley Windworks -> GalacticBoss`): read those lines as well as the pictures. After those, last of all, come the bands (plan 09 · L15): Roark's in his Gym and Cynthia's in her room, each begun through `StartTrainerBattle` with the person of the room carrying their team, at five moments of the band by its own beats and as the battle opens out of its white (`l38_band_roark_1_wiping_in` to `_6_opening`, `l39_band_cynthia_1_halves` to `_6_opening`); it prints the way picked, whose band it is, the look each portrait is of, how many portraits are rendered at each shot and that their targets are freed at the end. And after the bands, the very last, the legendary's and the mythical's (plan 09 · L14): a wild Dialga in Twinleaf Town's street and a Giratina in Oreburgh Gate, each begun through `StartWildBattle`, at four moments of the camera's closing and its white and as the battle opens (`l36_legendary_1_widening` to `_5_opening`, `l37_mythical_1_first_lenses` to `_5_opening`); it prints the way picked, the lens the rig holds at each shot beside the one the way asks for, its step and the widest made ready, and that the camera is let go as the battle opens. (The diamonds that stood in for a Gym Leader's way are gone with their four shots, `l30_leader_*`; the mode still spends the frames they took, so the shots after them stand where they did.) `world` shows the towns, routes and caves of the imported world by day and after dark (`w*`; the south-west that plan 01 · M5 opened is `w30` to `w74`, the centre of M6 `w90` to `wb0`, the east and the sea of M7 `wc0` to `we3` with `w78` and `w79` after dark, the north and the end of M8 `wf0` to `wg5` with `wg6` and `wg7` after dark, and last the Distortion World's floors (`wh*`, the same at every hour) and the north's last landmarks (`wi*`), which the `distortion` mode (not part of `all`) shoots on their own, and after them the Battle Zone of M10, part a (`wj00` to `wj44`, with `wj50` to `wj55` after dark: Victory Road's back rooms and Route 224, the Fight Area with its quay, containers and fountains, Routes 225 to 227, the Survival Area, Route 228 in its sandstorm, Routes 229 and 230, the Resort Area with its pools and the Ribbon Syndicate) and the rest of the post-game of part b (`wj60` to `wj9f`, with `wja0` to `wja8` after dark: Stark Mountain's foot, mouth, crater and rooms, Snowpoint Temple's floors, the two islands and their woods, the Seabreak Path, Flower Paradise and Route 224 with the path revealed, the Battle Frontier from its gate to each hall's doors, and the Ribbon Syndicate through its own arrival script, shown out with no ribbons and let in with ten; it prints what the swap and the hostess left behind, and waits a second where a place has a climate of its own, whose light eases in), which the `battlezone` mode (not part of `all`) shoots on its own, or, given places after its name (`battlezone Sinnoh,650,430 Sinnoh,802,474,night`), shoots those instead (`wjx_<map>_<x>_<y>`); the harness fixes the date, `GameClock.FixedDate`, so the calendar's weather is the same every run), a cave made dark for the picture, before and after Flash is used (`w80` to `w82`), then walks three ways at running pace (from the player's door in Twinleaf Town to the top of Route 202, on through Jubilife City to Oreburgh Gate, and from Jubilife City up Route 204), timing every frame and printing what streaming cost (chunks made ready, chunks a frame had to wait for: there should be none). Shots written for the six hand-made maps the world replaced (Jubilife City was the last) go through `GoTo`, which moves each old spot to its place on the map of Sinnoh (`movedSpots` in the harness); new shots use `At` with tiles of the map itself. `story` (the last part of `all`) shows scripts at work in the field (`st*`): the nurse's question with its answers, a PC, a clerk and their counter, a signboard, a clown's quiz and the coupon it gives, a scene written in the harness itself (bubbles, two people walking up to one another, the camera sent away, someone taken off the map, a line over a black screen), each scene effect at its height and as it goes (`st60_effect_<kind>`, `st61_effect_<kind>_fading`, plan 09 · L11), a trainer who sees the player, challenges, is beaten and has a last word, and a Machop of Oreburgh's mine spoken to and the yard's (`st35` to `st37`, plan 10 · F1), and last the machines of plan 09 · L12: a team of six seen to at a Pokémon Center's machine, its tray empty, with three balls, with six and lit (`st70_heal_machine_0` to `_3`), and the PC's screen dark, starting, on, going and off (`st71_pc_*`, `st72_pc_*`), each with a `_close` crop at the screen's own resolution (the other set pieces are shot where their rooms are: a lift's ride in `sunyshore`, the Hall of Fame's machine in `champion`, the shake and the flicker in `legends`). It talks to people through the game's own `TryInteract`, answers with the question box's own buttons (`engine.Choice`), and prints what each script left behind (healed or not, the coupon and its flag, the trainer beaten): read those lines as well as the pictures. `fieldmoves` (not part of `all`) shows the field moves and key items at work (`fm*`), through the game's own `TryInteract`, party menu and bag: a tree's question, the cut-in and the tree falling, Strength's question and a boulder sliding, Wayward Cave in the dark and lit by Flash chosen in the party menu, the menu's word when a move can't be used, Fly's map and the flight, the Cycling Road's gate keeper turning someone on foot back, Surf's question and the ride out, the Pokétch's three apps, and a rod's cast from the wait to the bite, the catch and its battle; it prints what each left behind (the tree's flag, the boulder's tile, the cave lit). `opening` (not part of `all`) shows the first chapter's scenes in a new game of its own, played by its own scripts (`op*`): the bedroom and the television, the rival bursting in and running out of his door, the professor on Route 201, the briefcase, the first battle, Cyrus at Lake Verity, the Pokédex in the lab and the assistant's tour of Sandgem Town, and last, each as its moment of the chapter finds the house, Mom's three scenes at home (`op22` to `op31`: the foot of the stairs, the warning on the mat, the walk from the kitchen to the cushions and the Running Shoes, the rival's mother and the parcel), and after them a scene skipped (plan 12 · Q20): the lab's scene under the options' `Seen`, the hold doing nothing before the scene has ever been played, then the ring half full (`op40_skip_held`, its corner close up in `op40b_skip_held_close`), the hurry stopped at the professor's question, and the lab once a second hold has skipped to the end (`op41_skip_done`); it prints where everyone stands, the flags, the variables, the team and the bag for the scene played and for the scene skipped, side by side (`WhatASceneLeft`, `TellBothWays` in `Modes/Skips.cs`), and a line that begins with `!!` is something the skip left otherwise. `jubilife` (not part of `all`) does the same for the second chapter (`j*`): the assistant and Looker in Jubilife City, the rival's parcel at the Trainers' School, the Pokétch campaign, the rival on Route 203, the hiker's HM, the boy and the rival at the Gym's door, Roark down the mine, the Gym, its battle and Badge, the rival on the way out and Team Galactic's tag battle at the north gate; it wins every battle a scene starts. `windworks` (not part of `all`) does the same for the third chapter's first half (`ww*`): the grunts at Floaroma Meadow's door, Floaroma's people standing and then about their day (`ww02`, `ww03`: the one place the harness lets people move), the little girl and the bridge on Route 205, the meadow's grunts, their battle and the Works Key, the grunt at the Valley Windworks' door and the door opening, Commander Mars inside, Looker outside, and Eterna Forest with Cheryl: joining, following, walked back into, two wild Pokémon at once, a pair of trainers who see the player together and their tag battle, and her parting. `eterna` (not part of `all`) does the same for its second half (`et*`): Eterna City as the chapter finds it, the rival running into the player and Cyrus at the statue, Cynthia and HM01, Gardenia at her Gym's door, the tree before Team Galactic's building cut (the Forest Badge given as the Gym's script gives it, the Gym's inside being plan 01 · M9's), Looker in disguise, the building's floors, a trainer and Commander Jupiter, the cycle shop's Bicycle, the ways out watched until the Explorer Kit, the Underground Man, the Pokémon Center and Gardenia before the Old Chateau. `veilstone` (not part of `all`) does the same for the fifth chapter (`vp*`, plan 02 · S8): Route 215's black belt, the television crew in Hearthome's gate to Route 212, the rival in Pastoria's Gym doorway, the assistant and Crasher Wake before the Veilstone Gym, the warehouse's guards, Maylene's Badge won in her Gym and the assistant's call for help outside it, the tag battle by the warehouse, Looker and HM02 inside it, the rival's battle in Pastoria, the Fen Badge won and Crasher Wake coming out, the bomb at the Great Marsh and the rival at its door, the grunt's flight along Route 213 and Looker, the grunt cornered at Valor Lakefront, Cynthia and the Secret Potion, and the road to Sunyshore shut; it prints what each left behind (the TMs, HM02, the states of Pastoria and the warehouse, the Secret Potion), and `VEILSTONE_FROM=pastoria` starts it at the rival's battle in Pastoria. `celestic` (not part of `all`) does the same for the sixth chapter (`cs*`, plan 02 · S9): Route 218's blockade before the Canalave gate, the Psyduck on Route 210 cured and Cynthia with the Old Charm, northern Route 210 in its fog, Celestic Town's elder and Team Galactic's grunt, the ruins' painting, the elder and Cyrus, his battle and HM03, Cynthia outside the ruins, the elder's house, the Fuego Ironworks' floor and Mr. Fuego, the sea south of Sandgem Town, the blockade gone and the rival's battle on Canalave City's bridge; it prints what each left behind (the Old Charm, HM03, Celestic's and Canalave's states, the blockade), and `CELESTIC_FROM=ruins` starts it at the ruins' painting, `CELESTIC_FROM=sea` at the Fuego Ironworks. `lakes` (not part of `all`) does the same for the seventh chapter (`k*`, plan 02 · S10): the cameramen at Valor Lakefront and Team Galactic's grunts in Mt. Coronet's northern hall as the chapter finds them, Riley and HM04 at Iron Island's door, his partnership two floors down, a pair of trainers and their tag battle beside him, the grunts before the lift and the Riolu Egg, the rival out of Canalave's Gym and before the library's door, the library's three floors, the meeting, the explosion and the television's news, a book of myths, the sailor's news outside, Lake Verity quiet, Lake Valor drained with its Magikarp, Valor Cavern and Commander Saturn's battle, Lake Verity with the professor held back, its grunts and Commander Mars's battle, the hall clear, Route 211's TM, Maylene on Route 217 and HM08 in its ball; it wins every battle a scene starts and prints what each left behind (HM04, the Egg, Canalave's and the library's states, the lakes' flags, Lake Acuity's state, TM77, HM08), and `LAKES_FROM=library` starts it at the rival outside the Gym, `LAKES_FROM=valor` at Lake Verity before Team Galactic and Valor Lakefront, `LAKES_FROM=verity` at the lake Team Galactic holds, `LAKES_FROM=north` in Mt. Coronet's hall. `snowpoint` (not part of `all`) does the same for the eighth chapter's first half (`sn*`, plan 02 · S11, part a): the rival at the head of Acuity Lakefront's rock face, calling down and running on, the face refusing a climb with no Badge, Snowpoint City, its temple's guard turning the player back and the sailor on the quay, Maylene thawing out in the Pokémon Center, the Mart, Candice's battle and the Icicle Badge, Maylene after it and gone home once the lakefront is come to again, Rock Climb up the face and the way into the lake, Commander Jupiter and the beaten rival at Lake Acuity from the west tile of its way in and from the east, the shore empty afterwards and the TM on its far shore, and the lake as it was with the rival on watch; it wins the battle a scene starts and prints what each left behind (the lakefront's state and the rival's flag, the Badge and TM72, Maylene's two flags, Lake Acuity's state and everything its scene sets), and `SNOWPOINT_FROM=city` starts it in Snowpoint City, `SNOWPOINT_FROM=lake` at the rock face with the Badge. `encounters` (not part of `all`) shows the wild Pokémon beyond the tables at work (`en*`, plan 06 · R13): the Poké Radar's patches shaking, a patch's battle and the patches set again after it, a honey tree asked, slathered, shaking and its battle, poison biting in the field and a Pokémon pulling through, the assistant's sister's news of swarms and a swarm's battle, a roamer met and fleeing, and Feebas's lake and its battle; it prints what each left behind (the chain, the tree's honey, the roamer's HP and place, the day's Feebas tiles and 200 casts onto one). `rooms` (not part of `all`) shows the rooms rebuilt to the original's own plans (`r*`: the three houses whose people trade, the Valley Windworks' hall from its door, its middle and Commander Mars's corner, a crop at the screen's own resolution, and a house after dark). `field`, `look`, `terrain`, `buildings`, `menus` and `world` also write before/after boards (`compare_*`) when given the folder of an earlier run. `cities` (not part of `all`) is how a town is looked at before its area is open: it builds the whole of Sinnoh from `tools/MapImporter/out/world` (run the importer first), opens every area, and for each town takes a shot before one of each of its building models and props (`c_<key>_<n>_<model>`, with a `_top` shot over the roof of a tall block) and puts them on one sheet (`city_<key>`); name the towns to do only those.

The harness is a file per mode (plan 16 · T5): `tools/ShotHarness/Program.cs` keeps the arguments, the window, the seeds and the order of `all`, `Harness.cs` the game and the helpers every mode uses (`Frames`, `Skip`, `Shot`, `At`, `StartBattle`, `ToMainMenu`…), `Modes/<Mode>.cs` one mode each (a method of the partial class `Harness`) and `Compare.cs` `diff`, `contact` and `crop`. A new mode is a new file and a line in `Program.cs`. It reaches the engine through its `Driver` (`engine.Drive`, `Core/GameEngine.Driving.cs`; `game` in the harness): the engine's state (`State`, `Map`, `Player`, `Battle`, the party, bag, Pokédex and boxes, the story, every screen, the renderers, `VirtualScreen`) and its own steps (`Place`, `StartWildBattle`, `StartTrainerBattle`, `StartTransition`, `Interact`, `ArriveOnMap`, `ChooseFromStartMenu`, `UseFieldItem`, `EncounterMomentNow`, `KeepPartnerAlong`), and calls the engine's internal classes directly, since `InternalsVisibleTo` covers `ShotHarness` as well as the tests. Nothing is found by name any more, so renaming a field is caught by the build. Something new the harness must see or do is a member of `Driver` that calls the engine's own field or method, never a second way of doing what the game does. A battle is brought to its menu with `ToMainMenu`, which reads on past whatever lines the Pokémon's abilities say as they come in; a move is chosen only once the menu is open. The engine starts on the title screen, so the harness calls `StartNewGame()` first; only its `title` mode goes back there. A new game begins in the player's room with nothing (plan 02 · S4), so the harness then takes it past the first chapter (`PastTheOpening`: `common.OpeningDone`, a Turtwig, a few items, in front of the house), as every mode but `opening` expects.

`gyms` (not part of `all`) shows the Gyms rebuilt to the original's plans with their puzzles at work (`g*`), through the game's own `TryInteract`, `ArriveOnMap` and scripts: the Eterna Gym from its door, its flower clock at each time, turning (`clockTurn`) and a fountain draining, the hop over the hour hand's tip; the Veilstone Gym's bags before, during and after a kick (`bagRun`), all the kicks that open the way to Maylene, and the stacks gone; the Hearthome Gym's guide, its dark rooms with the lights round the player and the trainers, the sign on the floor, a wrong door back to the entrance and the right ones on, Fantina's twirl and the bollards gone; the Oreburgh Gym's tiers, its pit and bridge and its stairs; the Pastoria Gym's water at each of its three levels and on its way, a raft and the buttons; the Canalave Gym's floors, a lift rising and a cart running; the Snowpoint Gym's bowl of ice, a slide gathering speed down a slope, smashing a snowball and going on through its place, a slip back off a slope, and a slide with no speed stopped by the same snowball once the player has come in again (`g80` to `g8a`); the Sunyshore Gym's three rooms of gears, a quarter turn from a button before, during and after, its bars on edge turning over and lying flat, and its upper gears laid out to open the way to Volkner (`g90` to `g9b`, last in the mode so the Gyms before it keep their battles); each Leader's line, the band their battle opens on once it is lit (`g_band_<name>`, plan 09 · L15) and battle. It prints what each puzzle left behind (the clock's state, the stacks standing, the doors chosen, the heights underfoot, the water's level, the gears' states, the Badges and TMs); name Gyms (`gyms hearthome`; through `tools/dev/shots.sh` after its filter, `shots.sh <dir> gyms "" hearthome`) to shoot only those.

`boutique` (not part of `all`) shows the clothes (`bq*`, plan 11 · C9, C10): the Jubilife boutique's door and inside, its clerk's counter through the game's own `TryInteract`, the wardrobe screen on each slot's tab, the buy question and a garment bought and worn, the player in the street in the new outfit, the bedroom's wardrobe and its screen, and the turntables of six outfits on both looks (`bq20_turntable_<look>_<outfit>`).

`daycare` (not part of `all`) shows breeding (`dc*`, plan 06 · R15) through the game's own `TryInteract`, question box, party menu and steps (`DayCareStep`): Solaceon's Day Care and the Day-Care Man, the room, the Lady's question and the team to choose from, the fan who gives the Day-Care Checker, the Egg offered and received, the Egg on the team's cards and its summary, "Oh?" and the hatching phase by phase (`HatchScreen`), and Cynthia offering her Togepi Egg in Eterna City; it prints what each left behind (the Day Care's state and how well its two get on, the Egg's species, nature, cycles and moves, the hatched Pokémon and the Pokédex). The Lady won't take a Pokémon that would leave the player with two able to fight between team and boxes (the original's rule), so the mode adds a Bidoof first.

`poffins` (not part of `all`) shows Poffins and the contest condition (`pf*`, plan 06 · R14c) through the game's own `TryInteract`, scripts, bag and screens: Hearthome's two doors, the Pokémon Fan Club and its chairman's Poffin Case, the Poffin House and its cook's question, the bag on its berries, the berry going in, the stirring (the spoon at work, spilling, burning, the last stage), the results and "cook another?", the case opened from the bag, a flavour's tab, GIVE, the team, the condition before and after and how the Pokémon took it, the summary's condition page, and the Contest Hall boy's Mild Poffin. It stirs with the screen's own arrows held by the harness (`PoffinCookingScreen.Held`) and prints what each left behind (the case, the Poffin cooked and its numbers, the fed Pokémon's condition, the flags).

`contests` (not part of `all`) shows a Super Contest (`sc*`, plan 06 · R17a) through the game's own `TryInteract`, scripts, question boxes, team screen and the contest screen's own buttons: the Contest Hall's middle counter and its receptionist's questions (enter, the rank, the contest, the Pokémon), then a Cool Contest of the Normal Rank: the line-up, the theme, the Fashion Case with nothing on, three things on and full in the last ten seconds, the first entry's look and the player's with their hearts, the dance announced, an opponent leading, the player repeating its steps, the player leading and the others repeating, the dance's points, the acting's moves, judges, first act, an effect, a voltage rising and a judge's hearts, the second turn with the move just shown barred, the acting's points, the results' board round by round, the winner, the ribbon, the prize at the counter; and at the practice counter a Visual Competition by itself and its result. It dances as a player would (`SuperContestScreen.Held`, a step for the frame to come) and prints what each round gave every contestant and what the contest left behind (the place, the ribbon, the prize, the Fashion Case, the opponents seen in the Pokédex).

`capsules` (not part of `all`) shows the Ball Capsules, the Seals and the ribbons (`bc*`, plan 06 · R17b) through the game's own `TryInteract`, scripts, question boxes and the screens' own buttons: Solaceon Town's east house, its mother's Seal Case and her boy with an Unown on the team; the Sunyshore Market, the Seal merchant's stall of the day, a Seal's burst on its little stage and "how many?", and the woman of the Effort Ribbon before and after; a PC's question with a Seal Case, the twelve capsules empty, a capsule's menu, the case's pages, a Seal in hand, off the ball and stuck on, those on the ball picked up, the burst on the stage, the question on backing out, a capsule kept, put on a Pokémon of the team, a capsule of letters, two swapped (`bc13` to `bc27`); then battles: a capsule's Seals bursting round the player's Pokémon at four moments, a word spelt out of letter Seals, and a Gym Leader's Pokémon coming out of its trainers' capsule (`bc30` to `bc36`); Julia's house and her day's ribbon, Dr. Footstep's house and the Footprint Ribbon; the summary's page of ribbons, one looked at, and every ribbon on five pages (`bc52_*`); the Fashion Case on its accessories and on each of the eighteen backdrops (`bc62_*`), a winner's photograph from the Contest Hall's frame, an empty frame's words, and the Trainer Card's back. It prints what each left behind (the case's kinds, the capsules, who carries which, the money, the ribbons, the records). A burst is a moment: the mode waits for the middle of one (`MidBurst`) before it shoots a stage.

`league` (not part of `all`) shows the Pokémon League's rooms (`lg*`, plan 01 · M9, part 2d) through the game's own `TryInteract`, `ArriveOnMap`, steps and scripts: the League's Pokémon Center rebuilt to the original's plan, its guard asking for the Badges and stepped aside (`lg01` to `lg06`); a passage at its foot, its platform rising with the player on it and at the top (`lg10_*`); each room of the Elite Four with the door shut behind, its master's greeting, the battle and the door on open (`lg20` to `lg54`); the Champion's passage, her greeting and battle, the platform up to the gallery (`lg60` to `lg75`); the hallway with Cynthia and the professor (`lg80` to `lg82`); the Hall of Fame, its machine, the record and home (`lg90` to `lg94`); and the band each of the five's battle opens on, lit, the player's portrait facing theirs (`lg_band_<name>`, plan 09 · L15). It wins every battle (the lead never tires) and prints what each room left behind (the guard's tile, the platforms' heights, the doors, the flags, the Hall of Fame's entries); name rooms (`league aaron champion`; `center`, `aaron`, `bertha`, `flint`, `lucian`, `champion`) to shoot only those.

`headquarters` (not part of `all`) shows the eighth chapter's second half (`hq*`, plan 02 · S11, part b), played by its own scripts through the game's own `TryInteract`, steps and question boxes: the terrace before the Galactic Veilstone Building, the grunt who knows the player and runs, Looker with the Storage Key, his question refused and agreed to, the building's lobby, its locked shutter and the room behind its third door with the green pad that carries nobody (`hq01` to `hq16`); the warehouse's door, Looker coming in to unlock it and going on ahead down the stairs (`hq20` to `hq27`); the basements' store rooms, a shutter locked and a grunt's battle (`hq30` to `hq37`); the ground floor's rooms and a warp panel ridden, caught turning and on the far panel (`hq40` to `hq44`); the second floor's rooms, a bed rested in, the kitchen's report, round by the stairs to the Galactic Key, the shutters it opens and the third floor's rooms (`hq50` to `hq78`); the hall with its ranks, Looker, Cyrus on the dais through his speech, the roar, and the hall empty (`hq80` to `hq93`); the fourth floor, Cyrus turning from his desk, his battle, the Master Ball, his leaving by the panel, the reports and the bollards (`hq100` to `hq116`); the laboratory's tanks and its doorway (`hq120` to `hq124`); the control room, Charon, Commander Saturn's battle, the call back from the door, the button, the three Pokémon of the lakes come out of their machines and gone (`hq130` to `hq146`); and the way out: the laboratory empty, the bollards gone, the green panel ridden and the street (`hq150` to `hq153`). It wins every battle a scene starts and prints what each left behind (the warehouse's state and flags, where each panel put the player and facing which way, the Galactic Key and the shutters' flags, the hall's state and who is left in it, the Master Ball, the control room's state and everything the button sets for Mt. Coronet), and `HQ_FROM=warehouse` starts it at the warehouse's door with Looker's word given, `HQ_FROM=floors` in the basement, `HQ_FROM=hall` before the speech, `HQ_FROM=cyrus` on the fourth floor with the key, `HQ_FROM=lab` in the laboratory with Cyrus beaten.

`underground` (not part of `all`) shows the Underground (`ug*`, plan 06 · R16a) through the game's own `UseFieldItem`, `TryInteract`, scripts, steps and the screens' own buttons: the Explorer Kit used in Eterna City's street and the fade down, Roark's welcome, the tunnels, the radar's pulse beside a wall that holds something (`ug01` to `ug05`); the wall's question and its game from the first line to the finds (the lesson, the pick, the hammer, the first thing bare, the last, what was dug out), and a second wall with dark rock in it hammered until it falls in (`ug06` to `ug18`); every family of trap sprung under the player and got out of as it asks (a Reverse Trap, a Pit Trap half climbed and free, smoke whole and half wiped, a boulder whole and cracking, bubbles, flowers, a flame high and low, a Hurl Trap, an Alert Trap; `ug20` to `ug2f`); the bag below ground tab by tab and a treasure put in the bag above ground (`ug30` to `ug36`); a Sphere buried, found by the radar five days on and dug up, a trap set and stepped on (`ug37` to `ug3d`); a hiker of each kind, his wares, the Sphere chosen to pay with, a good and a treasure traded in (`ug40` to `ug4b`); the Digger Drill and the cavern it opens, the base bare, its PC's four tabs (`ug50` to `ug58`); the decorating: the menu, the store, a good that can't stand before the PC and one that fits, ten out, the eleventh turned away, a boulder that stays, a good being moved (`ug60` to `ug68`), and the room afterwards from four places (`ug69` to `ug6c`); another digger's way in, base and flag, the flag put up at home and the base under its bronze flag (`ug70` to `ug78`); the way back up (`ug80`, `ug81`); and the Underground Man's kit and first reward (`ug90`, `ug91`). It drives a trap with the keys a tool may hold for it (`GameEngine.TrapKeys`) and prints what each left behind (where it came down and up, the walls and traps spread, each wall's things and what was found, the turns, blows and breaths each trap took, the bag, the Sphere's growth, the trades, the base, the diggers of the day, the flags, the mission).

`pillar` (not part of `all`) shows the ninth chapter (`sp*`, plan 02 · S12), played by its own scripts through the game's own `TryInteract`, steps, pushes and question boxes: Mt. Coronet's cave painting standing and read, the grunt of the northern hall, the hole broken open with its rubble, Looker running up with the Black Flute, the tunnel's lost grunt and the last guard's battle (`sp01` to `sp16`); Spear Pillar, the two grunts and their double battle, Commander Mars and Commander Jupiter closing in, the rival beside the player, the tag battle, his medicine and his leaving, Cyrus at the altar, Dialga, Palkia, the shadow and the dark (`sp20` to `sp44`); the torn pillar, Cynthia, the three Pokémon of the lakes, her question refused and agreed to, the rifts and Team Galactic left behind (`sp50` to `sp61`); the Distortion World: the portal, Cynthia and the slab on 1F (`sp70` to `sp76`), Cynthia over the gaps and Mesprit on B1F, Cynthia on B2F, Cyrus on B3F, the shadow on B4F (`sp80` to `sp90`), B5F's boulders, each guardian's showing, a boulder pushed to its hole and gone (`sp91` to `sp97`), B6F's wings, a wrong pit, each boulder at rest and its guardian's cry, Cynthia and the stepping stones (`sp100` to `sp114`), B7F's argument, Cyrus's battle and his leaving (`sp120` to `sp132`), Giratina's room, its coming, its battle, Cynthia and Cyrus on the stones, the portal and its question (`sp140` to `sp152`); Sendoff Spring and Cynthia before the cave's mouth (`sp160` to `sp165`); Spear Pillar whole again and Saturn in the lobby (`sp170` to `sp172`); and Turnback Cave's Giratina, the portal that hangs in its place and the Griseous Orb (`sp180` to `sp184`). It wins every battle a scene starts (Giratina is beaten, not caught) and prints what each left behind (the hole and its way, the Black Flute, the summit's states and flags, the Pokédex's two, the count of the Distortion World floor by floor, each boulder's flags, Giratina's, everything Sendoff Spring sets, where Mt. Coronet's and Valor Lakefront's ways lead afterwards, the Orb), each read after the place has been come to again, since a scene's own `hide` and `show` hold until then; `PILLAR_FROM=summit` starts it at Spear Pillar, `PILLAR_FROM=distortion` on the torn pillar, `PILLAR_FROM=puzzle` on B5F, `PILLAR_FROM=cyrus` on B7F, `PILLAR_FROM=giratina` in its room, `PILLAR_FROM=spring` at the portal out.
