`pastimes` (not part of `all`) shows fossils, the Game Corner, the Villa and Pal Park (`pt*`, plan 06 · R16b) through the game's own `TryInteract`, `ArriveOnMap`, scripts, steps and the screens' own buttons: the Oreburgh Mining Museum's door, hall, cases and east wing, an exhibit read, the scientist's question with a Skull Fossil in the bag, the machine at work, and the Pokémon handed over once the player has gone out and come in again (`pt01` to `pt08`); the clown's house in Veilstone City, his question and the Coin Case (`pt10` to `pt12`); the Game Corner's door, hall and rows of machines and its counter's question (`pt20` to `pt23`); a machine before the bet, turning, with one reel stopped and with a line that pays, the sevens asked for, the jackpot, the bonus game's first round, the order pointed out, the second reel stopped, a win, and a game under a red moon and under a bright one (`pt30` to `pt3b`); the prize exchange and its list (`pt40`, `pt41`); the boy in the Resort Area, his offer, the Villa empty, the first table, the catalogue's list, the sofa delivered with the caller it brings, the room with everything the catalogue has from three places, Cynthia walked in and spoken to, the piano looked at, Flint and Volkner, the professor at the door, his question and the professor inside (`pt50` to `pt5h`); Pal Park's door, its lobby, the attendant with nothing brought over, the Park Balls handed out, the countdown, the gate, the grass, a show's battle and its ball, the count over the field, the last catch, the score and the lobby afterwards (`pt60` to `pt6c`). It rigs a machine's draw where a shot needs one (`SlotMachine.Rig`, `RigBonus`: the reels are still stopped by the machine's own rules), puts six Pokémon in the park as the migration will (`PalParkState.Stock`), and prints what each left behind (the Fossil and the team, the coins at each step, what was lined up and paid, the bonus's rounds, the prize, the Villa's state, pieces and money, the day's visitor, the show's six and their corners, each catch, the score's three parts, the record, the boxes and the berry); name parts (`pastimes villa park`; `museum`, `corner`, `villa`, `park`; through `tools/dev/shots.sh` after its filter, `shots.sh <dir> pastimes "" villa`) to shoot only those.

`interiors` (not part of `all`) shows the rooms plan 01 · M11 built to the original's plans (`in_*`; part a: the south-west and the centre; part b: the east and the sea; part c: the north and the Battle Zone). For each town named (`twinleaf`, `sandgem`, `jubilife`, `oreburgh`, `floaroma`, `eterna`, `chateau`, `hearthome`, `solaceon`, part b's `veilstone`, `lakefront`, `pastoria`, `mansion`, `canalave`, and part c's `celestic`, `snow`, `sunyshore`, `battlezone`, `syndicate`; every one when none is) it shoots every room from its door (`in_<town>_<nn>_<Room>`; a room wider than the picture from two more places, `b` and `c`), puts them on one sheet (`interiors_<town>`) and prints each room's size, people, props and ways out: the sheet is how a town's rooms are looked over together. With no town named, or with `work`, it then shows a few of part a's at work through the game's own `TryInteract`, steps and question boxes (`in_x*`): a gift given once and the line afterwards, a flight of stairs taken, the flower shop's Sprayduck, its berry of the day and its menu of accessories, the Berry Master and the mulch, a rest on Route 205, a lift's doors and the floor above, Bebe's Eevee, the Old Chateau's hall from its floor and its gallery, the old man at the head of the dining table on a visit the die shows him, the room after he is gone, and the television that says nothing yet. It prints what each left behind (the item and its flag, where the stairs and the lift put the player, the berries, the money, the HP, the team, how many visits the ghost took). With no town named, or with `workb`, it shows part b's at work the same way (`in_y*`): the café's milk over its bar, the Massage Girl, the Seven Stars Restaurant's tables, a couple asked and their double battle, the Suite Key handed over and the White Flute, a rest at the Hotel Grand Lake's reception, the Safari Game from the observatory's counter into the marsh and back out to the attendant's question and the cowgirl's app, the binoculars, the Pokémon Mansion's hall, a corridor and a door of it taken, the maids' challenge and a prodigy (the day's number made the number the sweep took), Mr. Backlot, his butler sent out, the statue's guard, and the old man of Route 221. It prints what each left behind (the money, the friendship, who sits at the tables, the Safari Game's state, the day's number of turns, the Trophy Garden's newest), and it takes more than ten minutes, longer than one tool call may: run it in the background. With no town named, or with `workc`, it shows part c's at work (`in_z*`, `Modes/InteriorsNorth.cs`; under a minute): Celestic Town's shop over its counter and the man in glasses, a rest in the Snowbound Lodge's bed, the hiker's Icicle Plate, the woman of the snow and her house empty afterwards, the fisherman measuring a Remoraid and one that beats his record, the Pikachu Fan Club and the trainer among its Pikachu, the inventor's app, the Item Expert's list, the Meister's trade, a pile of books read, and the Ribbon Syndicate's lift ridden up to the spa, a treatment there and the ride back down. It prints what each left behind (the items and flags, the sizes and the record, the team, where the lift put the player and where it stands, the friendship, where the attendants ended). The `syndicate` town is shot as a member's, since the room's own arrival shows anyone else out. Part d's towns are `gates` (the sixteen gate houses), `tutors` (the tutors' and the move men's houses, with the Name Rater's), `store` (the Department Store's six floors and its lift), `centers` (a Pokémon Center and the League's north one with the floor above and the floor below each) `terminal` (the Global Terminal's three floors) and `gallery` (Pal Park's lobby and the viewing gallery over it); with no town named, or with `workd`, it shows part d's at work (`in_w*`, `Modes/InteriorsLast.cs`; under a minute): the Old Rod in Jubilife's gate, the Pokédex's upgrade in Canalave's, the television crew in the gate to Route 212, a keeper of the Cycling Road's gate over his counter, the Battle Frontier's attendant and her topics, a shard tutor's list and the move taught, Grandma Wilma's Draco Meteor, the Move Deleter's question, the store's counter, its vending machine, its Poffin seller and its lift ridden to the roof, an attendant upstairs in a Pokémon Center and the Global Terminal's receptionist. It prints what each left behind (the Old Rod and its flag, the upgrade's flag and the gate's state, each pupil's moves and the Shards left, the drinks, the Poffin, where the lift put the player). The last rooms' towns (the hand-made rooms of the first chapters, laid out again) are `pokecenters` (the seventeen ground floors), `marts` (the thirteen) and `firstrooms` (the Trainers' School, the Pokétch Company's three floors, Jubilife TV's lobby, the rival's house, and since the plan's last step the player's house on both floors and the professor's lab); with no town named, or with `worke`, it shows them at work (`in_v*`, `Modes/InteriorsFirst.cs`; a few seconds): the rival's room as the opening finds it, his start at the sight of the player, his run round to the stairs and the room without him, the Pokétch Company's president before his counter, a Center's PC used from the tile before it, the attendant before its way down and the way open once the Coal Badge is won, and a Mart's own counter. It prints what each left behind (where the scene put the player and the rival's flags, the screens the PC and the counter opened, where the attendant kept the player). Through `tools/dev/shots.sh` the towns go after the filter (`shots.sh <dir> interiors "" chateau work`).

`sunyshore` (not part of `all`) shows the tenth chapter (`sy*`, plan 02 · S13), played by its own scripts through the game's own `TryInteract`, steps and arrivals: the road east of Valor Lakefront shut, the professor and the assistant inside the lab's door, his news, the two afterwards and the road open (`sy01` to `sy0b`); Lake Verity's island, Mesprit in its cavern, its cry, the cavern without it, the professor come in after it, his words and his leaving (`sy10` to `sy1a`); Lake Valor full again, Azelf in its cavern, its battle and the cavern empty, and the same at Lake Acuity with Uxie (`sy20` to `sy34`); Route 222's rich boy and his TM, Flint at Sunyshore City's gate and before its Gym's door, the city's Pokémon Center and Mart with their people, and the lab once Sunyshore has been reached (`sy40` to `sy4f`); the Vista Lighthouse's door, the lift on its way up, the gallery, Volkner before the binoculars, his walk round to the lift, the binoculars' view, the car in its shaft and the ride down (`sy50` to `sy5g`, with the car's indicator close up on each ride, `sy51b` and `sy5fb`: the lift's own set piece, plan 09 · L12); Flint leaving the Gym's door, Volkner's battle and the Beacon Badge (`sy60` to `sy67`); and the beach: Jasmine, the rival running up beside the player, HM07 (`sy70` to `sy7b`). It wins every battle a scene starts (Azelf and Uxie are beaten, not caught) and prints what each left behind (the lab's states and the guardians' flags, where Mesprit roams, each cavern's flags, TM56, the city's state and where Flint stands, the lift's variable, Volkner's flags, the Badge and TM57, HM07), and `SUNYSHORE_FROM=lakes` starts it at the lakes with the professor's scene played, `SUNYSHORE_FROM=city` on Route 222, `SUNYSHORE_FROM=lighthouse` at the lighthouse's door with Flint met, `SUNYSHORE_FROM=gym` before the Gym with Volkner back in it, `SUNYSHORE_FROM=beach` on the beach with the Badge.

`tower` (not part of `all`) shows the Battle Frontier's frame and the Battle Tower (`bt*`, plan 06 · R18a) through the game's own `TryInteract`, `ArriveOnMap`, scripts, question boxes, team screen and battles: the Tower from the Frontier's grounds and its doors, the lobby from its door, its counters and its two wings, the judge reading a Pokémon, the records' board with nothing on it, the Wi-Fi counter (`bt01` to `bt0a`); the middle counter's questions, a team of three with a Dialga turned away and the banned species named, the team's screen with three entered in their order and one not able, two holding the same item refused (`bt10` to `bt16`); a Single Battle Room challenge: the save's question, the lift, the corridor and one of its doors, the battle room, a trainer stepping up, the battle with the Bag refused and RUN's question, the attendant healing the team and asking about the next battle, the seventh won, its Battle Points, the lobby's congratulations and the board afterwards (`bt17` to `bt2f`); the Tower Tycoon announced, walking in, his battle, beaten, his Battle Points, the silver print, the ribbon, the trophy and the board with a print (`bt30` to `bt39`); the Multi Battle Room: the west counter, the Battle Salon, a partner's offer, the corridor and the room for two, a pair stepping up, the battle beside the partner, the challenge put down and found waiting in the lobby, taken up again and given up (`bt40` to `bt4c`); and the Exchange Service Corner, a booth's list with the points, a prize traded for and one the points don't reach (`bt50` to `bt54`). It wins every battle (the foes are left a single HP at each menu) and prints what each left behind (who was entered, the set's trainers, each battle's teams with their items, natures and IVs, the EXP and money a battle gave, the records, rooms, Battle Points and print after each challenge, the ribbons, the secret base's PC, the partner and their two, what was put down).

`factory` (not part of `all`) shows the Battle Factory (`bf*`, plan 06 · R18b) through the game's own steps, `TryInteract`, `ArriveOnMap`, scripts, question boxes, rental screen and battles: the Factory from the Frontier's grounds and the walk in under its porch, the lobby from its door, its aisle, floor and counter, the records' board with nothing on it, the second counter and one of the lobby's people (`bf01` to `bf09`); the first counter's questions, the corridor, the staff's hint, the six on offer, a rental's menu and its summary, two chosen, REMOVE offered, the three and the question, the battle room, a trainer stepping up, the battle and its menu (`bf10` to `bf21`); the staff between battles, the next hint, the trade's question, one's own three, a Pokémon's menu, the beaten three, the question and the trade made, the seventh won, its Battle Points, the lobby's congratulations and the board afterwards (`bf22` to `bf2f`); the second round's hint, the challenge put down and found waiting, taken up again and given up (`bf30` to `bf37`); and the Factory Head: the third round's hint, his announcement, his coming, the number his machine gives, his battle, beaten, the twenty points, the silver print and the board with it (`bf40` to `bf49`). It wins every battle (the foes are left a single HP at each menu), waits for the rentals' pictures before it shoots their screen, and prints what each left behind (the round's trainers, the six with their items, natures and IVs, the three rented, each battle's teams, the trades, the records, Battle Points and print after each challenge, what was put down).

`champion` (not part of `all`) shows the eleventh chapter (`ch*`, plan 02 · S14), played by its own scripts through the game's own `TryInteract`, steps and arrivals: Victory Road from its mouth, the collector before its back rooms, the boulders of its upper floor, and a statue of the League's steps read (`ch01` to `ch05`); the League's Pokémon Center, the rival running in at the first step onto the row before the guard, his words, his battle, his leaving, and the guard counting the Badges off and stepped aside (`ch10` to `ch1a`); the Hall of Fame: the walk up, the professor's and Cynthia's words, the balls laid on the machine one, three and all (`ch20` to `ch28`, with the machine close up while they are plain and while lit, `ch27b` and `ch28b`: the machine's own set piece, plan 09 · L12); the scene of the team's entry: a Pokémon coming in, its card beginning, the card of each of the six whole, one going out, the Champion stepping up, the team coming in, the title, the paper falling and the picture closing (`ch30` to `ch44`); the save's line over the dark (`ch45`); the credits: the title card, five sections of the roll with the team beside it, the last card (`ch50` to `ch56`); and home: awake in the bedroom, Mom's news downstairs, the assistant in Sandgem Town, the Day Care's fan and Victory Road's way to its back rooms (`ch60` to `ch67`). It wins the rival's battle, holds the credits' button between shots (`CreditsScreen.Held`), and prints what each left behind (the rival's variable and flag, the machine's balls, everything the victory sets, the record and the ribbons, each card, the Champion's line, where the save was written, the roll's size, where the game goes on, Mom's flags, who the victory brought on); it deletes the save it wrote. `CHAMPION_FROM=center` starts it in the League's Pokémon Center, `CHAMPION_FROM=hall` in the Hall of Fame, `CHAMPION_FROM=credits` at the credits with the victory recorded, `CHAMPION_FROM=home` in the player's room afterwards.

`postgame` (not part of `all`) shows the time after the Hall of Fame, part a (`pg*`, plan 02 · S15), played by its own scripts through the game's own `TryInteract`, steps and arrivals: the professor's rating of the Pokédex, the assistant before the lab, Professor Oak's visit, the upgrade and the Poké Radar (`pg01` to `pg0h`); the assistant's grandfather and sister, her questions, her offer and the radar's lesson on Route 202 with the camera sent to the grass (`pg10` to `pg1d`); Pal Park's doors without their workers, Professor Oak in its lobby, the Trainer Counter and the rival's record, Professor Oak at the table in Eterna City with the Upgrade and the three birds, Byron in Iron Island's cabin and Victory Road's way to its back rooms (`pg20` to `pg2i`); the ship: Snowpoint's sailor asked and refused, Cynthia come down to the quay, the sailing (`pg30` to `pg38`); the Fight Area: the rival on the pier, the gate with Flint, Volkner and their crowd, the offer refused, each of them and an onlooker spoken to, both blockades kept, the offer taken, the tag battle, Palmer out of the gate, the rival's leaving, Buck, and the roads open (`pg40` to `pg4x`); Route 227's rival and Crasher Wake, Buck's question refused and granted, the Survival Area's step empty and then with Buck and the rival, on a weekday and at the weekend with his battle (`pg50` to `pg5k`); Marley in Victory Road's back room: waiting, asked along, walking behind, spoken to, a pair's tag battle beside her, sent back, taken through and gone (`pg60` to `pg6d`, the fog lifted after the first as Defog lifts it); Lucian in Canalave's library and Looker at the Game Corner (`pg70` to `pg75`). It wins every battle a scene starts (a Garchomp leads the team), makes the day a Saturday for the rival's battle, sets what part b's last scene will set for the Survival Area's two and for Looker, and prints what each left behind (the Pokédex seen and upgraded, the Poké Radar, the assistant's states, the workers, the lobby's app and record, the Upgrade and the birds, the Metal Coat, where the ship put the player, the Fight Area's state and every flag of its scene, the blockades, Route 227's states, the rival's team, Marley's state and who travels along, Lucian's flag). `POSTGAME_FROM=radar` starts it before the lab with the National Pokédex, `POSTGAME_FROM=palpark` on Route 221, `POSTGAME_FROM=ship` on Snowpoint's quay, `POSTGAME_FROM=fight` on the Fight Area's pier, `POSTGAME_FROM=zone` on Route 227 with the tag battle won, `POSTGAME_FROM=marley` in Victory Road, `POSTGAME_FROM=library` in Canalave.

`hall` (not part of `all`) shows the Battle Hall (`bh*`, plan 06 · R18c) through the game's own steps, `TryInteract`, `ArriveOnMap`, scripts, question boxes, team screen, board of types and battles: the Hall from the Frontier's grounds and the walk in at its door, the lobby from its mat, its floor, counter and seats, the records' board with nothing on it, the record keeper, the second counter and the fan (`bh01` to `bh0a`); the first counter's question, the team with one Pokémon to enter and entered, the save's question, the passage down, the stage and its announcer, the board as it opens, a type under the cursor, its question, the entered Pokémon's place and its summary, the battle called, the trainer on the stage, the battle and its menu (`bh10` to `bh20`); the Pokémon restored, the question between battles, the board with a win on it, another type, the tenth won, its Battle Points, the lobby's congratulations, the board afterwards, the record keeper's count and its Battle Point, the fan (`bh21` to `bh2c`); a challenge put down, found waiting, taken up again and given up (`bh30` to `bh36`); a Double challenge: the team for two, two of a kind entered, the board with both, the battle against the same Pokémon twice (`bh40` to `bh47`); and the Hall Matron: the bigger crowd of a fifth round, the board with four types cleared, her notice, her place lit, its question, her coming, her battle, beaten, the twenty points, the silver print and the board with it (`bh50` to `bh5c`). It wins every battle (the foes are left a single HP), chooses the first type not yet beaten ten times where a shot asks for none, and prints what each left behind (the type chosen with the wins over it, the first battle's trainer, Pokémon and level, each battle's teams with their IVs, natures and personalities, the records by species, the Battle Points and the print after each challenge, the record keeper's next mark, what was put down).

`castle` (not part of `all`) shows the Battle Castle (`bk*`, plan 06 · R18c) through the game's own steps, `TryInteract`, `ArriveOnMap`, scripts, question boxes, team screen, counters and battles: the Castle from the Frontier's grounds and the walk in at its door, the lobby from its mat, its floor, counter and south end, the records' board with nothing on it and the second counter (`bk01` to `bk08`); the first counter's question, the team with three to enter and entered, the save's question, the valet's room, his welcome and his services with the Castle Points over the field, the throne room, a trainer coming forward, the battle and its menu, the valet with the Castle Points it earned, and the question between battles (`bk10` to `bk20b`); the counter for one's own three: the three, a Pokémon's menu, the healing counter, its question, HP restored, a rank that is wanted, the rental counter, the berries, the question, a berry rented, the summary and the moves (`bk21` to `bk2c`); the counter about the trainer to come: nobody known, the menu, one identified, its level raised, the info menu, its stats, the moves refused and the three with one known (`bk30` to `bk39`); the second trainer, the Castle Points for a raised foe, the seventh through, its Battle Points, the lobby's congratulations and the board afterwards (`bk3a` to `bk44`); the valet brief in the second round, the challenge put down, found waiting, taken up again and given up (`bk50` to `bk57`); a battle passed in the fourth round: the pass explained, offered, asked about and presented in the throne room (`bk60` to `bk66`); and the Castle Valet: announced, coming forward, his battle, beaten, the twenty points from his own hand, the silver print and the board with it (`bk70` to `bk77`). It wins every battle with the three left as the battle left them, since that is the Castle's own rule (the foes are left a single HP; whoever leads is kept whole only in the Castle Valet's battle), and prints what each left behind (the round's trainers, the three as they went in beside the team's own, each battle's teams and what it left of the three, the Castle Points held, spent and on record, the counters' ranks, what was put down with HP and items, the Battle Points and the print).

`legends` (not part of `all`) shows the time after the Hall of Fame, part b (`le*`, plan 02 · S15), played by its own scripts through the game's own `TryInteract`, steps and arrivals: Stark Mountain's road to the pool of lava, the grunts seen across it, their walk to the mouth and in (`le01` to `le0a`); the first room: Mars, her battle, Jupiter's turn and battle, the two walking out on Team Galactic, Charon going on for the stone, the room empty (`le10` to `le1g`); the great room: Buck running up where the way opens out, joining, walking behind, spoken to, a pair's tag battle beside him, waiting when the player turns back, joining again, and going ahead into the heart (`le20` to `le2f`); the mountain's heart: Charon before the Magma Stone, the tremor, Buck calling out, the stone taken, the Croagunk snatching it back, Looker out of his rock, the grunts' flight, the policemen and Charon led off (`le30` to `le3j`); the parting before the mouth, the heart empty and the Survival Area's two (`le40` to `le4b`); Heatran at the head of the hall, its cry, its battle and its going (`le50` to `le54`); Aaron's rematch (`le60`); Snowpoint Temple's door, the guard, Candice coming up the street, the player let in and the hall (`le70` to `le76`); Regigigas on its dais, read as a statue, stirring for the three (the statue itself shaken, close up thrown east and west, `le7ab` and `le7ac`: plan 09 · L12), its battle, the dais empty and the town come out to again (`le77` to `le7f`); the sailor's son in his bed, his mother, the boy looked at, the sailor's question on the quay, Fullmoon Island's pier and wood, Cresselia, its flight (a moment of its flicker it isn't seen and one it is, `le8ba` and `le8bb`), the Lunar Wing found, the island's sailor, the boy woken, his father running in and going back to his boat, the house the visit after, and the sailor's question of where to sail (`le80` to `le9c`). It wins every battle a scene starts (Heatran and Regigigas are beaten, not caught), sets the Battleground's flag by hand for Heatran (`FLAG_TALKED_TO_BATTLEGROUND_BUCK`, which Buck sets in his room: the `epilogue` mode), puts Regirock, Regice and Registeel in the team for Regigigas, and prints what each left behind (the outside's and the three rooms' states and flags, Mars's and Jupiter's teams, who travels along, everything the arrest sets, Heatran's flags and its return after the Hall of Fame, the rematch teams' levels, the temple's state and where its door leads, Regigigas's flags, the nightmare's variable at each step, where the ship lands, where Cresselia roams and at what level, the Lunar Wing). `LEGENDS_FROM=rooms` starts it in the great room, `LEGENDS_FROM=heart` at the way into the mountain's heart, `LEGENDS_FROM=heatran` with Charon caught, `LEGENDS_FROM=league` at the League, `LEGENDS_FROM=temple` in Snowpoint City, `LEGENDS_FROM=nightmare` in the sailor's house, `LEGENDS_FROM=island` on Fullmoon Island's pier.

`epilogue` (not part of `all`) shows the time after the Hall of Fame, part c (`ep*`, plan 02 · S15), played by its own scripts through the game's own `TryInteract`, steps and arrivals, in the order a game comes to it: the Old Chateau's room with the television, the set by day, after dark, its question, the thump, Rotom's battle, its slipping back and the set afterwards (`ep01` to `ep0a`); the assistant's word of the elder, the elder at home reading from her book, and the assistant's word of Spear Pillar (`ep10` to `ep16`); the two orbs in Mt. Coronet and one picked up, the floor under the summit, Dialga's pillar from its way up, its altar and its rift, the rift read, the question, the cry, the battle and the rift quiet, and Palkia's the climb after (`ep20` to `ep2e`); the Battleground from the street with Buck before its door, the door, the old man looking up, his welcome, the room from three places with a day's four guests, Buck's words and his leaving, Roark asked, refused, asked again and battled, his father, Fantina and Cheryl each asked and battled with what they say afterwards, the old man across his bar, and another day's draw (`ep30` to `ep54`); and the ruins: Cynthia coming in after the player, each of her three questions and what she tells between them, her leaving, the painting alone, and her cut short at the first question after the next victory (`ep60` to `ep6c`). It wins every battle a scene starts (Rotom, Dialga and Palkia are beaten, not caught), makes the hour night for Rotom, sets a day's draw for the Battleground so that every place is taken (the last shot's is the game's own), sets by hand what parts a and b leave (the Hall of Fame, the National Pokédex, Charon's arrest), and prints what each left behind (Rotom's two flags, the elder's, where the mountain's way out leads at each step and the two flags that say so, each rift's state, what the next victory puts back, the door, the welcome's state and who is in, Buck's three flags, each rematch team, the count of battles and the day's flags, the next day's draw, Heatran awake in Stark Mountain, Cynthia's two flags). `EPILOGUE_FROM=elder` starts it in Sandgem Town, `EPILOGUE_FROM=summit` in Mt. Coronet with the elder heard, `EPILOGUE_FROM=battleground` before its door with Charon caught, `EPILOGUE_FROM=painting` in the ruins.

`arcade` (not part of `all`) shows the Battle Arcade (`ba*`, plan 06 · R18d) through the game's own steps, `TryInteract`, `ArriveOnMap`, scripts, question boxes, team screen, game board and battles: the Arcade from the Frontier's grounds and the walk in at its door, the lobby from its mat, its floor, counter and lounge, the records' board with nothing on it, the second counter and one of the lobby's people (`ba01` to `ba09`); the first counter's question, the team with three to enter and entered, the save's question, the corridor, the game room, the host's welcome and a trainer walking in (`ba10` to `ba19`); the game board coming up, counting down, with its events shown and the cursor running, and stopped with every panel showing the event, then the event announced, the battle under it and its menu, the attendant restoring the three and the question between games (`ba20` to `ba2b`); the events of a first round one after another: the trainer's three poisoned and their battle, a berry lent and the board with it held, a faster cursor, a jumping one and the board under both, and nothing (`ba30` to `ba3d`); the seventh won, its Battle Points, the lobby's congratulations and the board of records afterwards (`ba40` to `ba44`); a game under a sandstorm, the challenge put down, found waiting, taken up again and given up (`ba50` to `ba57`); a fifth round: its first board as drawn, the teams swapped and the battle fought with the trainer's three, a Battle Point handed over and a battle passed (`ba60` to `ba6b`); and the Arcade Star: announced, coming up through the crowd, her battle, beaten, the twenty points, the silver print and the board with it (`ba70` to `ba79`). It wins every battle (the foes are left a single HP and whoever leads is kept on its feet; in the Arcade Star's battle her three are left only Struggle, since a Medicham at one HP fells all three with Reversal), stops each board's cursor by the frame, makes every wait for the two threes' pictures a whole three seconds (how long the bakers take must not move the clock, or two runs would differ), shows an event it wants by rigging the board's panels (`ArcadeBoard.Rig`, which the game never calls: the cursor is still stopped by the board's own rules), and prints what each left behind (the round's trainers, the three as they went in beside the team's own, every board with its round, streak, the last battle's score and row of weights, speed, sixteen panels, where it stopped after how many frames and the number it drew, each battle's teams, turns and what it left, the score, the records, the Battle Points with those the board gave, the print, and what was put down).

`townmap` (not part of `all`) shows the map of the region (`tm*`, plan 01 · M12) through the game's own bag, `UseFieldItem`, `OpenTownMap` and the screen's own buttons: the Town Map used out of the bag and put away again (`tm01`), the cursor at home, on a route, on a town not visited yet, on the sea, on a city with the way come behind the player, on a landmark found and one not, on the mountain, zoomed in on a city, on the mine and in a corner of the map, on a hidden island before and after its variable is set, on the Spring Path revealed, and the map opened from inside a room (`tm02` to `tm16`); Fly's choice as it opens, on an open town, on one not visited, on Pal Park before and after its gate has been come to, and zoomed in (`tm20` to `tm25`); a wall's map, with no way come (`tm30`); the picture by itself at the screen's own resolution, whole and zoomed in (`tm40`, `tm41`); and the Pokédex's area page on the same picture (`tm50`, `tm51`). It prints what each shot stands on (the mode, the cursor's cell, whether the map is zoomed, the place under the cursor and whether it is told of, Fly's place there and whether it is open, the player's cell and place, the way come) and what the bag, the A button over a shut place and the picture's repainting left behind.

`amity` (not part of `all`) shows Amity Square's walk (`am*`, plan 06 · R16c) through the game's own steps, warps, scripts and question boxes: Hearthome's west gate and its receptionist, who only talks, and the square's lobby beyond its door (`am01` to `am03`); the lane before the counter, a team with none of the square's species told which they are and turned back a step (`am04` to `am07`); a team with two of them asked about the first, the Pokémon let out behind a dark screen, the two stepping into the square (`am10` to `am12`); the walk north, east, south and west with the Pokémon on the tile just left, a step caught in its hop, the two changed places as the player walks back into it (`am13` to `am18`); its mood, two hundred steps on the lawn, "is holding something" and what it found (`am20` to `am22`); a hut of the ruins from before its door, in its door, inside, out of the hut it leads to and two steps on (`am30` to `am34`); the east half: the east hut, the woman and her Happiny on the north ground, the man of the park wherever the day has him, his question and his gift, the Spooky Plate in its hollow (`am40` to `am47`); the goodbye at the lane and the player alone again (`am50`, `am51`); in again with no to the first and yes to a Torterra, which keeps two tiles behind, north, east, south and west, and its mood from the tile between (`am52` to `am57`); every species the square lets in behind the player, facing east and walking north, as crops at the screen's own resolution (`am6a_<species>_east`, `am6b_<species>_north`: `contact am6a` puts the twenty on one sheet); and a game saved on a walk taken up again with the Pokémon out, and walking on (`am70`, `am71`). It prints what each left behind (the story's variables of the walk, who walks and where, the man's place and gift, the next find as decided, the bag's berries and the Fashion Case, the Spooky Plate) and deletes the save it wrote.

`fulldex` (not part of `all`) weighs the game with every species in it (plan 03 · D13, `Modes/FullDex.cs`): it prints where the start went step by step (the log's phases, `Log.Phases`, plan 16 · T3), what a Pokémon of every species and form costs to make, what a model drawn and let go two hundred times leaves behind (nothing), the National Pokédex complete and walked down a species a frame, back up and through its entries (the frames' times; the memory, the models, the menu sprites and the cries held after each), Platinum's eighteen boxes full and the National Pokédex's hundred with one of every species, a save of it all (its size, the time to write and to read it), and forty wild battles against species never met; then it shoots the screens of a full game (`fd*`: the two diplomas, the National list at its first, at Arceus and at its last species, an entry's three pages, the eighteen boxes full, the hundred with their track). A line that begins with `!!` is something out of its bounds: memory that grew where it must not, more models or sprites held than the game allows. `FULLDEX_ONLY=start,data,uploads,pokedex,boxes,save,battles,shots` runs only those parts and `FULLDEX_WALK=150` goes only that far down the list. Its megabytes and counts are the same on any machine; its milliseconds want a quiet one. Given a `POKEMON_CACHE` with models and no sprites in it, the walk bakes every sprite: the first time a player opens a complete Pokédex.

`saves` (not part of `all`) writes no shots (plan 24 · X3, `Modes/Saves.cs`): saves of today's game, each the engine's own `BuildSave` (`Driver.BuildSave`) written as `SaveManager.Write` writes a slot's save, into `<out dir>/saves/` as `w<world>-s<story>-v<save>-<moment>.json`: a new game in the player's room once the television has had its say, past the first chapter in front of the house, surfing on Twinleaf Town's pond, on the deck of Canalave City's bridge, a Safari Game under way and the girl. It prints where each stands and what it holds. They go into the test project's corpus of saves (`PokemonPlatinumTests/Fixtures/saves`, below).

`crash` (not part of `all`) shows the crash handler at work without a real game crashing (plan 16 · T3, `Modes/Crash.cs`): it saves the game as the save screen does, goes on a little, then runs the game's loop inside the game's own guard (`Program.Guarded`, exactly as `Program.Main` does) and throws in it on purpose. It opens a log in its out dir, which no other mode does, and prints what the crash left there (`logs/crash-<date>.txt` whole, `savegame.crash.json` read back, the note, the log's last lines) and that the player's own save is byte for byte what it was; then it does what the next start does (the save read, the note taken up) and shoots the title's notice and the menu after it (`crash_1_title_notice`, `crash_2_title_menu`), crashes a second time to show what is kept of the first, tells the game its window isn't in front with the options' "Pause in the background" off and on and prints what moved in thirty frames (the wait behind another window, which only `Program.Main` can tell a real game of), and deletes the save it wrote. The date in the files' names is the harness's fixed one. `crash thread` throws on another thread instead, which nothing can catch: the files are written from `AppDomain.UnhandledException` and the run ends there with the runtime's own exit code, as the game's would. The same handler was seen on the real executable by starting it hidden with `--crash-after <frames>`; the `title` mode's `title_21` to `title_23` show the notice from made-up notes (a save intact with a copy kept, nothing saved, and a crash followed by a save that couldn't be read), and the `look` mode's `look_13*` the options' two rows of the same session.

`formats` (not part of `all`) shows the formats of the later games (`ft*`, plan 14, `Modes/Formats.cs`; B1, inverse battles) through the game's own `TryInteract`, scripts, question box and battle menus: the Fight Area's plaza before the tag battle (nobody by the Mart) and after it with the scientist (`ft00a`, `ft00b`); what an inverse battle is, his offer, a refusal, the offer again and taken (`ft00c` to `ft00i`); the battle's own line as it opens, its menu and the move cards' hints, which tell the battle's chart (`ft00j` to `ft00l`); then a hit Platinum's chart resists, one a type would have stopped and one that was four times as strong, each with the move's own line (`ft01_inverse_line`, `ft02_inverse_line_an_immunity`, `ft03_inverse_line_a_weakness`, and `<shot>_a_move`), a Ground move kept off by Magnet Rise all the same (`ft03b`) and the first hit of the scientist's own Pokémon that the chart has a word for (`ft04`); his word after a win, the same day's "come back tomorrow", the next day's offer (the harness moves `GameClock.FixedDate` on a day, so the game's own daily events clear the flag) and his word after a battle lost (`ft05` to `ft09`). The player's three Nidoking and the scientist's Magneton are made whole at every menu, so each hit is seen on a full bar; the foe's moves are the trainer AI's, so `ft03b` and `ft04` are shot when they happen and the mode prints whether they did. It prints what each left behind (the day's flag, the battle's chart, its field's and its record's, the team, each hit with what was said of it and the HP either side, the money, who is beaten). Later sessions of plan 14 add their shots to it: B14's are last, the party screen choosing a Bidoof with a Starly greyed (`ft60_master_choose`) and the Bidoof Master's battle with BAG greyed (`ft61_master_bag_closed`), and it prints the master beaten, the Bidoof's level and the money.

`skips` (not part of `all`) takes no shots (plan 12 · Q20, `Modes/Skips.cs`): it plays four of the story's scenes to their end and then, with the story and everyone put back as they were, skips each by the hold (`Driver.SkipHeld`), and tells what the two left side by side as `opening` does for the lab's: the speech in Team Galactic's hall (the camera kit, the ranks leaving), the meeting in Canalave's library (a leader and a follower round the stairwell, three people sent off, the explosion's ripple and its lines over a black screen) the torn pillar (the three of the lakes shown and gone, the camera sent and brought back), which ends at Cynthia's question, and the assistant at Sandgem Town's edge, whose scene warps into the lab (a hurry arrives by a warp at once, and the lab's own scene then begins as it does played). A line that begins with `!!` is a difference, or something that never happened. It is the engine's own check: the tests play every scene both ways on a host with no screen, where a walk is over as it is told, and only the game walks people a frame at a time. A scene added to it gives its room's name, so that whoever the first telling walked about stands where they stood for the second.

`demo` and `menus` also show the level-up panel (plan 08 · P5). In the demo battle the lead is a point short of its level as the foe faints, and after the faint the level's line is shot with the panel's gains over it and, a press on, with its new values (`d26_level_gains`, `d27_level_stats`); the battle is left there, as it was left at the faint, because a battle read to its end is taken down by the game, which pays its money and rolls for what a battle leaves behind (`AfterBattle`, on `Dice`), and every later shot would move. `menus` ends on a Rare Candy used from the bag, the panel over the team's cards on both pages (`22l_bag_rare_candy_gains`, `22m_bag_rare_candy_stats`), and puts the Pokémon and the candy back as they were. Both print the panel's numbers beside the Pokémon's own: read those lines with the pictures. A level in any mode's battle now takes two more confirms, so a mode that wins battles in which a Pokémon levels has run a little longer by its later shots, and what moves with the clock (the message box's arrow, an icon's hop) can stand elsewhere in them.

Platinum's battle options (plan 08 · P1) are shot at the end of four modes, so nothing before them moves: `battle` ends on Battle Style Shift's offer in a battle of its own (`23b_battle_offer`, the team its Yes opens `23c_battle_offer_team`, and the switch made, `23d_battle_offer_switched`), `look` on the three rows (`look_14*`, and after them the pad's three rows and the hints on the pad, `look_15*`, plan 12 · Q4), `demo` on three moves with the scene off (`d40_menu_scene_off` to `d44_after_scene_off`) and `conditions` on a battle in the rain with the scene off (`96_scene_off_*`); each prints what was offered or what the scene left out (the cues, the HP as the bar has it). Every other mode stays in at every offer through `Confirm`, and prints an `offer:` line there.

The camera kit's shots (plan 09 · L4) are the last of three modes, so nothing before them moves. `story` ends with a second scene written in the harness that uses every command (`st80` to `st86`: the bars and a zoom in with a line read across the lower bar, the camera kept on someone while the player walks off and while she walks, a zoom out on a path toward the rival's house and at its end, a walk under slow motion, everything released); it prints the rig at each (`zoom`, `bars`, `pace`, `away`, `moving`), what streaming cost on the path and how many frames two tiles took slowed. `life` ends with the lens alone, set through `game.World.Camera` (`l50` to `l56`: Twinleaf Town zoomed in and its native crop, zoomed out to twice the ground, and the same sent to the town's west side, where the view's edges and their shadows are looked at; a row of people zoomed in and out, where every sprite across the picture must stand as straight as at the place's own lens; the Pokémon Center at its own lens and zoomed in on the player at its PC, the camera off the room's middle). `title` ends with the loop (`title_16` to `title_20`: the title half a second before its half minute is up, the fly-over shots played again at the same moments as `title_2` and `title_4`, the reveal again, and the flash of a button pressed in the replay); it prints the phase at each.

The slots' shots (plan 12 · Q2) are the last of `title`, after the crash's notices, and of `menus`. `title` goes on with saves made up in the mode, as its first one is, with no file behind them (`title_24` to `title_42`): two saves and NEW GAME's question naming the slot it takes, three with the cursor on each card and a native crop of two (`title_26b`), NEW GAME refused with every slot taken, DELETE A SAVE asking which, its question and the column once the slot is gone, a slot that can't be read with its notice, its card and the question a press on it asks, one save alone in the third slot with its native crop (`title_39b`), the last save deleted, and a crash's notice with several saves. It prints the phase, the entry under the cursor, the cards and the slot a new game would take at each. `menus` ends with a game saved in the third slot (`31c`, `31d`) and prints the file that was written and how the three slots read. The one save a mode writes is its game's slot's (`game.SaveFile`, in the out dir), and it takes it away again with `game.DeleteSave()`.

**Two runs draw the same pictures.** The harness seeds the game's chance (`Dice.Seed`) and counts its own clock (`FrameClock.Fixed`), so a shot is the same every run and `diff` lists exactly the shots a change altered (the most changed first, with a board for each: the other run's shot, this one's, their difference eight times stronger). Before changing how anything is drawn, run the mode (or `all`) into a folder; afterwards run it again and `diff` the two, then look at what differs with `crop`. Modes carry state from one to the next (the party, the clock), so compare a mode with the same mode and `all` with `all`. Whatever is new in the engine must take its chance from `Dice` and its time from `FrameClock`, `WallClock` or the game's own clocks: `new Random()`, `Random.Shared`, `Raylib.GetTime()`, `DateTime.Now` and a string's `GetHashCode()` (which changes from run to run) each make shots differ between runs, and each is a build error (plan 24 · X1). The harness fixes the wall clock too (`WallClock.Fixed`, noon of `GameClock.FixedDate`'s day). `tools/dev/baseline.sh <commit> <mode>...` does all of it: a worktree of the commit with its own harness, both trees on one `POKEMON_CACHE`, each mode run alone in both and `diff`ed (which exits 1 when a shot moved), and `tools/dev/bisect.sh` finds the commit that moved a shot. While other sessions change the same tree, the way to tell a change of the renderer from theirs is a copy: `baseline.sh <commit> --only <path>... <mode>` runs the commit's tree with only those files of the working tree over it as the "after" (by hand: `git archive <commit> | tar -x -C <scratch>`, build its harness there, run it for the "before", and copy only the files of the change over it for the "after"). The same copy is how a change to the world's data is compared: every trainer placed draws from the game's seeded chance as the maps load, so adding people moves every battle and every later shot of `all`. Run the field modes one at a time in both trees (`field`, `world`, `terrain`, `buildings`, `lab`, `life`, `look`) and `diff` each pair.

**Where a frame goes.** `profile` (not part of `all`) times the heavy scenes (the towns and routes, a room, night, rain, fog, a battle at its menu and the same in rain, four moves at three moments each held still, a double battle, and a double battle in a sandstorm with every hazard and screen on both sides, `EverythingOnTheField` in `Modes/ConditionsField.cs`) and then runs each with `FrameProfiler` on: a line of the milliseconds of every pass (shadows, scene, blur, bloom, occlusion, composite, interface, present, swap) with the meshes and triangles it draws, and first how long the engine took to start and how long arriving in a town not seen yet took. `SHOTS_WINDOW=3840x2160` makes the hidden window that size, which is the game full screen on a 4K display (FXAA on); `SHOTS_PRESETS=High,Medium,Low` runs the presets in turn; `SHOTS_ONLY=battle,route` keeps the scenes whose names have one of those words. About 1 ms of every frame the harness times is the hidden window's own swap of its buffers (3 ms when the window is 4K). Time nothing while a build or another harness run is going: either shows as milliseconds. Even so a scene's frames fall in two groups (plan 01 · M12, "To be timed"): the harness shares the graphics card with the desktop, whose compositor takes the card once for every refresh of the display and holds the frame then in flight up by a millisecond or two. A quarter to a half or more of a scene's frames are slow for it (the longer the frame, the more), so its mean and its median move by tenths of a millisecond from one run to the next, and by half a millisecond where a frame is half a refresh long (8.3 ms at 60 Hz, which is where the battles are in a 4K window): the median then goes from one group to the other, and a scene nobody touched reads 7% slower. What two runs of the same code agree on, to a few hundredths of a millisecond, is the scene's **floor**, its 10th percentile: the frames nothing held up. So judge a change by the floor, and by runs made back to back: before, after, before again. `tools/dev/ab.sh <commit> [words]` runs those three (each writes `profile.json`: each scene's median, its 10th and 90th percentiles and the passes' medians) and `ab` prints each scene's three floors and their change, or "within noise" where it is smaller than the two befores' difference or than `Timings.FloorGrain`, with the medians beside them. Something else that takes the card for a while raises even the floor: a scene whose 90th percentile stands three milliseconds or more over its floor was disturbed and is timed again. The mode's battles have teams, foes and generators of their own made from fixed numbers, never from the game's seeded chance, which every session that puts people on the maps moves: a battle added to the mode is made the same way, or its picture is another one from one commit to the next. Since plan 01 · M12 the scenes go on through the rest of Sinnoh (forty more after the Pokémon Center: the towns where Fly lands, the routes, caves and rooms on the open tile nearest the middle of their ground, five Gyms, the largest halls); `SHOTS_LOOK=1` saves a picture of each scene as it stands when it is timed (`profile_<scene>`), and with a `SHOTS_ONLY` that names no scene the mode goes to every place and times nothing, which is how a new scene is looked at.

The hidden window never shows the screen 1:1 or in full screen. For window problems (full screen, resizing, DPI), write a small program in the scratchpad that mirrors `Program.Main` for a few seconds and logs `GetScreenWidth/Height`, focus and window flags each frame; a full-screen window will appear on the user's display while it runs, so say so first and keep the runs few.

## Architecture

### Game loop and states

`Program.cs` opens the window and calls `GameEngine.Update(dt)` then `GameEngine.Draw()` each frame. `GameEngine` (Core) owns everything: the current `Map` and `Player`, the party, bag, Pokédex and PC storage, the active `BattleEngine`, one instance of each menu screen, and a `GameState` enum that decides which of them updates and draws. State changes that swap scenes go through `StartTransition(next, onMidpoint, kind)`: close over the scene, run the callback (load the map, create the battle), open on the next. The kind is a plain fade, or one of the ways into a battle (`SceneTransition`). `GameEngine` is one class in several files: `GameEngine.cs`, `GameEngine.Scripts.cs` for the story's scripts ("The story and its scripts", below), `GameEngine.Battles.cs` for the battles the field starts (`StartWildBattle`, `StartTrainerBattle`, `PrepareModels`, `EndBattle`), `GameEngine.Encounters.cs` and `GameEngine.Gyms.cs`, and `GameEngine.Driving.cs`, the `Driver` the screenshot harness drives it through.

The game starts in `GameState.Title`: `TitleScreen` plays the opening and shows the menu. A save's card calls `ContinueGame(slot)`; NEW GAME goes to `GameState.Intro`, where `IntroScreen` asks who the player is and who the rival is, and ends in `StartNewGame(name, look, rival)` (every game by the modern rules, plan 06 · R19). Both load the game and fade into the field. Nothing in `currentMap`, `player` or the party is valid before one of them has run.

Saving is a question over the field (`GameState.SaveMenu`, `SaveScreen`): the engine builds the save (`BuildSave`), the screen shows its summary and names the slot, and the engine writes it when the screen asks (`TakeRequest`). `SaveManager` (plan 12 · Q1) writes a save to a temporary file beside it (`save1.json.tmp`), copies the old save to its backup (`save1.bak`) if it reads whole and moves the temporary file over the save; the file's first field is its `Checksum` (FNV-1a of the file with the field read as `null`, carriage returns passed over; a file without one loads on trust), and `SaveManager.Load` says what happened (`SaveLoad`: `Ok`, `None`, `Recovered` from the backup, `Damaged`), which the title tells before its menu (`TitlePhase.Warning`, `TitleNotice`). The window's close button doesn't end the loop: `Program.Main` calls `RequestClose`, and `GameState.WindowClosing` asks `ClosePrompt`'s question over whatever is on screen (SAVE AND QUIT only in the field with nothing under way; on the title before any game it quits at once).

**Where the saves live, and the slots** (plan 12 · Q2; all GPU-free but what draws the cards, and `ArchitectureTests` holds `SavePaths`, `SaveEnvironment` and `SaveSlots` free of raylib).

- **One place decides the folder and names every file in it**: `Core/SavePaths.cs`. `SavePaths.Resolve(machine, portable, chosen)` picks the folder by the rules under "Commands" and only reads whether a few files are there: it makes no folder and writes nothing, so a test asks it of a made-up `SaveEnvironment`. `SavePaths.Current` is what the running game uses: `Slot(n)`, `SettingsFile`, `LogFile`, `CrashSave`, `CrashReports` (and `Autosave` and `Screenshots`, named for plan 12 · Q3 and Q16, and the Vs. Recorder's `Replays`, plan 08 · P10). **Whatever the game writes asks it, never the working directory**: a later file of the game's (a suspended battle, the Home box) is a property there. `SaveManager` takes a path for everything and has no default one.
- **Only `Program.Main` sets `SavePaths.Current` to this machine's real answer** (`Program.Locate`, `Program.Settle`, which makes the folder), and only `Program` may call `SaveEnvironment.Here` (a rule of `ArchitectureTests`; `SavePathsTests` holds the test project to the same). Until something names it, `Current` is the working directory, which is what a test or a tool that makes an engine of its own gets, so nothing but the game itself ever writes in a user's own folder. `GameSettings.Load()` reads the options beside the saves and `Save()` writes them back where they were read (`ReadFrom`); `CrashReport.SaveFolder` is `Current`'s folder.
- **The slots** are `Core/SaveSlots.cs`: `Read` (the three, each a `SaveSlot` with its `SaveLoad`), `FirstEmpty` (a slot is empty when neither its save nor its backup is there), `Write`, `Delete` (the backup first and the save last, so a deletion cut short never leaves an older game in the slot) and `AdoptOldSave`, which `Initialize` runs before it reads the slots: an install's one old `savegame.json`, with its backup, is renamed into the first empty slot, never copied and never over anything, so it is byte for byte what it was. The two renames are not one step, so the slot is written down first (`savegame.moving`) and a start that finds the note finishes that move; with every slot taken the old save is left where it is and the title says so.
- **The game is saved in one slot** (`GameEngine.saveSlot`): the one it was continued from, or the first empty one a new game took (the title's `Slot`; a game begun without the title takes the first empty one too). **Every save the game writes goes through `GameEngine.WriteSave`**: the save screen's, SAVE AND QUIT's, the close button's, a script's `save`, the `Driver`'s. A tool asks the `Driver` for `SaveSlot`, `SaveFile` and `DeleteSave`, never a file's name (the harness sets its game to slot 1, whatever an earlier run left in its folder). A crash's report names the slot's file (`GameFacts.SaveFile`).
- **The title** (`TitleScreen`; style guide, "The slots") has a card for each slot that holds something (`Cards`, a `TitleSlot` each: `TitleScreen.WithSlots`; the old constructor is the title with slot 1 or nothing), then NEW GAME, DELETE A SAVE, OPTIONS and QUIT; `EntryRects` is where each stands, which drawing and tests share. A slot whose save and backup are both damaged is a card that can only be deleted (`TitleChoice.Unreadable`). The screen deletes nothing itself: after `TitlePhase.ChooseDelete` and `ConfirmDelete` it hands the slot over once (`TakeDeletion`), the engine deletes the files (`DeleteSlot`) and tells it (`Forget`, or `Tell` with a notice if they couldn't be deleted). A notice names its slot (`TitleNotice.ForSlot`; `ForSave` is slot 1's).
- **The autosave** (plan 12 · Q3; style guide, "Field menus and notices"; `Core/AutosaveFile.cs` GPU-free, `GameEngine.Autosave.cs`): the options' AUTOSAVE row (`GameSettings.Autosave`, off unless chosen) writes the game quietly into `autosave.json` (`SavePaths.Autosave`, with `.bak`, a checksum and `SaveData.AutosaveSlot` naming its slot; never a slot's own file) at the five moments: a battle over (`EndBattle`), a Pokémon caught (the same), a heal and a Badge (the script ops, through `IScriptHost.Milestone`, a default method no host with no screen need answer), and coming to a place (`ArriveOnMap`, held to a gap of `AutosaveArrivalGap` seconds of play, as every door is one). **A moment only asks** (`AutosaveSoon` sets `autosaveDue`); `KeepTheAutosave`, run every frame, writes when `CanSaveNow` holds (the field, no script, fade, trainer walking up, rod, trap or puzzle) and the run has not ended, so it is never in a battle and never while a script runs; `ModernUi.AutosaveDisc` turns in the bottom left corner for `AutosaveDiscSeconds` meanwhile. A save by hand of that slot takes the autosave away (`AutosaveFile.ClearFor`, in `WriteSave`, which leaves another slot's alone), deleting a slot takes its autosave, and the first autosave of a session replaces whatever a crash left. **After a crash** `AutosaveFile.Offer` (in `ReadTheSlots`) offers the autosave when its slot holds the same game (`TrainerId` and `Started`) with less time played, or holds nothing (a game never saved by hand: its card is a `TitleSlot` with `AutosaveOnly`); CONTINUE on a card with an `Autosave` asks `TitlePhase.ChooseAutosave` (SAVED GAME or AUTOSAVE), and `TitleScreen.UseAutosave` goes to `ContinueGame(slot, fromAutosave)`. Anything that reads a clock there goes through `WallClock`; the disc and the gap run on the game's own play time and frame time.

**The ways to play** (plan 13 · V1; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "Ways to play"): NEW GAME (after its YES, with saves) opens `TitlePhase.ChooseRun`, a `RunPanel` (`UI/Kit`, GPU-free: `Move`, `Change`, `Confirm`, rows by `RunRow`) over `RunRules` (`Models/RunRules.cs`: a record, presets Normal / Nuzlocke / Hardcore, `IsNormal`). Confirm on a Normal run starts at once, so the usual NEW GAME is one more press; the title hands `ChosenRun` over, `GameEngine.StartNewGame(name, look, rival, run)` keeps it in `GameFlow.Run` and `RunRules.Current` (set by the engine alone, like `Ruleset.Use`), `SaveData.Run` saves it whole (null for Normal and for every older save, so no version was raised), and it shows as `ModernUi.RunMark` on the save cards and as `CardPage.Run` of the Trainer Card. **V1 enforced nothing**: each later V session reads `flow.Run` where its rule lives. **Difficulty** (V2; rulings, "Challenge and Easy") is `Models/DifficultyRules.cs`: `StartTrainerBattle` hands each foe through `DifficultyRules.Prepare(trainer, flow.Run)`, the same object under Normal and a copy (`Trainer.Copied`) otherwise, so the maps' trainers and every shot stay as the import made them (the first battle and a Master Trainer's are skipped); Easy's EXP is `CoreSetup.Difficulty` read by `BattleCore.Give` (a `BattleSetup.Difficulty` the game fills from the run; a `BattleRecord` and its file keep it), and its prices are `ShopScreen.Difficulty` / `PriceOf` (set as the Shop screen opens), so a new place that charges asks `PriceOf`, never `item.Price`. **The level cap and scaling** (V3; rulings, "The level cap and trainers scaled to the lead") are `Models/LevelCaps.cs` (the table by region and Badge, `For(run, region, story)`; Sinnoh's only, uncapped once its Hall of Fame is entered) and `DifficultyRules.ScaleTo` (`Prepare(trainer, rules, leadLevel)`): the cap reaches a battle as `BattleSetup.RunLevelCap` / `CoreSetup.RunLevelCap` (0 for none; the engine fills it from `GameEngine.CurrentLevelCap()`, a `BattleRecord` and its file keep it) and clamps `BattleCore.Give`, and the bag as `BagScreen.RunCap` (set each frame) into `ItemUse` / `LaterItemUse`'s `runCap`. **The Nuzlocke** (V4; rulings, "The Nuzlocke") is `Models/Nuzlocke.cs` and `Models/RunState.cs` (`GameFlow.RunState`, saved as `SaveData.RunState`, null when empty): `StartWildBattle` asks `Nuzlocke.Meet` for the place (`PlaceKeyOf`) and hands the battle `NuzlockeTerms` (`BattleSetup` / `CoreSetup.Nuzlocke`, null under any other run), which `WhyNotItem` reads to refuse balls and revives; `EndBattle` buries the fallen (`Nuzlocke.Bury`) before anything heals them, a whiteout ends the run and writes the save once, after which **`WriteSave` refuses**; a catch is named on Q19's keyboard as the field comes back (`GameFlow.NameAfterBattle`, `NicknameReturn`). A new way the player's Pokémon can faint for good, or a new way to bring one round, asks the run. A new way to give EXP outside a battle asks `LevelCaps.Allowed` / `Holds` (the Day Care doesn't yet). The harness's `ways` mode (not part of `all`, `Modes/Ways.cs`) starts a game of its own under a run (`BeginRun`) and shoots `wy_level_cap_line` and Roark under Normal and Challenge; later V sessions add theirs. A new choice is a field of `RunRules`, a `RunRow` with its `run.row.*` and `run.help.*` words, and a case in `RunPanel.Change`. **The restrictions and the shiny odds** (V5; rulings, "Restrictions and shiny odds"): `Party.Limit` (an instance property, 6 by default, set by the engine from `PartyLimit`; read through `IsFull`), `RunRules.Has` and `if rule nocenter` (`Query.Rule`, `IScriptHost.Rules`, a default member), `RunTerms` on `BattleSetup` / `CoreSetup.Restrictions` (no catching, no running, a Monotype, no items but balls: refused in `WhyNot` / `WhyNotItem` for the player's side, `BattleCore.BagShutByTheRun` greying BAG), `RunRules.MayCarry` (a Monotype, the starter's line exempt) for `GivePokemon` and the PC (`PCScreen.WhyNotCarry`, kept by `KeepTheRunInView`), `OptionsScreen.StyleLocked`, and the shiny roll reading `RunRules.ShinyOdds` through `RunRules.Current`; the Shiny Charm is an item of the data and `ShinyCharm.Roll` the two extra rolls (nothing is drawn without it). A new restriction is a field of `RunTerms` and a case at its one place of enforcement, never a second check.

**The command line, the log and the crash handler** (plan 16 · T3; all GPU-free but the few lines of `Program.cs` and `GameEngine.Diagnostics.cs` that join them to the game).

- **The command line** is `Core/CommandLine.cs`: `CommandLine.Switches` is a table, a row to a switch (`Switch`: its name, what its value is called, what `--help` says, how it goes into the options), `Parse` reads the arguments once into a `LaunchOptions` and `Usage` prints `--help` from the same table. **A new switch is a value of `LaunchOptions` and a row at the table's end**, with what it does today said honestly in its help (`--verify` and `--dev` say their other half isn't built). `Program.Main` reads it once and keeps it as `CommandLine.Launched`; a tool or a test that makes its own engine leaves that as it is. Bad input (an unknown switch, a bare word, a missing or wrong value, switches that can't go together) is told a line each and the game exits with 2 before any window opens; the exit codes are `CommandLine.Ended` 0, `Crashed` 1, `BadCommandLine` 2 and `--verify`'s `Manifest.Differs` 3 and `NoManifest` 4. What a switch changes of the options it changes **for that run alone** (`GameSettings.SetForThisRun`: `settings.json` goes on holding what the player had until the player changes that row), so a switch that sets an option goes through it and never assigns the setting. `--safe-mode` also turns off the mesh cache (`SdfCache.Enabled`) and every hand-made file (`Graphics/Overrides.Enabled`, which whatever reads an override asks first). `--crash-after <frames>` is hidden from `--help`: it throws in the loop on purpose.
- **The log** is `Core/Diagnostics/Log.cs` (`LogBook` the writer, `Log` the game's own). Only `Program.Main` opens its file, so the harness's and the tests' runs write nothing and are the same with it as without; the last 200 lines are kept in memory all the same (`Log.Tail`). A line goes into a buffer and nowhere else: the disk is reached at an error, at `Log.Flush` (the end of the start-up, a crash, the way out) and once a second from a timer's thread, never from a frame. Its times are `WallClock`'s. `Log.Phase("maps")` in a `using` times a step: each step of `Initialize`, and the first arrival in each place from the moment the player is put there to the end of the next frame drawn (`GameEngine.TimeTheArrival`); `Log.Phases` is what the harness's `profile` prints after its start-up line. Nothing the log does may reach a frame, the dice or the clocks.
- **A crash** is caught by `Program.Guarded`, which runs the game with `AppDomain.UnhandledException` watched and a `try` round it; both hand the exception to `CrashReport.Caught` (`Core/Diagnostics/CrashReport.cs`), which writes beside the save: the report (`logs/crash-<yyyyMMdd-HHmmss>.txt`, the sections of `CrashReport.Sections`, from `GameEngine.DescribeForCrash` and the log's tail, scrubbed of anyone's folder by `Scrub`), then the game as it stood (`GameEngine.CrashSave`, written with `SaveManager.SaveGame` as `savegame.crash.json`, read back and kept only if it reads whole), then the note the next start takes up once (`logs/crash.pending`, `CrashReport.TakePending` in `Initialize`), which is the title's first notice (`TitleNotice.ForCrash`: "Your save is intact" only when the save read as `Ok`). **The player's save is never written by any of it**: a crash save is caught in the middle of whatever was happening, and nothing loads one by itself. The newest ten reports are kept, and one crash save with the one before it as its backup. A new section of the report is a name in `Sections` and a line of `Compose`; whatever the report should say of the game is a field of `GameFacts`, plain values, so the writer never needs a window.
- **Waiting behind another window** (the options' "Pause in the background", off unless chosen): `Program.Main` tells the engine every frame whether its window is in front (`GameEngine.WindowFocused`), and with the option on and the window not in front the whole of `Update` is held and the mixer glides to a quarter (`HeldForFocus`, `GameEngine.WaitsForFocus`, `AudioManager.Away`). **The engine never asks raylib about the focus itself**: what the system says of a hidden window is no answer (on Windows raylib calls one that never had the focus "in front"), and a harness run with the option on must go on whatever it says (the `look` mode shoots the row on; the `crash` mode tells the engine both ways and prints what moved). The frame limit (the options' "Frame limit", 60 unless chosen) is set by `WindowSettings.Apply`, which only the game's own window is given, never by `ApplySettings(window: false)`: the harness draws as fast as it can.
- **A new row of the options** goes at the end of the settings, before `OptionRow.Help` (it opens the help pages and a test holds it where it is); `OptionRow.ResetDefaults` (plan 12 · Q3, `GameSettings.ResetToDefaults`: every setting back as `new GameSettings()` has it, but the scenes the machine has seen, behind a question that opens on NO: `OptionsScreen.AskReset`, `AnswerReset`) is the very last row, after Help. The harness walks to a row by its name (`GoToRow` in `Modes/Look.cs`), never by counting from another: `look_12_help` had shown the options, not the help, from the day a row was put between.
- **The keys, the pad and what a prompt says** (plan 12 · Q4; GPU-free but `InputManager`'s two polling methods): `Core/InputBindings.cs` is the binding set as data (an action's keys and pad buttons; `For(ButtonMode)` the presets, `From(GameSettings)` a player's own `GameSettings.Bindings` laid over one, `Conflicts()` the check, `Resolve` a frame of `ActionFrame` from a keyboard and a `PadSnapshot`, `StickReader` the left stick's dead zone and eight ways). `InputManager` keeps the set in force (`Use(settings)`, called by `ApplySettings`), polls raylib once a frame in `ReadKeys` (the keyboard) and `ReadPad` (the pad at `PadIndex`, the options' Controller row), and remembers `LastDevice`; a tool or test that changes any of it calls `InputManager.Reset()` after, and runs in `Shared clocks and dice`. **Every key is an action**: F11 and Alt+Enter are `Fullscreen`, M is `Mute`, Enter is `Menu`; none is read by name, and a new one is a `GameAction` at the enum's end with its keys and buttons in every preset (`PadInputTests` hold that each is bound and that no two share an input, but for the pairs the game reads in different places, `InputBindings.MayShare`). **A prompt never writes a device's name**: `Text.Of` swaps a hint's key name (`shared.keys.z` and its kin, `Prompts.ForPad`) for the pad's button while `LastDevice` is the pad, and fills `{key:confirm}` and `{KEY:confirm}` in a line, so a new line that names a control uses the placeholder. Plan 12 · Q5 draws hints by action with pad icons and adds the Controls page.

Everything draws into the `virtualScreen` render texture, which is 3840×2160 and is letterboxed into the window. UI code lays out in 1920×1080 units (`VirtualWidth`, `VirtualHeight`); `Draw` wraps the 2D pass in a ×2 camera (`RenderScale`), so drawing code never multiplies by the scale itself. Sizes that must be one real pixel (anti-aliased edges) divide by `UiShapes.PixelScale`.

### Logic stays free of the GPU

Tests create `BattleEngine`, `Map`, `Pokemon` and the databases without a window, and drive battles through `SelectMainMenuOption`, `SelectMove`, `ConfirmMessage` and `Update(dt)`. Keep rules (battle flow, movement, map queries, texture baking into `PixelCanvas`) callable without raylib GPU or input calls; put drawing and key handling in separate methods.

The whole game runs without a window too (plan 16 · T12): `GameEngine.InitializeHeadless()` in place of `Initialize()` loads the data, the scripts, the options and the slots and nothing of the screen or the sound card, and `Update` plays the game fed its actions as `ActionFrame`s (`InputManager.Feed`); `Draw` refuses. `HeadlessGameTests` plays a new game, a wild battle, a Pokémon Center, a save and a continue that way. Whatever the field's update calls must stay callable there: a texture, a render target or a picture made in an update path (the region's picture was the one) asks `IsHeadless` first.

### Battles: the rules and their face

- **The rules** are `Battle/Sim/BattleCore` (plan 06 · R2 to R7; split over `.cs` for a battle's course and the turn's order, `.Choices.cs`, `.Moves.cs`, `.Effects.cs`, `.Families.cs`, `.Unique.cs`, `.Switch.cs`, `.Turn.cs`, `.Context.cs`) and nothing else is: no screen, sound, keys or clock. It is given the two sides, a generator and the rules (`CoreSetup`), says what it waits for (`Request`: an `ActionRequest` for the Pokémon nobody inside chooses for and that isn't in the middle of a move, a `ReplacementRequest` for a place whose Pokémon fainted or, in the middle of a turn, left by its own move) and is answered in plain data (`Submit` of `BattleChoice`s). Each answer is worked out to the next question **at once**, on the Pokémon it was handed, and written into a log (`BattleLog.cs`; `Log` is all of it, `TakeLog()` what is new). A battle's course is one iterator (`Course`) that reads from top to bottom.
- **The log** is how anything gets on screen. A line (`Said`) carries what is seen as it appears (`Shows`: a lunge, the move's effect, a stat change, a condition) and what lands a moment later (`OnImpact`: the hit's damage, its sound); events between lines are things nobody reads (a ball thrown, EXP, a level, the end). The core never calls `AudioManager` or `BattleAnimator`. Something new to show is a new event there and a case in `BattleEngine.Show`.
- **The face** is `BattleEngine` (`.cs` plays the log, `.Menus.cs` turns the menus into choices): what the game, the tests and the harness use, with the public members it always had. It hands the core **copies** of every Pokémon (`BattleMirror`), because the core is a whole turn ahead of the screen: the game's own Pokémon change as the log's events are shown (a hit's damage when the hit lands). So during playback `battle.PlayerPokemon` is behind `battle.Core`'s; whenever a menu is open they are the same (`Publish`), and whatever a test or the harness changed on the game's own Pokémon meanwhile is taken over before the rules are next asked (`Adopt`). A battler's own state (confusion, a Choice lock) is copied the same way (`Battler.CopyVolatileFrom`): a new field on `Battler` or `Pokemon` that a battle changes must be added to that method or to `Pokemon.CopyStateFrom`, or the screen and the rules drift apart (`BattleCoreTests.ACopyTakesOnEverythingThatCanChange` checks every public property of `Pokemon`). The screen's state is never copied over the rules' while lines are still being shown (`playingTheLog`): a tool that hands a choice over before the menu is open must not undo what the rules worked out.
- **An ability or an item named in a line** (plan 12 · Q11): a line that names a Pokémon's ability or held item asks the core for the name (`AbilityOf`, `ItemOf`; `ctx.AbilityOf` and `ctx.ItemOf` in the effects' tables), never writes it itself, as the original's message tags do: that attaches an `AbilityShown` or `ItemShown` to the line, which is all the trainer AI's memory (`AiMemory`) and the screen's information panel learn from (`BattleEngine.Revealed`, `Battle/BattleInfo.cs`, `BattleMenuState.Info`, opened from the commands by the `Item` action). A line built with a name and then not said forgets it (`Unsaid`), or the name goes with the next line. The panel never tells what no line has named, nor a count a hidden item could lengthen.
- **Trainers who speak** (plan 11 · C12; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "Trainers who speak in battle"): the core marks three moments of a trainer of the other side with a `TrainerReacts(Place, TrainerCue)` event (`BattleCore.Speaks.cs`): `LastPokemon` on the line that sends their last one out (never the opening's), `FirstDown` on the faint's line while others can still fight, and `LowHp` with the last line of the action (or the end of the turn) that left their last one in the red (`BattleCore.RedShare`, a fifth, which a test holds equal to `BattleAnimator.LowHpRatio`); each once per trainer, with no roll, never after `Ended`, whatever the options say. The words are `Data/trainer-lines.json` (`TrainerLines`, hand-written, keyed by `trainers.json` ids or a stem ending in `*`); `BattleEngine.Show` puts a line in the `interlude` queue only where `BattleSetup.TrainerLines` holds (the option `GameSettings.TrainerLines`, a `RulesDefault`), and the message box names the speaker on a pill (`CurrentSpeaker`). As the line appears the trainer steps back onto their platform with its `mood` and `act` (plan 11 · C13: `BattleAnimator.StepBack`, `Speaking`, a `SpeakingTrainer` timed by the animator's clock alone, drawn from the look kept as `EnemyTrainerLook` and `EnemyTrainer2Look` after the send-out cleared it; `BattleRenderer.SpeakerAt`; the camera's `ShotKind.Trainer`, `BattleCamera.SpeakerShot`), and off again once it is read (`SendOffSpeaker`, a `Happening` after the line); nothing with the Battle Scene off.
- **What lasts on screen** (plan 25 · Y1, Y2; style guide, "Weather in battle", "The Substitute", "What lies on the field"): the weather in the air, a Substitute's doll and what lies on the field are state of `BattleAnimator`, set by the log's events as their lines are read and **never read from `core.Field`**, which is a turn ahead; `SyncFromCore` only brings them level once a menu opens (`SettleWeather`, `SettleSubstitute`, `FieldLook.Settle`), with nothing played. The field's part is `Battle/FieldLook.cs` (`BattleAnimator.FieldLook`: a `ConditionView` of layers, ages and a seed of the look's own count for each `SideCondition` of each side, each `FieldCondition` and each `PlaceCondition` of each place; the enums and what the rules have of each, `SideState.Layers`, `FieldState.Has`, `PlaceState.Has`, are in `Battle/Sim/BattleField.cs`), and it changes by one method, `FieldLook.Tell(event)`, which `BattleEngine.Show` and the tests both use. **Every line that sets, wears off or clears a condition carries its event**: `SideConditionChanged(side, condition, layers)` (`BattleCore.Now(side, what)` makes it from the state just written), `FieldConditionChanged` and `PlaceConditionChanged`, on the moves' lines (`RaiseScreen`, `Scatter`, `TwistTheRoom`, `Intensify`, `MakeAWish`, `SendAhead`), Brick Break's, each of Defog's and Rapid Spin's, the turn's end, a Poison type taking the Toxic Spikes up, a Guard Spec., and a battle that opens under Trick Room; where the rules drop a Wish or an attack sent ahead with no line (nobody stands there) the event stands in the log by itself. A new write to `SideState`, `FieldState` or `PlaceState` (plan 06 · R19's terrains, Aurora Veil and hazards) needs its event on its line, its value at the end of its enum and its look in `Graphics/BattleFieldFx.cs`: `BattleFieldLookTests` follows the events alone through the two hundred golden battles and through battles of field moves answered turn by turn, and fails where the look and `core.Field` part.
- **The turn's moments** (plan 25 · Y3; style guide, "Marks on a Pokémon"): `BattleMoment` (`Battle/Sim/BattleLog.cs`) is the original's battle animations that are not moves (`generated/battle_sub_animations.txt`), but those other events draw (a stat's change, a heal, a condition given, the weathers, the doll's swaps) and plan 09's (shiny; a condition that lasts on the model): twenty-five, each named after its original, and the test's checklist accounts for all forty. `MomentShown(Place, moment, From)` rides on the line that tells of it, where the original plays it (`BattleCore.Moments.cs` has how): `Say(...).With(Moment(b, …))` on a line said in place (asleep, frozen, paralysed, confused or in love as it would have moved; the Great Marsh's bait and mud; a bag item on a Pokémon that stands on the field, the escape item, a level of one that does), and `LoseHp(Marked(b, …), …)` or `RestoreHp(Marked(b, …), …)` for a line said by those two (poison, a burn, a curse, a nightmare, Leech Seed with the seeder's place as `From`, Ingrain, a bind by its move, `BindMoment`): `Marked` rides as what a line names does, before the line's own `HpChanged`, and goes unsaid with it. **A held item going off is a moment too** (`HeldItem`), not plan 12 · Q11's `ItemShown`, which every line that only names an item carries (Frisk, Knock Off, Trick): a line of an item at work asks `ItemAtWork(holder)` (the core's and `IBattleContext`'s) where a line that names one asks `ItemOf`, an orb's condition is `TryInflictStatusByItem`, and a Quick Claw, which the original shows with no line, is the one moment that stands between lines. An attack sent ahead landing is `AheadLanded(move, type, category, From, To)` on the line that it was taken, added once its accuracy is rolled.
