None of it changes a line, a roll or anything a turn does: the golden battles moved by these events' lines alone. **The screen**: `BattleEngine.PlayMoment` is the one place a moment is played (`BattleAnimator.Moment`, a cue of `CueKind.Moment`, with `SoundBank.MomentSound`; an option that turns the battle's scene off skips it there), and `BattleAnimator.Shows` decides whether it is, as `BattleSystem_ShouldShowStatusEffect` does (none behind a doll or out of sight but the escape item's and a level's; Leech Seed's needs both its ends). **An `HpChanged` on a line that carries a moment lands quietly** `HitDelay` later (`OnAMomentsLine`): no `Anim.Hit`, no shake, no `hit_normal`, no heal's aura or sound, as the original skips the sprite's blink; an `HpChanged` with no moment (a sandstorm, recoil) is a hit as before. The moments count for themselves (`momentCount`), so a battle's moves keep the seeds, and the looks, they had.
- **A condition that lasts** (plan 09 · L2; style guide, "Marks on a Pokémon", "A condition that lasts"): what a Pokémon shows for as long as it has it (asleep, poisoned, badly poisoned, burned, paralysed, frozen; confused; in love) is state of `BattleAnimator` like the doll and the field's look: `Conditions`, a `ConditionLooks` (`Battle/ConditionLook.cs`) with a `ConditionLook` to each of the four places, which is also its view's `CombatantView.Condition` (the status shown and its mark's age, strength and seed, the status that is going while its mark fades, and a mark each for a confusion and a love). It changes by one method, `ConditionLooks.Tell(event)`, which `BattleEngine.Show` hands **every** event before its own case and which the tests use the same way: `Entered` clears the place, `CameInWith` and `StatusChanged` set its status, `VolatileChanged` its confusion and love, `Fainted` and `Left` clear it. It is **never read from `core`**, which is a turn ahead; `SyncFromCore` only settles it once a menu opens (`Settle` with `ConditionLooks.RulesHave(battler)`), with nothing played. **Two events are new**, at the end of `BattleLog.cs`, and `BattleCore.Conditions.cs` has how a line comes to carry them. `VolatileChanged(Place, Confused, InLove)` is the two as they stand from that line on: `Say(...).With(Passing(b))` once the change is made (whatever confuses, Attract, Cute Charm, a Destiny Knot, a confusion running out, the bag's items, a berry eaten out of turn), `ctx.PassingChanged(self)` for an item whose line is said through the context (a Persim or a Lum Berry, a Mental Herb), and what `LetGoOf` hands back for a love that ends because the one loved left: by itself in the log on a recall, on the line of its fall on a faint. `CameInWith(Place, Status)` rides on every send-out's line (`Bringing(line, place)`; by itself beside a wild Pokémon's `Entered`), with a `VolatileChanged` for a confusion Baton Pass handed on: an `Entered` carries the rules' own Pokémon, which may have woken or been cured by the time its line is read, so the line itself says what the newcomer brings. **The screen's own Pokémon takes that status as its `Entered` is shown**, none where the line brings none (`BattleEngine.Show` reads it off the line being shown, and the `CameInWith` that stands beside a wild Pokémon's entry sets it by itself; the rules are never asked): a cure on the bench has no event (Heal Bell, Aromatherapy, Natural Cure on the way back, medicine out of the bag), and the screen's copy is the rules' only once a menu opens, so the HP box's pill (`ModernUi.StatusPill`, drawn from `CombatantView.Shown`) and the send-out's cry (`Cries.SendOutMode`) would otherwise tell of a condition that is gone for the rest of the turn. The six conditions needed nothing: every line that gives or cures one carried its `StatusChanged` already. None of it changes a line, a roll or anything a turn does: the golden battles moved by `VolatileChanged` lines alone. **A new write to `Battler.ConfusionTurns` or `Volatiles.InLoveWith`, a new send-out, or a new condition that should show while it lasts** (plan 06 · R19's: a field at the event's end, a mark in `ConditionLook`, its look in `MoveFx.Conditions.cs`, its row in the style guide) needs its event on its line: `BattleConditionLookTests` follows the events alone through the two hundred golden battles and through sixty battles of status moves answered turn by turn, holds the looks to the rules after every answer, and holds every moment the turn plays (`Asleep`, `Poisoned`, `Confused`…) to what is shown at its line, which is what finds a line that was forgotten. **The body's own** part is the pose's and the shader's: `PokePose.Asleep` (eyes shut) and `Frozen` (the idle loop held at one moment, `PokemonAnimation.IdleTime`, an imported model's own clip too; eyes held open), and `MoveFx.Cast`, a colour `BattleRenderer` blends into the model with `SetFlash` wherever no send-out's, recall's or silhouette's flash plays. **The order of `PokePose`'s fields and of what `EyesOf` shows first is the style guide's** ("Pokémon (version 2, G7)", "The pose's fields and the eyes' order"): plan 25 · Y5 and plan 10 · F2, F6 and F10 put theirs in the places it names, and a session that adds a field or a case changes that list first.
- **Bodies that answer** (plan 25 · Y5; style guide, "Bodies that answer"; our own, Platinum draws none of it): `PokePose.Dodge`, `Tired`, `Cheer`, `Charge` and `Stat` and their clips in `PokemonAnimation` for every body plan (`ClipRole.Dodge`, `Tired`, `Cheer` for an imported model, whose missing ones play the body plan's), set from the view's ages (`CombatantView.DodgeAge`, `CheerAge`, `ChargeAge`, `StatAge`, `Tired`), which the log's lines set and never the rules: a `MoveShown` that missed starts a dodge on its target (`BattleAnimator.Dodge`; the step aside is the renderer's, `PokemonAnimation.DodgeAway` and `DodgeAside`, away from the side `FxCtx.Aim` sends a miss), `Won` and `Caught` cheer the player's side and the first line after the player's last fall in a batch that ends in `Ended(PlayerDefeat)` the foe's (`BattleEngine.Enqueue`), a stat's change is in the eyes for 0.6 s, and tiredness eases in and out with the HP **bar** in the red (`BattleAnimator.InTheRed`, `DisplayedHp`), never the rules' HP. **The rules' one event** is `Charging(Place, Move)` on a charge turn's line (`BeginCharge`), which the face turns into the crouch (in place of the line's hop) and the move's charge half, `EffectCue.Charging` (`MoveFx.ChargeHalf`, seeded by its own count so no move's seed moves), held until the next lunge from that place or a menu whose rules say the charge is over (`SettleCharge`); a charge that goes up or under on its line gathers nothing. With the scene off there is no dodge, cheer or charge; tiredness stays. `BattleBodiesTests` holds all of it.
- **Who chooses**: an `IBattleController` chooses for a side from inside the core, with the battle's own numbers (the opponents' and a partner's is `TrainerAi`; `BattleCore.ControllerOf` says whose). The player's choices come from outside. A seed and the answers given are therefore the whole battle: `BattleCore.Record` is that pair (`BattleRecord`), `Replay` plays one again to the same log.
- **The Vs. Recorder's file** (plan 08 · P10; `docs/data-files.md`, "Recorded battles"): `Battle/Sim/BattleRecordFile.cs` (GPU-free) is a battle as plain data, its sides as they began (taken from the `CoreSetup` in `BattleEngine`'s constructor, `BattleEngine.Opening`, before the core changes anything), its rules preset, conditions, the player's name, the day, the record and `GameDataFiles.Version` (a hash of the five data files of rules); `BattleEngine.RecordFile(day)` closes it, `Replay()` fights it again with no screen to the same log, and `Read` refuses a file of other data or a newer shape (`ReplayRefusal`). `Core/GameEngine.Recorder.cs` asks "Record this battle?" once the field is back after a trainer's battle with the Vs. Recorder in the bag and the options' `RecordBattles` row on, (a Frontier battle's too, since P11) and keeps the Elite Four's and the Champion's without a word; the files go to `SavePaths.Replays`. **Watching one** (plan 08 · P11): USE on the Vs. Recorder opens `ReplayListScreen` (`GameState.ReplayList`, `Core/GameEngine.Replays.cs`), and a recording chosen is `BattleEngine.ForReplay(file)`, a battle on the file's own Pokémon, bag and Pokédex whose answers the record feeds (`BattleEngine.Watching.cs`: `Watch`, `FeedTheRecord`; Menu leaves, Run held is ×3, `WatchBroke` where the record no longer fits); a battle watched never reaches `EndBattle` or `KeepTheRecord`, so it changes nothing of the game. **A new field of `CoreSetup` or `BattleConditions` that changes how a battle goes is a field of the file too** (`Opening` and `ToSetup`), or a recorded battle plays otherwise; and a tool that changes a Pokémon in the middle of a battle makes a record that doesn't replay.
- **The trainer AI** (plan 06 · R9) is Platinum's, ported routine by routine from the decompilation's `src/battle/trainer_ai/script.s`: `Battle/Sim/Ai/AiThinking.cs` holds the script's commands as methods (scores, rolls, who is who, what the AI knows, its damage and 40ths type chart), and `AiThinking.Basic.cs`, `.Expert.cs`, `.Expert2.cs`, `.Tag.cs` and `.Flags.cs` the routines in the script's own order, quirks and bugs kept (`docs/mechanics/rulings.md` lists them). A routine asks a move by its battle effect (`MoveData.EffectId`, `MoveEffectId`, `battleEffect` in `moves.json`), never by its `effect`. `AiMemory` is what the AI has seen. `TrainerAi` is `TrainerAI_PickCommand`: a switch, an item, a move in a single or double battle, who comes in after a faint; a wild Pokémon draws a move at random. Every roll it makes is `RollKind.AiChoice` on the battle's generator. A trainer thinks with its `Trainer.Ai` flags and uses its `Trainer.Items`, both from `trainers.json` (`TrainerDatabase`).
- **Battles of their own** (`BattleKind`, `BattleCore.Special.cs`): a roamer (runs unless held), the catching lesson (the assistant's battle, which plays itself: one move, then a ball that can't fail; no misses, no critical hits; nothing reaches the player's Pokédex or team), the Great Marsh (`Safari`: no Pokémon of the player's, `ChoiceKind.Bait` and `Mud`, `SafariCatchStage` and `SafariEscapeCount`, the Pokémon's flight after each throw) and Pal Park; beside them `CoreSetup.CannotFlee`, `FirstBattle` (no critical hits) and `Partner` (a trainer filling the player's second place with a team and a mind of its own; the player loses with their own team).
- **Formats of the later games** (plan 14; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, under the same name; `BattleCore.Formats.cs`): extras of ours that a battle is handed as it is made and that nothing changes while it runs. **An inverse battle** (B1) is `BattleConditions.Inverse`, copied to `FieldState.Inverse` in the core's constructor: the type chart is turned round in one place, `TypeChart.GetEffectiveness(attack, d1, d2, rules, inverse)` (each type against each before the two multiply: twice is half, half and nothing are twice), and **whoever asks how a type does against another in a battle hands it the battle's flag**: `DamageCalculator.Matchups` reads the target's field (so the damage, the lines, the check before a move, the berries, Anticipation, Wonder Guard and the move cards' hints follow by themselves), and Stealth Rock, Conversion 2 and the trainer AI's three sums pass `Field.Inverse`. A new caller that leaves it out is wrong only in an inverse battle; `InverseBattleTests` has a test to each kind of caller. An ability's immunity and Magnet Rise are not the chart and stay (Showdown's Inverse Mod, the source for the rule). The flag reaches a battle through its conditions alone: a script's `battle … inverse` hands the host a `BattleTerms` (`Battle/BattleSetup.cs`; later formats' flags join it), which `GameEngine.BattleConditionsHere(terms)` lays over the place's own; a `BattleRecord` keeps it, and `Replay` refuses a battle under the other chart. The battle says `BattleCore.InverseLine` once everyone is out. The one inverse battle in the game is the Fight Area's scientist's (`fight_area.InverseBattle`, an overlay `npcs` person shown once the tag battle's crowd has gone, once a day by a `FLAG_DAILY_…` flag). **Someone a script battles `as` a team of the table takes that team's name and class in the battle and beats its id**: a person of our own has a team of ours in `Data/our-trainers.json` (`TrainerDatabase.Ours`, plan 14 · B14; the scientist is `scientist_ingram`).
- **Master Trainers** (plan 14 · B14; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "Master Trainers"): a master for every species is a record of `Data/master-trainers.json` (`master_<species>`, `TrainerDatabase.MasterId`; one Pokémon at 75, generated by `tools/DataImporter`'s `MasterTrainers.cs` from Pokémon Showdown's random-battle sets, never edited by hand), which `TrainerDatabase.Get` finds and `Masters` lists while **`TrainerDatabase.All` stays Platinum's table alone**, so the golden teams and battles read it as before; trainers of our own people are `Data/our-trainers.json` (`Ours`), found the same way. A script chooses the Pokémon with `choosepokemon "Bidoof"` (the party screen greys the rest, `PartyScreen.OpenToChoose(allowed, refusal)`; a choice the species refuses is `RESULT` 255) and fights with `master self [canlose]`, which hands the host a `BattleTerms` with `NoBattleItems` and `Alone`, the chosen member itself: `GameEngine.StartTrainerBattle` makes the player's side a `Party` of that one, so what the battle does to it stays. **`CoreSetup.NoBattleItems` is the one rule of a closed bag** (plan 13 · V5's run rule sets the same flag): `WhyNotItem` refuses every item to both sides (`BattleCore.NoItemsRefusal`), `TrainerAi.ShouldUseItem` reaches for none, and `BattleEngine.BagClosed` greys BAG (`ModernUi.Closed`). What a Pokémon may know is `Models/Legality.cs` (GPU-free: its line's level-up, Egg, tutor and machine moves, Draco Meteor and a starter's strongest move), which the importer and the tests share; a master beaten is `master_<species>` in `StoryState`'s beaten trainers, which is all a title is (B15).
- **The later mechanics** (plan 06 · R20 first; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "Mega Evolution and Primal Reversion" and "The later mechanics in the story"): each is handed over in its own region, so nothing of Sinnoh's has one. Mega Evolution is `Battle/Sim/BattleCore.Mega.cs` behind `Ruleset.MegaEvolution`: a flag on the move's choice (`BattleChoice.Mega`) refused by `WhyNotMega` (a Key Stone: `CoreSetup.KeyStone`, which the face sets from the bag, or `Trainer.KeyStone`; the Pokémon's own stone or Rayquaza's Dragon Ascent; once a battle for each trainer), carried out after the turn's switches and before its moves with the order sorted again, the form taken by `Pokemon.ChangeForm` and given back in `End`; Primal Reversion as the orb's holder comes in; `MegaEvolved` on the line; `TrainerAi.WithTheLaterMechanics` uses it whenever it can, drawing nothing; `ClingsTo` keeps a stone or an orb in its own species' hands. Plan 06's R20 entry has the pattern R21 to R23 follow (a choice's flag, a `WhyNot`, one a trainer, an event of its own, the AI's whenever-it-can, all behind the `Ruleset`). Z-Moves and Ultra Burst (R21; the rulings' "Z-Moves and Ultra Burst") are `Battle/Sim/BattleCore.ZMoves.cs` behind `Ruleset.ZMoves`: `BattleChoice.ZMove` and `UltraBurst`, `WhyNotZMove` and `WhyNotUltraBurst` (a Z-Ring: `CoreSetup.ZRing` or `Trainer.ZRing`); the move chosen pays its PP and becomes its Z-Move in `ExecuteMove` (`BecomeZMove`: a type's Z-Move of the move's `zPower`, category and priority, a status move's Z-Power version with its `zBonus` first), a damaging one through a protection at a quarter (`ThroughProtection` in `StrikeOnce`); a signature Z-Move's effect is its data's (`ZEffects`, or a later move's effect by name); the Ultra Burst rides with the turn's Mega Evolutions; `ZPowerUsed` and `UltraBursted` on their lines; the move menu's item button arms a Mega Evolution where one can be had and the Z-Power otherwise (`ToggleLaterMechanic`).
- **Arithmetic** is `Battle/Sim/Formulas.cs`: Platinum's own, in whole numbers rounded down after every step, each function named for the original's (`FormulaTests` holds them against numbers worked out by hand). `DamageCalculator` and `CatchCalculator` put them in the original's order. A bonus is applied with `Formulas.Scale` in its own place, never multiplied together with others as a fraction. **Under the modern rules the damage is generation 5's** (plan 06 · R19c, `Ruleset.ModernDamageFormula`, `DamageCalculator.Modern.cs`): the same bonuses gathered into chains of 4096ths (`Formulas.Chain`, rounded half down by `RoundHalfDown`) for the power, the attacking stat, the defending stat and the final modifiers, the weather and a spread hit after the + 2, a burn after the type chart, the screens last (`ModernFinalDamage`); a new bonus in the formula is a line in both paths, each in its own place, and the oracle (`OracleTests`) says where Showdown has it.
- **The field and passing conditions** (plan 06 · R3): `FieldState` (`Battle/Sim/BattleField.cs`) is the weather, Trick Room, Gravity, a `SideState` for each side (screens, veils, what lies in wait) and a `PlaceState` for each place (a Wish, a Future Sight on its way). Whatever lasts while a Pokémon stays in is `Battler.Volatile` (`Volatiles`), and what holds for one turn `Battler.Turn`. They hold plain values only (a `Place`, a `MoveData`, a count), never a `Battler` or a `Move`, because the screen's battler keeps a copy (`CopyVolatileFrom`). A counter counts as the original's does and its comment says how (some end at nothing, Encore and Disable a turn end later). `Battler.Ability` is the ability in force (none under Gastro Acid): rules read that, never `Pokemon.Ability`, and `BattleEffects.Of` leaves the item out under an Embargo.
- **The field of the later games** (plan 06 · R19b; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "The field of the later games" and "Shapes that change in battle"; Showdown's rules): values at the ends of `BattleWeather` (`Snow`, `HarshSun`, `HeavyRain`, `StrongWinds`), `SideCondition` (`AuroraVeil`, `StickyWeb`) and `FieldCondition` (the four terrains, one at a time: `FieldState.Terrain`, `TerrainTurns`, `IsTerrain`). **`FieldState.WeatherInEffect` is the weather as the rules read it**: the strong sun and rain as the sun and the rain, snow as hail, the strong winds as none (`AsTheRulesRead`), so every rule that asks after Platinum's weathers follows them; what is the later weather's own asks `SkyInEffect` (hail's wear, snow's Defense, the winds' Flying matchups in `DamageCalculator.Matchups`, a Water or Fire move put out, `WashedOut`). Under the modern rules hail is snow (`Ruleset.SnowInPlaceOfHail`, `Ruleset.Sky`; `SetWeather` makes it so, with snow's line). The moves and abilities that set these are R24 to R27's: they call the core's entry points (`SetStrongWeather`, `SetTerrain`, both on `IBattleContext`; `RaiseAuroraVeil`, `LayStickyWeb`), each of which says its line with its event, and `BattleEffect.OnTerrainChange` is Mimicry's hook. The terrains' rules are in `BattleCore.Terrains.cs` and a line each where they bite (the power in `DamageCalculator`, `TerrainKeepsOff` in `TryInflictStatus`, Misty Terrain in `Confuse`, Psychic Terrain in `Reaches`, Grassy Terrain's heal at the turn's end, the terrains' rows of Nature Power, Camouflage and Secret Power in `Ground`); who stands on a terrain is `DamageCalculator.OnTheTerrain` (`IsGrounded`, which Spikes ask too). **Shapes that change in battle** are `BattleCore.Shapes.cs`, by the ability's name (Stance Change, Zen Mode, Schooling, Shields Down, Disguise, Gulp Missile, Ice Face, Hunger Switch, Zero to Hero) and Relic Song's: a passing shape goes through `Volatiles.Original` (`Become`) and is given back by `Revert`; a lasting one (Mimikyu busted, Eiscue's face broken, Palafin the hero) is kept in `lastingShapes` and given back as the battle ends. Each says its line with `Reshaped`. None of this reaches a battle of Platinum's: `battles-platinum.txt` held still.
- **A move's own effect** is an entry of the table in `BattleCore.Effects.cs` (the families whose rule is a formula on the battle's numbers in `BattleCore.Families.cs`, plan 06 · R4), under the `effect` name `moves.json` gives the move, with only the parts it has (`Does` for a move that is more than a hit, `Charge`, `Start`, `Power`, `Follows`, `Reach`, `Hit`, `Then`, `Miss`; a power of the move's own `BasePower`, a fixed amount `Deals`, several `Strikes`, a hit the move decides itself `Decides`, `NoVariance`, `SelfKo`, `LeavesOneHp`). A move with no entry hits, or does what its data fields say (`ByItsFields`). After writing one, run the importer: it asks `BattleCore.HasMoveEffect` and marks the move fully run. A move runs in the original's order (the header of `BattleCore.Moves.cs`) and so does the end of a turn (`BattleCore.Turn.cs`): a new step goes where the original has it, with the function or script it was read from named beside it. `Strike` plays a move's strikes one after another on each target (`StrikeOnce`), and what a target took this turn is `Battler.Turn.Took` (the counters, Revenge, Assurance and Focus Punch read it). A move's course from "X used Y!" to what follows its hits is `Perform`; a move called by another (`CallMove`: Metronome, Sleep Talk, Me First…), bounced by Magic Coat or snatched (`UseAs`) goes through it too, and the moves of their own and the battlefield's are in `BattleCore.Unique.cs` (plan 06 · R5, R6). All 467 of Platinum's moves run in full since R6. The later generations' moves are written a batch to a file (`BattleCore.Effects.Later1.cs`, plan 06 · R24, and `Later2`, R25; R26 adds `Later3`, each a dictionary the static constructor in `BattleCore.Families.cs` merges), by Showdown's `data/moves.ts`; a move that hits with other numbers (Foul Play, Psyshock, Chip Away, Photon Geyser, a sure critical hit) has an empty entry and its rule in `DamageCalculator`, both paths, asked by the move's effect. The importer marks a later move fully run once its effect has an entry, whatever PokeAPI's meta fields could say of it.
- **The ground** a battle is fought on is `BattleConditions.Terrain` (`BattleTerrain`: the original's thirteen, "special" for the League's rooms, the Distortion World and the Battle Frontier). `GameEngine.TerrainAt` picks it as the original does: the tile under the player first, then the area's battle background (`MapArea.BattleBackground`, from the world files' headers), then the map's stage. Camouflage, Nature Power, Secret Power and the Dive and Dusk Balls read it.
- **What a move changes for the battle alone** (Transform's shape, stats, ability and moves; Mimic's move; a copied or swapped ability; Power Trick's swap) is kept the first time in `Volatiles.Original` (`Keep`) and put back by `Revert` (`BattleCore.Switch.cs`) when the Pokémon leaves the field or the battle ends, as the original rebuilds a Pokémon's battle data from its party data; a knocked-off item is `knockedOff` until the end. A new in-battle change to a Pokémon's own fields must go through `Keep`, or it outlasts the battle through `CopyStateFrom`. A Pokémon's types in battle are `Battler.Types` and `HasType` (Conversion, Transform), never its species'; the damage formula reads them.
- **A status move is told by its category** (`MoveCategory.Status`), never by a power of 0: the original's table gives every move of variable power (Counter, Seismic Toss, Return…) the power 1, which the importer writes as 0, so Taunt and Sucker Punch ask the category, and `DamageCalculator` takes a power worked out as 0 back to 1, as the original does. `BattleCore.PayDayMoney` is what Pay Day scattered, added to the player's money with the winnings.
- **Abilities** (plan 06 · R7: all 123 of Platinum's; R27: 167 of the later 190, listed in `Data/AbilityEffectTable.Later.cs` with their classes in `Battle/Effects/LaterAbilityEffects.cs`, their bonuses in the modern formula's chains in `Battle/DamageCalculator.Abilities.cs` and what the core reads by name in `Battle/Sim/BattleCore.Abilities.cs`; rulings.md, "The later abilities"): an ability's code is its entry in `Data/AbilityEffectTable.cs` (classes in `Battle/Effects/AbilityEffects.cs`), with three exceptions that go by the ability's **name** where the original reads it. A bonus inside the damage formula is a line of `DamageCalculator.Calculate`, in the original's own place and order (`BattleSystem_CalcMoveDamage`), never a hook: a new one goes where the original has it. What works only in the field (Pickup, Stench, Illuminate, Honey Gather, and the field side of many others) is in `Overworld/WildEncounterRules.cs` and `BattleCore.PickUp`. And the turn's own (Truant's `Loafs`, Stall, Slow Start, Unburden, Quick Feet in `EffectiveSpeed`, Normalize's `Normalized`, Lightning Rod's `Drawn`) is in the core beside the rule it changes. A held item's effect is asked for through `BattleEffects.ItemOf` (and the item itself through `ItemInHand`), which is where an Embargo and Klutz take it away.
- **What acts as a Pokémon comes in** is `BattleCore.SwitchInChecks` (`BattleCore.Switch.cs`): the original's phases in its order (the place's weather, Trace, the weather abilities, Intimidate, Download, Anticipation, Forewarn, Frisk, Slow Start, Mold Breaker, Pressure, the shapes, the held item), within a phase by Speed, each once per stay on the field (`Volatiles.Announced`, an `EntryCheck` flag each). It runs as Pokémon come in, after every action and as every turn begins, so an ability gained in the middle of things acts then (a Transform clears the flags). An ability that acts on entry needs its phase in `EntryPhases`; `BattleEffect.OnEntry` alone would run it last. `CheckShapes` gives Castform, Cherrim and Arceus their shapes (`Shapes.Take` keeps the one to give back); Arceus's type is read from its plate (`Battler.Types`).
- **Held items** (plan 06 · R8: all 158 of Platinum's): an item's code is found by its **hold effect** (`ItemData.HoldEffect`, the decompilation's name, with `HoldParam` its number), never by the item's name, so every item sharing an effect works and a new item of a known effect needs no code: `Battle/Effects/HeldItemEffects.cs` is the table of hold effects, with the hooks items answer (`AtEndOfTurn`, `AfterTheTurn`, `OnConditionChanged` for the berries and herbs, `AfterAttacking`, `EnduresHit`, the flags). A bonus inside the damage formula is a line of `DamageCalculator.Calculate` by hold effect in the original's block (`BattleEffects.HoldEffectOf`, `HoldParamOf`: the original's `Battler_HeldItemEffect`, nothing under an Embargo or with Klutz), and so are Speed's items (`EffectiveSpeed`; the halving ones are read from the item itself), accuracy's (`Hits`) and the critical-hit items (`ItemCritStages`). What the core does with items is `BattleCore.Items.cs`: the turn's order (Quick Claw, Custap Berry, Lagging Tail), the Metronome's count, the berries against a type as a hit lands, the items that answer a hit, Destiny Knot, the Amulet Coin's `PrizeMoney`, and the bag's items used in battle (`ExecuteItemUse` by the item table's use parameters, `WhyNotItem`, `CanUseInBattle`; `BattleChoice.UseItem` names the party member and the move an item is for). An item that went off this turn (`Volatiles.ItemWentOff`) is kept from an Embargo, Knock Off and Trick until its holder's move begins.
- **What may be chosen** is one place: `BattleCore.WhyNot(choice)` gives the reason a choice can't be made, `Submit` throws on one, and the menus ask it before handing a choice over (`BattleEngine.Menus.cs`). Whoever chooses from inside (`TrainerAi`) picks from `UsableMoves`.
- **After the battle** (plan 06 · R10): EXP and effort are given in `BattleCore.AwardExp` (effort first, `Models/Effort.cs`, so the levels it brings count it); a move that doesn't fit is a `MoveWanted` event and the face asks about it (`BattleEngine.Learning.cs`, `BattleMenuState.LearnMove`; `ConfirmMessage` answers so as to keep every move, and test helpers that read a battle on must treat `LearnMove` as a message). A traded Pokémon (`Pokemon.OriginalTrainer` not the player's `TrainerMark`, which `BattleConditions.Player` carries with `Badges`) obeys by `Models/Obedience.cs`. A loss's money is `BattleCore.MoneyLost`; the engine takes it and wakes the player in the last Pokémon Center (`GameEngine.WhiteOut`). Pokérus (`Models/Pokerus.cs`) and Giratina's and Arceus's forms are the engine's after every battle (`AfterBattle`, on the field's chance), Burmy's cloak the core's (`End`); `Models/FormRules.cs` holds every form rule outside battle.
- **A level on screen** (plan 08 · P5; style guide, "Battle panels", "A level"; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "The level-up panel"): the log's `LevelRose` carries the Pokémon's six stats before the EXP and at the new level (`Before` and `After`, `StatSpread`s made by `Pokemon.Stats()`), read in `BattleCore.Give` where the original reads them: the foe's effort first, then the old stats, then the level. `BattleEngine.Show` puts the level-up panel up from them (`BattleHUD.LevelUp`, a `UI/Kit/LevelUpPanel`: the two spreads, the page on show and the press it waits for, GPU-free; painted by `ModernUi.LevelUp`) and brings the screen's Pokémon to the same numbers (`Pokemon.TakeStats`), because the screen's copy levelled as its EXP was shown, without the foe's effort. The panel's numbers are the event's and are never read off a Pokémon as it is drawn: the rules' copy is a turn ahead and the screen's behind, and a later change must keep it so. The panel stands over the level's line, which is the log's next line: `ConfirmMessage` turns it from the gains to the new values and, pressed again, takes it down with the line, so whatever reads a battle on gets past a level like a message, in two confirms with the level's line under both (the tests' `Scenario.Settle` lists that line once; a helper that notes the message at every confirm notes it twice), and the move the level brings (`MoveWanted`) comes after. One `LevelRose` is every level a gain reached, so one panel spans them. The bag shows the same panel for a Rare Candy (`BagScreen.LevelUp`, `ConfirmLevelUp`, `ModernUi.LevelUpOverParty`): while it is up nothing else is used, moved or cancelled, and the level's moves and its evolution (`Teaching`, `TakeEvolution`) come only after its two presses, so a test or a tool that uses a Rare Candy through the bag presses twice before it asks for either.
- **Platinum's battle options** (plan 08 · P1; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "Battle Scene and Battle Style"; style guide, "Move effects (G8)" and "Battle panels"): the options' Battle Style (`GameSettings.BattleStyle`, Shift unless chosen) reaches the rules as `CoreSetup.OffersSwitch`, which `GameEngine.StartTrainerBattle` alone sets: in a single battle against a trainer, for a player answered from outside, never at the Frontier, `BattleCore.OfferSwitch` (called by `ReplaceFainted` once the trainer's next Pokémon is picked, before it comes out) says its line and yields a `SwitchOfferRequest`, answered by `BattleChoice.Stay` or `Switch` and kept in the record (`BattleRecord.OffersSwitch`). **On screen it is a question** (`LearnStep.Shift`, `BattleEngine.IsOfferingSwitch`, `OfferedFoe`): Yes opens the switching cards, whose cancel stays in (`StayIn`), and No, B or `ConfirmMessage` stays in, so whatever reads a battle on as messages gets past it; the harness's `Confirm` answers it as it comes (`Harness.AnswersOffers`). A battle a tool or a test makes for itself offers nothing unless its `BattleSetup` asks. The Battle Scene (`GameSettings.BattleScene`, `BattleSetup.Scene`, `BattleEngine.Scene`) is the face's alone: off, `Show` plays no lunge, no move's cue but Transform's, no stat's, heal's or condition's cue, no moment and no weather cue, nor their sounds, and what a line carries lands at once (`ImpactDelay`); something new that `Show` plays as one of the original's battle animations is skipped there too.
- **Nothing is written after the end**: `Ended` is the log's last event (the random-battle tests hold it), so what a move tidies up afterwards must not say or emit anything once `Result` is set.
- **Testing a rule of the turn**: `BattleFieldTests`, `BattleFamilyTests`, `BattleUniqueTests` and `BattleAbilityTests` drive the bare core with both sides answered for from outside (`CoreScenario.cs`: `Wild`, `Against`, `Doubles`, `Turn`, `DoubleTurn`, `SwitchTo`, `SendIn`, `Calm()` for a battle with nothing left to chance), so a test says exactly what each Pokémon does. The faster Pokémon moves first, a move can knock its target out before the rule under test is reached, and a move's side effect is a roll (`Calm()` fixes it): each has made a scenario test something other than what it meant to test.

### Words on screen

Plan 17 · N1 and N2 (plan 17 lists their parts). **What a screen or a battle on the table says is a key of `Data/text/en/ui.json`**, asked for with `Text.Of(key, ("name", value)...)` (`Core/Text.cs`, GPU-free; `docs/data-files.md`, "Text"), never a quoted literal: `TextTests` holds every key asked for in English's table, every entry asked for somewhere, and the files on the table free of words in quotes: `TextTests.OnTheTable` is every file of `UI/` and `Core/` but those `NotOnTheTable` names with why (what speaks only to a terminal or a report), with the rules' files outside them that hand a screen its words (`AlsoOnTheTable`, the battle's panels and menus among them), so a new file there is on the table from its first line. **The battle's log is on the table too** (plan 17 · N2: the sections `battle`, `battleInfo`, `battleMenu`, `evolution`, `intro`, `classes`): every `Say`, `Announce` and reason is `Text.Of`, and `TextTests.TheBattleSaysNothingInQuotesOfItsOwn` fails on a literal with a word in lower case anywhere in `Battle/` but the trainer AI (a name of the data, `Griseous Orb`, has none); a line kept in a table built once (a charge turn's, an ability's entry line) is its key, said through `Text.Of` where it is said; a trainer's class is `Trainer.ClassName` (`classes.<the English class's letters>`); the English must stay what it was to the character, which the golden battles hold, and every `battle.*` entry must fit the message box's two lines (`EveryEnglishBattleLineFitsTheMessageBoxsTwoLines`). A line that writes to the log or throws is no screen's, and a name of the code's or the data's kept in quotes in a file on the table is a `NotWords` entry with its reason. A key is a literal whose first segment is a section of the table, so a new section's name must be no prefix of other dotted names the engine writes (the scripts' `common.Nurse` is why the shared words are `shared`). The language is the options' first row (`GameSettings.Language`, `OptionRow.Language`, set by `ApplySettings` through `Text.Use`; a language of `languages.json` is offered only once it has its own `ui.json`, `Language.HasTable`, so French is listed and not offered) and the whole process's, like `Ruleset`: a test that changes it runs in the collection "Shared clocks and dice" and puts English back. `SHOTS_LANGUAGE=xx` runs the harness in the pseudo-language, which brackets every string the table gives. The name of a type, a nature, a status, a move's category, a stat, a Badge, a day or a month on screen is `Core/Words.cs`'s, never an enum's `ToString()`; a key made from a value interpolates a plain variable (`$"party.tag.place.{nth}"`, never an expression with brackets or commas, which the test doesn't take for a key), and a word chosen in code to make one (`"half"` in `help.matchup.{matchup}`) is all lower case, or the test takes it for a word on screen; and a list of words is a property, never a `static readonly` array, which would keep the language it was first read in.

**Names in every language** (plan 17 · N3). `Name` on a species, move, item or ability is the identifier the data, the save, the scripts and the rules go by and is **never drawn**: what is shown is `DisplayName` (`DisplayDescription`, `DisplayKind` for a species' "Seed Pokémon"), which reads `Data/LocalNames.cs` (`Data/text/<tag>/names.json`, written by `tools/DataImporter/NameFiles.cs` from PokeAPI's names tables for French, German, Spanish, Italian, Japanese, Korean and both Chinese; never edited by hand; a name the file lacks is English, and Romanian has no file because PokeAPI has none). A line or label that names one of them from a string (a script's `{item}`, a Journal event's subject, a saved record's species) asks `LocalNames.Species`, `Move`, `Item` or `Ability`. A Pokémon with no nickname has `Nickname` empty and `DisplayName` is its species' display name (a nickname equal to the species' English name is none; `HallOfFameMember.ShownName` is the same for the records). In the battle's core `AbilityOf`, `ItemOf` and `ItemAtWork` return display names for the line, while `AbilityShown` and `ItemShown` carry the identifiers. The Pokédex entry nobody wrote by hand is `Models/DexText.cs` from `dexText` in `ui.json`, and the importer writes `species.json`'s `dexEntry` with it, so the English composition is that field to the character (`LocalNamesTests`). The search's name groups and A-to-Z order follow the language (`nameGroups` in `languages.json`, `PokedexSearch.GroupLetter`, `Collation`). To find the sites that show a name after a change, mark the `Name` properties `[Obsolete]` for one build and read the warnings.

**Scripts, overlays and rooms in other languages** (plan 17 · N4). They keep their English; a language's sibling files (`Data/text/<tag>/scripts/<file>.json`, `world/<key>.json`, `maps/<Name>.json`: `docs/data-files.md`, "Translating the scripts, the overlays and the rooms") hold the same lines in order, each entry `{ "en": [...], "text": [...] }`, and a line is translated as it is shown: `ScriptParser` numbers the speaking instructions of a script (`Instruction.TextIndex`: `say`, `text`, `ask`, `choose`, a named `speaker`), the runner asks `Text.Line(script, index, k, english)` before it fills placeholders, a person's name and lines are found by `NPC.LinesPlace` and `LinesKey` (`Text.PersonName`, `PersonLine`), a sign's by `Map.SignKeys` (`Text.SignLine`), a room's name by `LocalNames.PlaceShown`. An entry whose `en` is not the game's English is stale and the English shows; the pseudo-language brackets every line. **A rewritten or inserted line makes translations stale**: `dotnet run --project tools/TextTool -- report` says which, `extract <tag> --update` brings the files up to date, and `docs/localisation.md` (held true by `ScriptTranslationTests`; `GOLDEN=accept` or `report` rewrites it) has the counts, so a session that adds scripts or people refreshes it. **Anything the save keeps is English**: `Map.DisplayNameAt` and the runner's `speaker` are identifiers; `Map.ShownNameAt` and `speakerShown` are what is drawn. A line the runner says for itself is `ui.json`'s (`scriptLines`, `found`), never a literal. The lines' address for plan 26's voices is `Text.ScriptLineKey` and `PersonLineKey`.

### Rules, chance and what is covered

- **Whose rules** (`Data/Ruleset.cs`; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md` has the table; plan 06 · R19): **every game is played by the modern rules**, as the user ruled on 2026-10-12. Nothing asks after NEW GAME; `StartNewGame` and loading a save both put `Ruleset.Modern` in force, and a save begun under Platinum's rules is brought over as it loads (`SaveData.Rules` is read for nothing else: effort over 252 and PP over a move's modern most are cut in `SavedPokemonData.ToPokemon`). `Ruleset.Platinum` stays as the **baseline**: the modern rules are written as differences from it, the tests of R1 to R18 hold it to the decompilation, and a battle handed no rules of its own is fought by it, as is anything a test makes, since `Ruleset.Current` is Platinum's until the engine says otherwise. A battle is fought by `BattleSetup.Rules`, or by `Ruleset.Current` when it is given none. A rule that differs between the generations is read from the `Ruleset` and the difference is added there, never as a constant beside the code. `Ruleset.Use` is called by the engine alone, as a game begins or loads, before any Pokémon is made: it also swaps the moves' and the species' own values in place (`MoveData.Modern`, `PokemonSpecies.Modern` and each form's: types, base stats and, since R19, the moves learned by level, the newest game's list of each species). It is static like `PlayerIdentity`, so tests must not call it: they hand rules to their battle and ask a move or a species for `Under(rules)`; a test that runs a whole game (`HeadlessGameTests`, in the collection that runs alone) puts Platinum's back as it ends. **A Pokémon learns by level only moves that do something** (`Pokemon.Learnset` asks `MoveDatabase.Runs`: a move whose `support` is `None` is passed over until R24 to R26 give it its effect).
- **The level cap and a region's levels** (plan 18 · W4, part a; rulings, "The level cap past 100 and a region's levels"): `Ruleset.LevelCap` is 100 by Platinum's rules and 260 by the modern ones (`Ruleset.HighestLevel`), and a Pokémon is made or loaded at 1 to 260 whatever the cap. **Whatever stops a Pokémon growing asks the cap, never 100**: a battle its rules' (`GainExp(..., Rules.LevelCap)`), the rest the game's (`Pokemon.Cap`, `AtTheCap`). Erratic and Fluctuating past 100 are a rule of ours in `GetExpForLevel`. A region file's `levels` (`LevelMapping`: pairs of its own game's level and the level met) lift every later region a step above the Champion of the one before; **the world files keep their game's levels**, and the mapping is applied as a Pokémon is met, only in a region the game didn't begin in (`GameFlow.BeganIn`, saved): `GameEngine.LevelHere` for a wild Pokémon's drawn level, `Trainer.Relevelled` (copies: the trainer of the map keeps their team) for every trainer of a battle, `Pokemon.Relevel` for a script's `wildbattle` and `givepokemon`. A new way of meeting a Pokémon in the field asks `LevelHere` too. Scripts keep writing their game's levels (1 to 100).
- **The crossing** (plan 18 · W4, part b; rulings, "Regions"): `Models/Crossing.cs` (GPU-free) is what going to another region does, and under the user's option B the whole team goes (`CanTake` lets everyone along, `Depart` boxes nobody); `TravelTo` (the attendant's `travel`, `Driver.TravelTo`) runs it and `Crossing.Arrive` (the region's Pokédex entered, `{rival}` its rival's). **A Pokémon belongs to a region** (`Pokemon.Region`, saved, copied by `CopyStateFrom`): `GameEngine.GiveIds`' sweep gives every one with none the region the player stands in, so a new way of receiving a Pokémon runs `GiveIds` before anything could cross, and a trade joins the receiver's (`Crossing.Join`). **Each region has its own rival**: `PlayerIdentity.RivalNames` by region, `InRegion` on every arrival (so `{rival}` is the rival of the region the player is in), `SaveData.RivalNames` beside the introduction's `RivalName`; a region crossed into names its rival with the script's `namerival` (`RivalNamingScreen`, `GameState.RivalNaming`), and a test that names a rival with `SetRival` puts `SetRival(null)` back. **The journey on screen** (plan 18 · W5; rulings, "The journey on screen") is a script, `common.Crossing`, which `common.Attendant` calls when `travel` answers 2 (ready; 1 not yet, 0 no way on): a question, the script's `fade out`, `travel go` (the field's music stopped, the vessel's `crossing_horn` or `crossing_engine`, `RegionDatabase.VoyageLine` over the dark, then `TravelTo` as the line is put away) and `fade in`; nothing of a vessel is drawn. A region's opening names its rival for a Champion who crossed in by asking the built-in `CROSSED_IN` (`IScriptHost.BeganIn`) before `namerival` (Oak's lab, Elm's officer). The headless host plays `travel` and `travel go` as the game does (it crosses at `travel go` with the region's Hall of Fame set, `Crossings`, `BeganInRegion`) and `namerival` (`RivalNameGiven`). Every link between two built regions names a departure with an attendant (`RegionTests`; Sinnoh's captain stands on Canalave's pier from `SinnohHallOfFame`, Hoenn's on the gangway of Lilycove's harbour from `HoennHallOfFame`, plan 21 · H9, part b).
- **A battle's chance** (`Battle/Sim/BattleRandom.cs`): rolls are asked for by kind (`rng.Roll(RollKind.Accuracy, 100)`), on whatever `Random` the battle has. A `BattleRandom` is Platinum's own generator (a seed replays the same anywhere; its state is one number) and can have a kind of roll fixed (`Force`). A battle given no generator makes one, seeded from `Dice`, so the game's battles roll with it too and the harness's are the same every run. A die's faces are the original's and `RollKind` says which face is which (the damage roll's 0 is the strongest hit and 15 the weakest; paralysis holds, a thaw comes and confusion hurts on a 0). Nothing in a battle may draw from anything but the battle's generator: a roll from `Dice` or a `new Random()` inside the rules breaks every replay, which `BattleCoreTests.ARecordedBattleReplaysTheSame` would show. A test whose outcome hangs on a roll fixes it by kind, running included (it can fail now).
- **Scenario tests** (`PokemonPlatinumTests/Scenario.cs`): `Mon` (known stats), `Steady()` (no critical hits, the strongest roll, nothing misses), `Battle`, `Turn`, `Damage`. One rule to a test, the rolls it depends on fixed by kind, and the expected numbers worked out from the decompilation's code in a comment. Take the numbers from `src/battle/` of the pinned commit (`git -C tools/DataImporter/.cache/pokeplatinum show <commit>:src/battle/battle_lib.c` reads a file without changing the checkout), not from memory.
- **Coverage** (`docs/mechanics/coverage.md`, written by the data importer): every move, ability and item with how much of it runs. `CoverageTests` fails when that file isn't what the data and the engine give today, and when a number falls under its floor. So after giving a move's effect, an ability or a held item its code, run the importer and raise the floor.
- **What must not move** (plan 24 · X2; `PokemonPlatinumTests/Golden/`): golden files hold what the code gives as text, a block to each thing (`=== battle 17`), read from the repository (`Repository.Root`), never the build output. `battles-platinum.txt` and `battles-modern.txt` are two hundred battles made from their seeds alone (`BattleCorpus`: seeds 1 to 100 under Platinum's rules from its 493 species and held items, 101 to 200 under the modern ones from all 1025 built `Under(Ruleset.Modern)`; every third a double battle, every fourth against a trainer of `trainers.json` with its own AI flags; both sides chosen by `TrainerAi`), each block its seed, rules, format, both teams, the log as `BattleText` writes it (an event to a line, a line's `Shows` (`+`) and `OnImpact` (`!`) under it, `P0` and `E1` for places, `mine#2 Luxio` for a Pokémon) and the result; `trainers.txt` is every trainer's team as `TrainerDatabase.PartyOf` builds it. A test that finds the text moved writes `<name>.received.txt` beside the file (git-ignored) and fails naming the blocks. A session that changes a rule, the data or the log's events on purpose accepts with `GOLDEN=accept dotnet test PokemonPlatinumTests --filter Golden` (a file not there yet is written the same way), reads `git diff PokemonPlatinumTests/Golden` and says in its close-out which blocks moved and why; a block of Platinum's rules that moved without a reason is a bug, which is what the files are for. A change of the log's text format rewrites the whole corpus: it goes in a session of its own. A new battle event needs nothing in `BattleText`, which writes any event's fields in their declared order.
- **Scripts and saves that must not move** (plan 24 · X3): `scripts.txt` is every script on every way through it, played as `StoryTests` plays them (`ScriptWalk`, in the test project beside `WorldWalk`: `Starters`, `EveryWayThrough`, `PlayEveryScript`, each class with maps of its own): a block to each script, its starters, then each way's answers, lines said, questions answered, log and what it left changed of the story (a way after the first told from the answer where it parts from the ones before), so a chapter shows its whole scene in the diff. `PokemonPlatinumTests/Fixtures/saves/` holds a save of every `WorldVersion`, `StoryVersion` and `SaveVersion` the game has written (`w<world>-s<story>[-v<save>]-<moment>.json`; the older ones written by hand with the fields their versions had, today's by the harness's `saves`), and `SaveCorpusTests` reads each as `SaveManager.Read` does: it must wake on a tile of a map of today, its Pokémon must be the data's, its story must come up to date (`StoryMigration.Upgrade`) the same every time, and what each comes to is `saves.txt`. **A session that raises a save's version** (`SaveData.CurrentWorld`, `StoryState.CurrentVersion`, or plan 07 · O2's `SaveData.CurrentSave`, the version of the save's own records, brought up to date by `Core/SaveMigration.cs` a step to a version) runs the harness's `saves`, copies the new version's files from `<out dir>/saves/` into `PokemonPlatinumTests/Fixtures/saves/` (`EveryVersionHasASave` fails until it does), keeps the old ones, and accepts `saves.txt` with the command above, reading in its diff what the migration did to every older save, instead of writing an old-save test of its own.
- **The levels of every place** (plan 03 · D13): `levels.txt` (`GoldenLevelTests`) is every place with wild Pokémon or trainers in it, a block to an area's key: the levels met in its grass or cave, on its water and on each rod, and its trainers by id with the highest level of each. They are Platinum's own, so a block that moved is an import that changed a level; a session that places wild Pokémon or trainers of this game's own (plan 03 · D12a) accepts the file with what it changes and sets its levels against the Battle Zone's (grass 47 to 55, trainers 51 to 61).
- **The damage oracle** (plan 24 · X6; `tools/CalcOracle/README.md`; rulings, "Oracle disagreements"): `OracleTests.TheModernRulesAreGeneration9s` holds `DamageCalculator` under `Ruleset.Modern` to Pokémon Showdown's calculator on 3,000 scenarios (`PokemonPlatinumTests/Oracle/`): the scenarios are drawn by `OracleScenarios` (`ORACLE=write`), the answers given by `node oracle.mjs` in `tools/CalcOracle` (pinned `@smogon/calc`; the workflow `oracle.yml` gives them afresh) and both committed, so no test runs node. A difference fails unless `known-gen9.json` names it with the session that owes it; **a session that changes the damage formula, an ability's or an item's part in it, or what a move hits with** runs `--filter OracleTests` and takes off the entries it settled (an entry that matches nothing fails as stale), and one that changes what the scenarios draw writes them, runs the oracle and commits both files.

### Data

Species, moves, abilities, items, trainers and maps are JSON files in `PokemonPlatinumEngine/Data/` (`species.json`, `moves.json`, `abilities.json`, `items.json`, `trainers.json`, one `maps/<Name>.json` per map), copied to the build output and read through `GameDataFiles` (camelCase names, enums as strings). The first four hold the whole National Pokédex and are generated by `tools/DataImporter` from the Platinum decompilation (Generations 1–4) and PokeAPI (later ones): don't edit them by hand; put corrections in `tools/DataImporter/Overrides/` and re-run it (`dotnet run --project tools/DataImporter`), which also rewrites `docs/mechanics/coverage.md`. A move's `effect` names what its fields can't say, and is the name the engine finds its code by (`Battle/Sim/BattleCore.Effects.cs`); moves the engine can't fully run also carry `support` (`Partial`: hits but the effect is missing; `None`: does nothing yet). A species lists its **forms** (plan 03 · D11: Mega, regional, Gigantamax, Rotom's appliances, Unown's letters; `PokemonForm`, `FormKind`), each named as Pokémon Showdown names it (`Charizard-Mega-X`, `Meowth-Galar`) and holding only what it doesn't share with its species; `PokemonDatabase.SpeciesOfForm` finds a form's species. A Pokémon in a form (`Pokemon.Form`, set by `ChangeForm`, kept in the save; `ChangeForm` leaves it the moves it had, as the original leaves a trainer's, so one that is *met* in a form with a learnset of its own is made with `Pokemon.InForm`, plan 03 · D13) takes its types, stats, abilities, learnset and size from it: read them from the Pokémon (`PrimaryType`, `HasType`, `Abilities`, `Learnset`, `Weight`, `CatchRate`), never from `Species`, and ask for its model and sprites by `ModelName`. A female of a species whose females are a form of their own (Meowstic, Basculegion, Pyroar) is in it from the start. The data also holds what later sessions write their rules from and nothing reads yet: the rest of Platinum's item table, and from Pokémon Showdown (a third pinned source) each move's power as a Z-Move and a Max Move, the Z-Moves and Max Moves themselves (`MoveKind`, never learned) and whose each Mega Stone and Z-Crystal is (Showdown also gives each form its colour). The importer leaves a file alone when its content didn't change. What abilities do is code in `Data/AbilityEffectTable.cs` (and, for a bonus in the damage formula or an effect in the field, by the ability's name where the rule is: "Battles", below), held items in `Battle/Effects/HeldItemEffects.cs`, keyed by the item's hold effect (plan 06 · R8). `PokemonDatabase`, `MoveDatabase`, `AbilityDatabase` and `ItemDatabase` load their file in a static constructor; `MapDatabase.Initialize()` reads every map file into fresh `Map` objects (new NPCs, newly rolled trainer Pokémon) through `MapFile.ToMap`, then makes the maps of the imported world (below). `MapDatabase.Get` gives the map of Sinnoh for a name it doesn't know. `docs/data-files.md` describes the formats; `MapFile.FromMap` writes a map back out, so a tool can generate map files. `TypeChart` is still code (Platinum's chart plus Fairy for later species). Maps are tile grids (`Overworld/Map`: ground layer, overhead layer, solid grid) plus NPCs, props, warps, signboards and wild encounters. What a person does when spoken to is a script (below): their own, or the common one for what their flags say they are (`IsTrainer`, `IsHealingNurse`, `IsPokeMartClerk`, `IsPCTerminal`, `IsStarterBriefcase`, `IsTransportAttendant`; `FieldScripts.For`).

Sinnoh's outdoors is the **imported world** (plan 01). `tools/MapImporter` reads the real Sinnoh from the decompilation and writes **world files** (`Data/WorldFiles.cs`; "World files" in `docs/data-files.md`): chunks of 32×32 tiles (a `TileBehavior` and a blocked flag per tile, a `TerrainCover` code for its look, height plates, props), matrices that lay chunks out in a grid (matrix 0 is the overworld), and areas with their events and land encounters. `Data/world/sinnoh/` holds what is built: `world.json` (hand-written: which matrices become maps, which areas are open), the generated files of the open areas and of the chunks in view of them (the importer's `--data`), and hand-written `overlays/<key>.json` (an area's music, where its doors lead, who its people are and our own lines for them), `habitats.json` (also `--data`), where the whole region's wild Pokémon live, open or not, for the Pokédex's area page (`Data/Habitats.cs`), `calendar.json` (also `--data`), Platinum's weather for each day of the year in the five places that follow a calendar, and hand-written `special.json`, where a Pokémon no table lists is met (Spiritomb's tower, `HabitatWays.Special`). To open an area: add its key to `world.json`, run `--data`, write its overlay.

`World` (`Data/World.cs`) loads that folder and `WorldMapBuilder` makes one `Map` of each matrix `world.json` lists: `Sinnoh` is the whole overworld, 960×960 tiles, so a position on it is a tile of the region and stays the same as more of it opens; each place that is a matrix of its own is a map too (`LakeVerity`, `FloaromaMeadow`, and a floor of a cave each: `OreburghGate1F`, `OreburghMineB2F`, `RavagedPath`…), reached by warps that the areas' files join by themselves. Such a map is **streamed** (`Map.IsStreamed`). Each of its chunks belongs to a `MapArea` with the name, music, wild Pokémon, trees and architecture a small map has for the whole of itself, so ask by place (`DisplayNameAt`, `BgmTrackAt`, `TreesAt`, `ArchitectureAt`, `RollWildEncounter(x, y)`, `HasLakeNear`), never the map's own properties. An area that isn't open is solid scenery, and where no chunk is loaded there is forest. `GameEngine.EnterArea` shows the name and changes the music when a step crosses into another area. Rooms and Kanto's stand-in are still hand-made map files, reached by warps (a town's Pokémon Center and Mart are rooms of its own, `OreburghPokemonCenter`, and a door that is still locked is one the story or a mechanic opens, or a house the original never used: plan 01 · M11 built every other room of Sinnoh in four parts, the gate houses among them, and the plan's "Ready from M11d" lists every door still shut). A building of several floors rebuilt to the original's plans (the Team Galactic Eterna Building, plan 02 · S6) is a map file per floor, joined by warps on its stairs' own tiles beside `SideStairsUp` (to the west of the warp) and `SideStairsDown` (to the east), as the original's `StairsWest` and `StairsEast` behaviours have them, and whoever goes up or down is put beside the stairs at the far end, walking away from them; saves made on the six hand-made maps the world replaced are moved by `SaveData.Place()`, each by the `WorldVersion` its map went with (Jubilife City's is `ImportedJubilife`), and a save made in a room that has been laid out again since wakes on the tile the room is come into by (`SaveData.PlaceOn`: "The hand-made rooms rebuilt", below).

A door can wait for the story (`"openedBy"` in an overlay's `doors`, `Warp.OpenedBy`): its tile is shut until a script sets the flag (`Map.ApplyDoors`, run with `ApplyPresence`); the door's own `read` script sets it (the Valley Windworks' Works Key, plan 02 · S6).

A way can be there for part of the story only (plan 02 · S10; `"switches"` in an overlay, `Warp.Until` and `Warp.From`): where the original keeps two warps on the same tiles and moves one of them away by script, both are kept, each with the flag it is there until or from, and `Map.ApplyDoors` shuts the one the story isn't at (`Warp.Shut`). `Map.GetWarpAt` gives the open one and `Map.WarpsAt` every way a tile leads at some point (`WorldWalk` takes them all). The lakes are the first: Valor Lakefront leads to the lake Team Galactic drained (`LakeValorDrained`, with `ValorCavern` in its middle) until `FLAG_GALACTIC_LEFT_LAKE_VALOR` and to the lake as it was (`LakeValor`) from then on, and Verity Lakefront to the lake as the game begins (`LakeVerity`, the decompilation's `lake_verity_low_water`) until Commander Saturn is beaten and to the lake Team Galactic holds (`LakeVerityGalactic`, its `lake_verity`) after. A way the story opens that shares its tile with no other (plan 02 · S12: the hole behind Mt. Coronet's cave painting) is a wall until then: `Map.ApplyDoors` shuts the tile itself (`ShutLoneWays`), which the builder leaves open, so a map fresh from the builder still has it. The summit is two maps on one way out the same way: Mt. Coronet's sixth floor leads to `SpearPillar` until `FLAG_SPEAR_PILLAR_IS_DISTORTED` and to `SpearPillarDistorted` (matrix 23) while it is set.

An overlay's people stand where the import puts them. A trainer's team is Platinum's (`res/trainers/data` in the decompilation: species, levels, and `moves` where the original chose them), the prize money its formula's, and the sight range the import's own (`AreaObject.Sight`; nought for one who battles only when spoken to). An overlay's trainer is their lines alone (plan 08 · P7, `OverlayTrainer`): who they are is `trainers.json`'s, found by the trainer their object's script names (`TRAINER_YOUNGSTER_TRISTAN`), and filled from it as the world loads (`TrainerDatabase.Fill`: the id, name and class, the AI's flags, the items, the team as the original builds it); only someone a script battles whose object names no trainer gives the table's `id` in the overlay. Two people can carry one trainer with one id (`doubleBattle`: Route 204's twins), and beating one beats both. Where two halves of a route list each other's people, each is placed by the half it stands in. A warp that is no door and leads nowhere yet is blocked.

**Caves** (plan 01 · M5): a map whose entry in `world.json` says `"setting": "Cave"` is rock (`TileType.CaveWall`) where the open country is forest, and so is everything that can't be come to from one of its warps (`WorldMapBuilder.CaveRock`, whose flood climbs a Rock Climb face along its grain and jumps a Bicycle's ramp as the field's rules do). An area's header may call itself a cave without being one (the lake, the meadow), which is why the setting is written there. Rock is stood up when the map is made (`RaiseRock`: two tiles above the nearest ground, a lip of three quarters right in front of open ground), in caves and outdoors alike, because the world's heights say nothing of rock nobody walks on. The mouth of a cave is its own tile (`CaveMouth`, the texture `dhole`), at the level of the ground. `Map.Camera` is the area's own camera (`FieldCamera`: default, cave, zoomed in), and `Map.IsDark` its darkness, which `Overworld/Darkness.cs` lifts once Flash has been used there (`Map.Lit`, plan 02 · S2). A place inside that the original builds as a cave or a dungeon of its own and a chapter needs is opened as such a map rather than rebuilt by hand (plan 02 · S9: `CelesticTownCave`, the ruins' painting, and `FuegoIronworksBuilding`, its moving floors), so its items, triggers and floors are the original's; its people are its overlay's, and someone a scene walks off whose movement is a single way of looking (`look_north`) has no `PersonMovement` and stays where the scene left them until the place's `OnEnter` puts them back.

**The centre** (plan 01 · M6) brought four things the world files didn't need before. A gate house with no rooms yet was walked through (`"through"` in the overlay: the warp into it came out at the warp on its far side); plan 01 · M11, part d built the last of them as rooms, no overlay passes one through any more and the builder no longer reads it ("The gate houses and the last rooms", below). The original uses one matrix for several rooms (the Solaceon Ruins' dead ends): `world.json` makes a map for each area, and `World.MapOf` finds the one made for the area asked about. An object of the area file can be left out for now (`"heldBack"`: an item reached only through a place not open yet). And a person has a height where the original stands them above the ground (`AreaObject.Y`, `NPC.Level`: the Cycling Road's riders on the deck over Wayward Cave's way in): `Map.NpcIn(x, y, height)` is who is in the way or within speaking distance at a height, and a way in under a bridge is lowered to the ground under it, with the deck kept over it.

**The east and the sea** (plan 01 · M7). Five places take their weather from Platinum's calendar instead of their header (`calendar.json`; `MapArea.Calendar` and `WeatherOn`, read by `Map.WeatherAt` for `GameClock.Today`; `Weathers.CalendarDay` is the original's row of a date). A puddle is a look of its own (`TerrainCover.Puddle`, `TileType.Puddle`, told by its behaviour whatever its texture), and whoever stands in one is mirrored in it (`WorldRenderer.DrawReflections`, `CharacterSprites.DrawReflection`). The Great Marsh's Safari Game is `Models/SafariGame.cs` (no drawing or input): `safari start` and `safari end` in a script, `if safari`, its steps counted by `GameEngine.OnStep`, its balls handed to the Safari battle, its end the common scripts `SafariTimeUp` and `SafariOutOfBalls`, its state in the save (`SaveData.Safari`). Someone whose script warps the player (the ferry between Canalave City and Iron Island) is a way somewhere: `WorldWalk` follows a person's scripted warps as it follows warps. Iron Island's lifts are `exits` of the overlay (a warp to the other level of the same map) until platforms move. Two models of a boat whose boxes overlap (Pastoria's) each keep their side of the line between them. The ground under a bridge for bicycles is walked any way: only its deck keeps the planks' one way (`FieldMovement.Step`).

**The Battle Zone** (plan 01 · M10, part a): the Fight, Survival and Resort Areas with Routes 225 to 230, and Route 224 behind Victory Road's three back rooms (`VictoryRoad1FRoom1` to `3`), are open (the rest is part b's, below). Nobody walks there from the mainland: the ship from Snowpoint City lands a Champion on the Fight Area's pier (`snowpoint_city.Ship`, plan 02 · S15, part a: 623, 434, which the walk of the whole game finds by the sailor's own warp), the pier's own sailor sails back (`fight_area.Ferry`), and Victory Road's collector stands in the way to the back rooms until the Hall of Fame and the National Pokédex (`victory_road_1f.OnEnter` sets his flag). Three things the builder learned there. The forest that fills the world between its places is one area with no trees of its own, so a chunk of it grows the trees its own cover shows (`Map.SetChunkTrees`, `Map.TreesAt`: the Battle Zone's filler is broad-leaved), and a cell with no chunk at all grows round trees where every wooded chunk beside it does. A ship keeps off the pier it is moored to: a boat's tiles leave out a row or column that lies on a pier's planks, where its model's box reaches over them (the Fight Area's ferry stood on its own sailor). And someone the overlay keeps `still` whom the original stands inside a model that blocks (the swimmer in the Resort Area's pool) keeps their tile open, and in a pool stands on its floor (`NPC.Wading`, rows of the sprite under the water, `WorldMapBuilder.PoolDepth`). The stand-ins built: the quay's containers (`PropType.Container`, four colours by place), the fountains in the pools before the Battle Frontier's gate (`GrandFountain`), the Resort Area's pools (`Pool`, set in paving), the Ribbon Syndicate (a hall with a `Veranda`: an open porch whose sides are glazed, `BuildingStyle.Veranda`) and the Battleground.

**The rest of the post-game** (plan 01 · M10, part b): Stark Mountain's foot and its three rooms (`StarkMountainRoom1` to `3`), Snowpoint Temple's hall and five floors (`SnowpointTemple1F`, `SnowpointTempleB1F` to `B5F`), Fullmoon and Newmoon Island with their woods (`FullmoonIslandForest`, `NewmoonIslandForest`), the Seabreak Path, Flower Paradise and the Battle Frontier's grounds (`BattleFrontier`) are open; the story's people and Pokémon there are in the area files under their flags and not on the maps (the plan's "Ready from M10b" lists them), the Frontier's fourteen ways into its facilities are `locked` until plan 06 · R18 builds them, the temple's two doors stay `locked` in Snowpoint City's overlay until plan 02 · S15 lets the Champion in, and the islands' landings and the temple's hall are in `WorldWalk.StoryArrivals` until the story's scripts lead there. Six things came with it. **Chunks the story swaps**: `world.json`'s `swaps` name a variable, its value and cells of a map's matrix with the chunk each becomes (the original's `MapMatrix_RevealSeabreakPath`: Route 224's four cells once `VAR_HIDDEN_LOCATION_SEABREAK_PATH` holds 0x1028); the map is built a second time round those cells (`WorldMapBuilder.BuildSwapped`: ground and models, no people), keeps both grounds (`Map.SwappedPlaces`), and `Map.ApplySwappedPlaces` lays down the one the story is at, which the engine does with `RefreshPresence`. The areas and their events are the same either way, and a swap of chunks a building stands on is refused. **Climates**: an overlay's `climate` (`AreaClimate.Volcanic`, `Frozen`; `MapArea.Climate`) is laid over the place's rig by `ArtLook.InClimate` ("Look", below). **Halls whose outline alone is blocked** (`WorldModel.Hollow`, the Frontier's four): the open ground shut in under the model's box is the building's, the building proper is the largest block ending on the row before the passage to its doors, and what it blocks from the passage south is a low forecourt (`BuildingInfo.Forecourt`). **Props of an overlay** (`props`: the Frontier's booths, model and statues, which its ground's own model holds) are laid down after the people, and whoever the overlay keeps `still` inside one keeps their tile. **Stairs in a passage** that runs past them (the temple's) put whoever comes up them down in the passage (`WorldMapBuilder.Join`). And **ground that is walked and does nothing is never water**: a tile painted as water that is neither surfed nor waded is paved (`WorldMapBuilder.Look`), and open ground of unnamed cover takes the nearest ground that isn't water (the Frontier's bridges). Stark Mountain's great room has the original's camera of its own (`FieldCamera.StarkMountain`).

What stands on a chunk is its **models**: the original's props by their short names (`pc`, `gym00`, `t1_h01`, `funsui`). `Data/WorldModels.cs` is the catalogue of those that stand outdoors, or in a cave that is open, and what each is here: a building (its kind, the town it is built like, its storeys, the name over its door), a prop (`PropType.Fountain`, `Boat`, `WindTurbine`…), a door, or part of the ground. `WorldMapBuilder.PlaceModels` gives each its place: a building takes the tiles the world blocks under its model's box, cut into rectangles (a main block, wings, and a porch where thin pieces flank a way in), with its doors where the world's warps are; the result is `Map.PlacedBuildings`. A building whose entry says its thin pieces are conveyors (`WorldModel.Thin`: the mine's yard) has them laid as belts and gantries (`WorldMapBuilder.Conveyors`, `PropType.Conveyor` and `Gantry`, which block nothing by themselves). The importer rewrites `docs/world-models.md` from the catalogue and reports any model it doesn't know that stands outdoors or in an open cave. No model has a stand-in any more (plan 01 · M10, part b built the last nine: Stark Mountain's lava and crater, the Battle Tower, the four halls and the lodges); `StandInUntil` names the session for one a later region brings.

The importer's README says exactly what it reads from the original's files and what it never keeps.

**A region's content is its file** (plan 18 · W1; `Data/regions/<id>.json`, `docs/data-files.md`, "Regions"): `Region.File` and the accessors over it (`Starters`, `Professor`, `Rival`, `HasStory`, `BadgeNames`, `OverworldMap`…) are what the engine asks for what once was Sinnoh's by name. The professor's lines of the introduction are in the file, said through `Text.OfData` (not `ui.json`); `IntroScreen.Open(cps, region)` and `StarterSelectScreen.Open(region)` take the region, `GameEngine.Here` is the one the player stands in, and `InitializeNewGame` goes by `HasStory`. Badges are the region's too (W2): `StoryState` keeps a mask per region, the field moves ask the region's `gates` (`FieldSpot.Region`), obedience, the Marts and a battle's money read the badges of `Here`, a script's badge words mean the host's `Region`, and the Trainer Card has a page per region (`CardPage.Region`, `TrainerCardInfo.RegionPages`) once the player has been in two. `StoryState.Starters` and `WorldModels.Of` are still Sinnoh's (later sessions make them per region), and `SpawnLocations.All` is Sinnoh's list beside the per-region `In`, `VariableOf` and `Respawn(story, region)` (W9); a new region's plan adds a file, not a constant.

`RegionDatabase` chains the regions Sinnoh first and then in generation order (plan 18 · W10): every new game starts in Sinnoh and nothing asks about regions, and finishing a region's story (its `<Region>HallOfFame` story flag in `StoryProgress`, saved as `StoryFlags`) lets the attendant at its departure map take the player to the next region (Sinnoh to Kanto, which plan 19 builds from FireRed's maps, has no attendant until the crossing of plan 18 · W5; Kanto to Johto by boat, its captain on Vermilion City's quay since plan 19 · K8; later links are `Transport.Undecided` until their region is built). Every map belongs to exactly one region and warps never cross regions. Sinnoh is the chain's first region and the fourth generation; a new game begins in the player's room there, with no Pokémon (plan 02 · S4), unless `--region` or `GameEngine.NewGameRegion` names another (Kanto's, since plan 19 · K3, is the player's room upstairs at home in Pallet Town with nothing, as FireRed's, and its story is scripts of ours on FireRed's beats and flags: `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "Kanto's first chapter"; a Champion crossing from Sinnoh arrives before the house, `Region.Arrival`; what a Kanto game starts with is `common.KantoNewGame`, which `StoryMigration.BeginNewGame` runs for every new game as each region's `<Region>NewGame`; Kanto's rival is FireRed's two classes, `TrainerDatabase.IsRival`, and a role FireRed has no song for is played by another of the region's own, `MusicDirector.StandIns`), and saves load where they were made. **Kanto** (plan 19) is `Data/world/kanto`, read from FireRed by the map importer's GBA reader (`--game firered --data`, plan 18 · W6, W7): its houses are drawn into its tiles and found at their doors (`world.json`'s `buildingsAtDoors`), its rooms are matrices built from their layouts (`MapSetting.Indoors`, sized to their floor by `WorldMapBuilder.RoomExtent`, styled by kind by `RoomStyleOf`), a sign is read by the words its overlay's `signs` gives the sign's script, and the look of a metatile no behaviour or label names is `GbaCover.Ranges`, written per tileset as each area opens (the import's `report.md` lists what is left). A room is furnished by the interior table (plan 19 · K2, `tools/MapImporter/Gba/GbaFurniture.cs`: a `PropType` by the GBA behaviour or by the tileset's ranges, written as chunk props `gba:<PropType>` and laid by `WorldMapBuilder.Furnish`, which stands the furniture against the back wall on the floor along its foot; a piece that blocks only on blocked tiles), and is level. A region's trainers are its world's `trainers.json`, their ids carrying the region's name (`kanto_youngster_ben`): `TrainerDatabase.Get(id, region)` finds a placed trainer in its own region first, and `All` stays Platinum's table. The Sevii Islands (plan 19 · K9) are the Seagallop's from Vermilion City, Four to Seven Island on the Rainbow Pass; Celio's last scene with the Sapphire sets `FLAG_SYS_CAN_LINK_WITH_RS`, by which the Elite Four and the Champion battle with their second teams, and Mewtwo waits in Cerulean Cave; a map FireRed lays out again by script is an alternate layout of the reader's (`GbaWorld.Alternates`, a chunk of its own) swapped in by `world.json`'s `swaps` on the flag the place's own arrival sets (the Dunsparce Tunnel dug through with the National Pokédex), and a measurer with a record of their own keeps it in a variable they name (`team size "<species>" <variable>`, `team record`), so Sinnoh's one size record is untouched (`docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "The first Sevii Islands", "The last Sevii Islands" and its part c). The stand-in Pallet Town is a test fixture (`Fixtures.Names`), and a save made on it wakes in Pallet Town (`SaveData.ImportedKanto`). **Johto** (plan 20) is `Data/world/johto`, read from HeartGold by the second DS reader (`--game heartgold --data`, `tools/MapImporter/Hg/`): a DS world like Sinnoh's, its buildings placed by their models through Johto's own catalogue (`Data/WorldModels.Johto.cs`; `WorldModels.Of(name, region)` looks there first and in Sinnoh's for the names both games share), its keys HeartGold's map constants (`new_bark`, `route_29`); its rooms are matrices too (`MapSetting.Indoors`, several areas to a matrix, a map each), cut to what their ways in reach (`WorldMapBuilder.RoomReach`, the rest of the chunk wall) and furnished from `bm_room.narc`'s models by the catalogue's furniture rows (`LayFurniture`). A grass slot there has a species for the morning (its own), the day and the night (`AreaEncounter.Day`, `Night`; `EncounterSlots.Grass` puts the hour's in). A new game begun with `--region Johto` stands before the player's house in New Bark Town with Chikorita until plan 20 · J4 writes the story. The Johto Safari Zone (plan 20 · J8, part b, and J9, part b; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "The Johto Safari Zone") is one map, `SafariZone`, of HeartGold's matrix 212 widened by the importer to hold all twelve areas side by side (`HgMaps.SafariCells`; no Area Customizer), each area with its own `safari_enc.json` tables and the name "Safari Zone"; its game is HeartGold's, thirty balls and no steps (`safari start endless`, `SafariGame.NoStepLimit`, `Endless`), ended by the last ball or by the zone's door, and Baoba's tests ask where a Pokémon was met (`if has "Geodude" met "Safari Zone"`, `Pokemon.MetLocation`). **The Bug-Catching Contest** (plan 15 · E5; rulings, "The Bug-Catching Contest") is HeartGold's own in the National Park: `Models/BugCatchingContest.cs` (GPU-free, the tables in `Data/world/johto/bug-contest.json`), the script command `bugcontest` at either gate house's counter, `BattleConditions.OnlyBall` (the Sport Ball in BAG's place, refused anywhere else, a catch kept by the contest and not the team), the park's grass swapped to the day's table in `GameEngine.MeetInTheField`, its minutes counted in the field and its battles (`GameEngine.BugContest.cs`), `GameState.BugContestResults`; the team set aside but for its first able Pokémon is saved in `SaveData.BugContest`. The Pokéathlon (plan 15 · E6; rulings, "The Pokéathlon"; style guide, "The Pokéathlon") is the same shape: `Models/Athlon.cs` (GPU-free: `AthleteStats.Of`, five stats of one to five by our own formula; `AthlonRun`, a course of three events against three rival teams drawn on the generator it is handed; `AthlonRecords`, the Athlete Points and bests, saved as `SaveData.Athlon`) and `Models/AthlonEvents.cs` (Hurdle Dash, Block Smash, Ring Drop: each an `AthlonEvent`, thirty frames a second, `Step(AthlonInput)` a frame and the rivals' `Think` on the event's own seed, so an event is a pure function of its seed and the player's presses; `Autopilot` plays the player's team for tests and the harness; a new event is a class there and a value at `AthlonEventKind`'s end), the script command `athlon able|open|points`, `GameState.Athlon` with `UI/AthlonScreen.cs` and `ModernUi.Athlon.cs`; the dome's clerk is `common.AthlonDesk`, who stands in the middle of the lobby's desk (`PokeathlonDome_1F`, 23, 8) since plan 20 · J13 opened HeartGold's square (`PokeathlonDome`, a map of its own with the dome a `Hall` of Johto's catalogue) behind Route 35's gate house's west door; the lobby's ways to the event rooms and the stands stay shut. The north (plan 20 · J10, part a: Routes 42 to 46, Mt. Mortar, Mahogany, the Lake of Rage, the Ice Path, Blackthorn, the Dragon's Den and the Dark Cave) is open with whoever waits for J11 left out of its overlays (rulings, "The north of Johto, first part"); the harness shoots it with `Modes/JohtoMountains.cs` at the end of `johto`'s places (`JOHTO_FROM=north` alone).
