The way to the League (plan 20 · J10, part b: Routes 27 and 26, Tohjo Falls, the reception gate, Victory Road, the Indigo Plateau and the League's rooms as places only, their battles J12's) is open too; HeartGold's keys that meet another region's (its Victory Road, Route 22 and Hall of Fame) take Johto's prefix (`HgWorld.Clashes`: `johto_victory_road_1f`), since a script file is found by its area's key alone (rulings, "The way to the Indigo Plateau"); the harness shoots it with `Modes/JohtoLeague.cs` after the north (`JOHTO_FROM=league` alone). **Johto's swarms, the Move Relearner and the Den after the League** (plan 20 · J13, part c; rulings, "The Dragon's Den after the League, Johto's swarms and the Move Relearner"): `--game heartgold --data` writes HeartGold's three swarm species into each area file (`swarm`, `surfSwarm`, `fishSwarm`) and `Data/world/johto/encounters.json`'s twenty places of `sSwarmMapLUT` (`HgWorld.SwarmTables`, each a `SwarmPlace` with a `way`); `Swarms.Places(region)`, `PlaceToday` and `PlaceOf` are region-aware (`SpecialEncounterTables.Of`), `EncounterMoment.SwarmWay` says whether the day's swarm is met in the grass, on the water (`EncounterSlots.Surf`) or on a rod (`EncounterSlots.Rod`), and Professor Oak's radio tells it (`RadioShows.SwarmLine`). `tutor remember` is the Move Relearner (`Models/Relearning.cs`: the Pokémon chosen, the move picked, `tutor learn` teaches it; the Heart Scale is the script's own `take`). The Den's post-game is `dragons_den.txt`'s `OnEnter` (the first arrival after `JohtoHallOfFame` sets `VAR_UNK_40FC` 1, standing in for Mt. Moon's rival battle), its trigger 1 `Rival` and the people it shows by their days and hours; the harness shoots all three last in `johto` (`Modes/JohtoPartC.cs`, `JOHTO_FROM=partc` alone). The fourth chapter's part a (plan 20 · J11; rulings, "Johto's fourth chapter, part a") opens the Team Rocket Hideout (`TeamRocketHQ_B1F` to `_B3F`, its count HeartGold's `VAR_UNK_40AC`) behind the souvenir shop's stairs, and the Mahogany Gym's blocks of ice (`npcType` `IceBlock`, `FieldMovement.IceBlockRun`: pushed by walking or sliding into them with no field move, they slide on over the ice until something stops them and are back where they stood when the room is come to again); `wildbattle … shiny` meets a shiny Pokémon. Its part b (rulings, "Johto's fourth chapter, part b") is Goldenrod's takeover on HeartGold's one count, `VAR_SCENE_ROCKET_TAKEOVER` (0 to 5, `goldenrod.txt`'s header): the Radio Tower's upper floors, lift and deck and the ways under the city (`Goldenrod_RadioTower_2F` to `_ObservationDeck`, `Goldenrod_Underground_B1F`, `_B2F`, `_Warehouse`, `Goldenrod_DeptStore_Basement`), the basement's gates things of the map behind flags of ours that its arrival sets back, and the third floor's slot asking the Director's `FLAG_UNK_1C1`, not the bag, since Silph Co.'s Card Key has the same name. Its part c (rulings, "Johto's fourth chapter, part c") is the Ice Path to the Rising Badge: the Blackthorn Gym's room names HeartGold's puzzle, `BlackthornPlatforms` (`Overworld/BlackthornPlatforms.cs`: three slabs on the lava, `TileBehavior.Magma`, each turned or slid by A on its buttons, `GameEngine.TryWorkSlab` and `PlaySlabs`, laid out afresh on every arrival; `WorldWalk` counts the lava as walked), HeartGold's `rock` objects are Strength boulders (`World.ObstacleFor`), a boulder fallen through a hole of the Ice Path is `npcType` `FallenBoulder` (a boulder of the map that stops a slide and is never pushed), and `givepokemon … knowing "<move>"` puts a move in a gift's first place (the Dragon Shrine's Dratini). The fifth chapter (plan 20 · J12; rulings, "Johto's fifth chapter") plays the League as Kanto's does: each Elite Four room's door ahead is opened by HeartGold's `FLAG_HIDE_<WHO>S_ROOM_EXIT` (an overlay `openedBy`), the way back locked, the hall's arrival (`pokemon_league_entrance.txt`) putting it all back; Lance's room's trigger plays his scene into `johto_pokemon_league_hall_of_fame.txt`, which sets `JohtoHallOfFame` and goes home to New Bark Town; and the ship on to Hoenn sails from Olivine's port hall (`RegionDatabase`'s link from Johto). **Hoenn** (plan 21) is `Data/world/hoenn`, read from Emerald by the same GBA reader (`--game emerald --data`), its trainers `hoenn_…` with Emerald's prize money (`GbaTables.EmeraldMoneyRates`); FireRed and Emerald share tilesets' names and not their metatiles, so Emerald's looks and furniture are `GbaCover.HoennRanges` and `GbaFurniture.HoennRanges` (asked before a metatile's label), never FireRed's rows. A new game begun with `--region Hoenn` steps out of the moving van beside the family's house in Littleroot Town with nothing (plan 21 · H2), and its story is scripts of ours on Emerald's beats and flags (`docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "Hoenn's first chapter"); its rival is the hero the player isn't, May or Brendan by the look (`hoenn.json`'s `rivals`, `Region.RivalFor`), drawn from an overlay's `Rival:MAY:BRENDAN`. Its key item screen is the PokéNav (plan 21 · H2, part b; `Models/PokeNav.cs`, `UI/PokeNavScreen.cs`, `GameState.PokeNav`), Hoenn's alone as the Pokégear is Johto's: the start menu's entry once `FLAG_SYS_POKENAV_GET` is set, its pages the map (`world/hoenn/townmap.json`), the contest condition, Match Call (Hoenn's book `world/hoenn/phone.json`, numbers kept by the Pokégear's own flags, calls in `phone.txt`) and the ribbons. Its berry trees are patches of their own past Sinnoh's 128 (`BerryPatches.HoennFirst`, numbered in the order of Emerald's tree names by the importer's `GbaTables.BerryTrees`; a save from before them is given them, `BerryPatches.Grown`), and a GBA room longer than a chunk is as wide as its floor (`World.cs`), so a hall is followed down its length. Where Emerald sets the weather by script by the player's row or column (Route 111's desert, Route 113's ash), an overlay's `zones` give a stretch of the area its own weather and song (`MapArea.ZoneAt`, read by `Map.WeatherAt` and `BgmTrackAt`; plan 21 · H4); a rock smashed over a Hoenn area rolls Emerald's `rock_smash_mons` (`Map.SmashOut`, the script command `smashencounter`), and on a GBA map a step along a stair's slope is never a cliff, since Emerald's stairs join any two levels (`FieldMovement.SlopedAlong`). A GBA warp panel that throws its rider onto plain floor (Lavaridge's Gym's vents) makes that tile a landing the builder lays no warp on (`GbaWorld`); a team of a region with an eye song of its own plays it from Hoenn's folder by the sound map's `eyeSongs` (`MusicDirector.EyeSong`: Team Magma's `eye_magma`); and Petalburg's Gym's doors are read, each asking before it lets the player into the room beyond (plan 21 · H4, part b; rulings, "Mt. Chimney, Lavaridge and Petalburg's Gym"). Dive (plan 21 · H5; rulings, "Dive and the sea floor") goes from a surfer's `DiveWater` to the same tile of `HoennUnderwater` (the floors of Routes 124 to 128, laid under their routes by `GbaWorld.LayUnderItsRoutes`) and back, by A on the tile underfoot (`common.Dive`, `Surface`, the script's `dive`, `GameEngine.Diving.cs`), in `TravelMode.Diving` at walking pace, offered only where the region's `gates` name it; a player on an underwater map always settles diving. The sea floor (part b; style guide, "The sea floor") has the waves' light over its picture (`WeatherFx.Caustics`, two layers of `LifeArt.Caustics` pinned to the floor, `WorldRenderer.DrawCaustics`), a diver's bubbles every 2.8 s of the field's clock (`FieldLife.Bubbles`, heard as `bubbles`), its own battle stage (`BattleArena.Underwater`, `BattleArenas.Underwater`, `ArtLook.UnderwaterArenaRig`; its terrain is still the water's) and its own bed of sound alone (`AmbienceBed.Underwater`, `FieldAmbience.UnderwaterGain`). Route 118 to Fortree City (plan 21 · H6, part a; rulings, "Route 118 to Fortree City") brought four things. **A room of the world can name a Gym puzzle** (`"puzzle"` in a `world.json` map entry, `WorldMapEntry.Puzzle`, made by `GymPuzzle.Create`): Fortree's Gym is `FortreeGates` (`Overworld/FortreeGates.cs`), Emerald's rotating gates, each turned a quarter by a step pushed against an arm and held where an arm would hit a wall or another gate, and put back as the player comes in. **Something in the way that nobody sees** is a thing of the map whose `npcType` is `Unseen` (`NPC.UnseenType`, `IsUnseen`: the Kecleon on Fortree's bridge and on Route 120); without the Devon Scope, talking to it runs `common.HoennKecleonLook`, and with the Scope the place's own script lights its tile, then cries, battles and hides it (`common.HoennKecleonFound`). `givepokemon … holding "<item>"` gives a Pokémon with an item in its hand (the Weather Institute's Castform). And **a stretch of the story with new people needs no story version**: `common.HoennChapterSeven` hides them and sets `FLAG_HOENN_CHAPTER_SEVEN`, `HoennNewGame` calls it, and the arrival scripts of Routes 118 and 119 call it for a save that lacks the flag. Fly over Hoenn lands where the region's `townmap.json` lists a `fly` place for the spawn. Route 121 and the Safari Zone (plan 21 · H6, part b; rulings, "Route 121 and the Safari Zone") brought three things: **Emerald's Safari Zone is the Great Marsh's `SafariGame`** (`safari start 500` at Route 121's gate, the common scripts' Hoenn way out to it), on one map, `HoennSafariZone`, whose six parts are areas keyed `hoenn_safari_zone_*` (Emerald's keys that meet FireRed's take the region's prefix, `GbaMaps`); a GBA trade can give each condition its own number and a sheen (`NpcTrade.Conditions`, `Sheen`); and the reader writes a warp under a buried trainer (`TRAINER_TYPE_BURIED`, Lavaridge's Gym) as a landing (`GbaEvents.BuriedOn`). `common.HoennChapterSevenB` sets what part b's places need, as `HoennChapterSeven` does part a's. The Seafloor Cavern to the Cave of Origin (plan 21 · H8, part b; rulings, "The Seafloor Cavern and Sootopolis") brought two things. **A floor that is no map of the sea's** (the Seafloor Cavern's pool, the pool under Sootopolis) is dived to and come up from at one place, Emerald's `setdivewarp`: an overlay's `diveTo` and `emergeTo` (`MapArea.DiveSpot`, `EmergeSpot`; `Map.DiveTargetAt` and `EmergeTargetAt` give the map and tile, tile for tile first). And **weather the story lays over a place for a while** is an overlay zone with `while` a flag (`OverlayZone.While`, kept by `Map.ApplyPresence`): Emerald's abnormal weather over the eastern sea while `FLAG_SYS_WEATHER_CTRL` is set, `FieldWeather.Abnormal`, which `Map.WeatherAt` turns into the downpour (`HeavyRain`) and the new `Drought` by turns of the field's clock (`Map.WeatherClock`, `Weathers.Alternating`; `ArtLook.Drought`). Lilycove City, Routes 122 and 123 and Mt. Pyre (plan 21 · H7, part a; rulings, "Lilycove City and Mt. Pyre") hide their later people by `common.HoennChapterEight` the same way, and Route 124 is come to by sea from Lilycove since then (`WorldWalk.OpenedAhead` is empty); Mt. Pyre's summit gives the Magma Emblem that part b's Team Magma hideout on Jagged Pass waits for. Sky Pillar to the Rain Badge (plan 21 · H8, part c; rulings, "Sky Pillar, Rayquaza and the Rain Badge") brought four things: **a map Emerald's own script always or first lays out with another layout** is read in that one (`GbaMaps.FirstLayouts`: Route 131 with Sky Pillar's island, the pillar's floors clean); **a cracked floor drops to the floor below** where its area's overlay names it (`holeTo`, `Map.HoleTargetAt`, `GameEngine.FallThroughCrackedFloor`, followed by `WorldWalk`); Juan's Gym is the puzzle `SootopolisIce` (thin ice cracked by a first step and broken by a second, the engine telling it every tile the player comes onto, `WalkTheIce`); and **a spawn on a map of its own names it** (`RegionSpawn.Map`, written by the GBA importer: Sootopolis City). The GBA import lays water a bridge crosses at the lowest level of its shores and stands an object of a bridge's elevation on its deck (Route 120's Steven), and draws shallows out at sea that touch no shore as a sandbar. Shoal Cave (plan 21 · H8, part d; rulings, "Shoal Cave and its tide, Routes 132 to 134 and Pacifidlog's three") brought **a place's `OnTransition`** (`ScriptLibrary.OnTransition`, Emerald's `MAP_SCRIPT_ON_TRANSITION`): `GameEngine.ArriveOnMap` runs it to its end behind the warp's fade, on a `HeadlessScriptHost` that shows nothing, and lays what the story swaps (`RefreshPresence`) before the place's first frame; it may only set flags and variables. The entrance room's runs `tide` (`Overworld/ShoalTide.cs`, Emerald's hour table, read only when come in from outdoors: `IScriptHost.Hour`, `CameInFromOutdoors`), whose `FLAG_SYS_SHOAL_TIDE` swaps in Emerald's high-tide layouts (`GbaWorld.Alternates`, Hoenn's `world.json` `swaps`); a walk of the whole game that must reach every room walks once at each tide (`HoennTests.Walked`). Ever Grande and the League (plan 21 · H9, part a; rulings, "Ever Grande City and the League") play as Kanto's and Johto's Leagues do: each room's way ahead an overlay `door` opened by the master's flag (a hall's by the flag of the room behind it, which is what makes a door's tile in a GBA wall open ground), the ways back locked, the hall's arrival putting it all back, the Champion's room one `OnEnter` scene into `ever_grande_city_hall_of_fame.txt` (`HoennHallOfFame`); Emerald's Victory Road is `HoennVictoryRoad_*` (Kanto's maps are `VictoryRoad_*`), and **its items' flags take the region's name** (`GbaEvents.ItemFlag`: a GBA game's item flags are named after the place, and FireRed's and Emerald's Victory Roads shared one). After the Hall of Fame (part b; rulings, "Home as Hoenn's Champion, the eon roamer and the way on") the family's house's arrival plays the television's report, whose colour named sets Latias or Latios roaming Emerald's routes (`Roamers.HoennRoutes`, `InHoenn`), and Dad's S.S. Ticket, and the ship on to Unova sails from Lilycove's harbour (`RegionDatabase`'s link from Hoenn). **Secret bases** (plan 21 · H10; rulings, "Secret bases") are `Models/SecretBase.cs` (GPU-free: `SecretBaseLayouts`, a spot's id to its room, Emerald's 24 `SecretBase_*` maps; `SecretBaseSave` and the decoration PC, `SaveData.SecretBase` and `Decorations`; `BaseFloor`, `SecretBases`), the reader's spots as `Map.SecretBaseSpots` (an area sign of type 3), `FieldMove.SecretPower` (party menu in Hoenn only), the script command `openbase` and `GameEngine.SecretBases.cs`, which lays a base's room out as the player comes in (its decorations as `PropType.Decoration` props, `Graphics/DecorationArt.cs`, solid where they block, its door aimed at the player's spot) and opens the Underground's `DecorationScreen` on its PC; **a room decorated through the seam may say where a piece may stand (`DecorationRoom.Allows`) and what each tile of its plan is (`TileAt`)**; `Data/decorations.json` is written by hand from Emerald's table, never generated. **Unova** (plan 22) is `Data/world/unova`, read from Black 2 by the third DS reader (`--game black2 --data`, `tools/MapImporter/Bw2/`, from fuddlesworth/pokebw2, whose maps are JSON, CSV and glTF; rulings, "Unova as Black 2's maps make it"). Its zones, matrices and terrain grids with their heights come as numbers, and each chunk's `model.glb` is read past its JSON chunk (textures' names, prop nodes) into its binary one only to find which texture lies over each tile (`Bw2Geometry`, the user's decision of 2026-10-20): a tile keeps nothing of it but a `TerrainCover` code, sorted by `Bw2Cover`'s rules of texture names (82.3% of the overworld's tiles at U2; the report lists the rest). Its tile classes are `Bw2Behaviours.Table`, with nine values of its own at `0xB0` to `0xB8`. U2 opened Aspertia City with its rooms and gate, Route 19, Floccesy Town with its rooms, Route 20 and Floccesy Ranch (`FloccesyRanch`, a map of its own); its keys are Black 2's zones in lower case, a route's, Victory Road's and the League's taking the region's name (`unova_route_19`, `Bw2World.KeyOf`), its flags and variables `FLAG_UNOVA_…` and `VAR_UNOVA_…` (`Bw2Events.Flag`, `Var`), its trainers `unova_…` with Black 2's teams, its buildings stand-ins of `Data/WorldModels.Unova.cs` until U5. Black 2's version is read where the two differ. An area whose Pokémon change with the season keeps spring's tables as its own and the others in `WorldAreaFile.Seasons` (`AreaSeason`), beside the dark grass's, the shaking grass's and the rippling water's tables; `RegionFile.HasSeasons` is Unova's alone. **What a season changes beside the look** (plan 22 · U4; rulings, "Seasons in Unova"): a matrix's `seasons` chunks are kept beside spring's as a story's swap is (`World.SeasonalSwaps`, `Map.AddSeasonalPlace`, a `SwappedPlace` with a `Season`, which `ApplySwappedPlaces` passes by) and laid by `Map.ApplySeason` wherever the engine takes or turns the season (`GameEngine.LayTheSeason`, behind a fade or with the options); an area's season's tables are `MapArea.SeasonTables` and `TablesFor(season)`, met through `EncounterMoment.Season` (null outside Unova); dark grass has its own table and now and then a pair (`Map.DarkGrassPairs`); Deerling and Sawsbuck wear the season (`Models/Seasons.cs`, `GameEngine.KeepTheCoats`) only where `SeasonHere` is one. A new game begun with `--region Unova` wakes in the player's room in Aspertia City with nothing (plan 22 · U5, part a; rulings, "Unova's first chapter"): its story is scripts of ours on Black 2's beats (Mom, Hugh at his door, Bianca at the outlook with the three and the Pokédex, the Pokémon Center's tour, Route 19's lesson, Alder, the ranch's Herdier and Team Plasma's first grunt, Cheren's Basic Badge, Route 20's hiker and Cheren's advice); **the decompilation's flags and variables by value (`FLAG_UNOVA_0x02e9`) can't be named in a script**, so a story person's overlay gives a hide flag of ours and a scene's trigger its own tiles and a variable of ours (`VAR_UNOVA_ASPERTIA`…), the rulings mapping each; `common.UnovaNewGame` is what a game starts with and `UnovaCatchUp`, called by the chapter's arrivals, brings an older save up to date with no story version; Black 2's "Pokémon Trainer Rival" is the rival's class (`TrainerDatabase.IsRival`); Aspertia builds as `Architecture.Hillside` and Floccesy as `Bellfield`, and `Data/music/unova/` has the chapter's songs. Part b (rulings, "Unova's first chapter, part b") goes on through Virbank City to the ship terminal (Roxie and the Toxic Badge, Team Plasma on the dock, the grunt on Route 20): its people are hidden by `common.UnovaVirbank`, which `UnovaNewGame` runs and `UnovaCatchUp` gives a save of part a's (a later chapter adds its own the same way, behind a `FLAG_UNOVA_<CHAPTER>_BEGUN`), Virbank builds as `Architecture.Dockyard`, and in Unova a warp on an opening's behaviour (`Entrance*`, `Exit*`) is walked into whatever the cover made of its tile (`World.PlaceEvents`); Part c (rulings, "Unova's first chapter, part c") sails to Castelia City and plays on to Colress on Route 4 (`VAR_UNOVA_CASTELIA`, `common.UnovaCastelia`): Castelia's avenue and north street stand on Black 2's rails, whose events the reader leaves out, so **the avenue is a hand-made map** (`CasteliaCity`, a map file of `Data/maps`), the streets' north ends open onto the plaza's rim by overlay `exits`, and every warp onto a rail is an overlay `door` (an area file's `toWarp` counts only the warps the reader kept); the reader writes Castelia's towers as chunk props of ours, `c3_tower` (`Bw2Cover.Towers`, Castelia's chunks alone), which `Architecture.Metropolis` builds as skyscrapers with a flat top (`BuildingStyle.FlatTop`). Plan 22 · U6, part a (rulings, "Unova's second chapter, part a") opens Route 4's trainers and houses, Join Avenue, the Desert Resort and Nimbasa City to the Bolt Badge (`common.UnovaNimbasa`, run by `UnovaCastelia`); Nimbasa builds as `Architecture.Funfair`; the reader writes Black 2's weathers 3 and 12 as `Sandstorm` (`Bw2World.WeatherName`) and reads a sign of Black 2's second condition outdoors (`Bw2Events.ReadToo`), and someone who stands in for one of two of Black 2's cast by the player's look (Nimbasa's Rosa and Nate) is two people of the overlay on one tile, the place's `OnEnter` showing one by `if boy`/`if girl`. Its part b (rulings, "Unova's second chapter, part b") opens Nimbasa's rooms, Route 5, the Driftveil Drawbridge and Driftveil City to the Quake Badge (`common.UnovaDriftveil`, run by `UnovaNimbasa`; Driftveil builds as `Architecture.Storehouse`): **a bridge over the sea** whose import is its deck alone names the sea's depth in its `world.json` entry (`seaBelow`, `WorldMapBuilder.DeckOverTheSea`: unnamed blocked tiles the sea, the rest a deck over it, the columns by its ways in ground), an overlay may name a `camera` where the area's header number means nothing here (`FieldCamera.Drawbridge`), and Clay's Gym, a mine on Black 2's rails, is a map file of ours (`DriftveilCity_Gym`) reached from the lobby by an overlay exit. Wave 48's look at the shots (rulings and plan 22, "Wave 48's fixes"): a texture is judged with `--game black2 --chunk <folder>` (the chunk's looks and every pair of textures on and over its tiles with how `Bw2Cover` sorts them); `yuk` after a number is a floor's, never snow's (Nimbasa had been snow country); a zone's `battle_bg` 2 and 3 are `City`, and **paving where an area's header names a `City` background is fought on the `City` stage** (`BattleArena.City`, a street; any region); an overlay's `interior` names a room's look (`InteriorStyle.Street`, Join Avenue) and `overTheDark` makes what a Gym hall's ways don't reach void (the Nimbasa Gym's runway); and **a room of several chunks takes its own part of the room's floor in each chunk's scene** (`MapScene.Prepare`: each chunk's floor had been the whole room's squeezed into it, which only showed once a multi-chunk room's floor was not one tile repeated). Part c (rulings, "Unova's second chapter, part c") opens Route 16, the Lostlorn Forest, Anville Town (`Architecture.Railyard`) at the end of the Gear Station's train (the station a map file of ours, `GearStation`, its Anville desk a ride over the dark), the Relic Passage, the Relic Castle and its buried halls, the World Tournament's grounds, the Plasma Frigate's deck and Join Avenue's three shops (`common.UnovaFrigate`, run by `UnovaDriftveil`; `VAR_UNOVA_PWT`): **Black 2's obstacles are told by the global script they run** as well as by their looks (`WorldMapBuilder.ObstacleFor(AreaObject)`: 10000 a Strength boulder, 10004 a tree Cut fells), and an overlay's `hiddenBy` for one takes it away for good; **a hole a pushed boulder fills** (the behaviour `StrengthHole`) is an overlay's `holes` entry with the flag that fills it (`Map.FilledHoles`, a wall until then, kept by `ApplyDoors`) and `pits` on its tiles whose script sets the flag (`common.BoulderFallsIn`); the Relic Castle's sand pits are its own triggers, scripts that `warp` to the floor below, which **`WorldWalk` follows as it follows a person's warps**; and a Unova way out that a cave stood up as rock while it led nowhere is cut down to the floor it is walked into from (`World.PlaceEvents`). The harness's `unova` mode (not part of `all`) shoots the four places by day and after dark (`u01` to `u08`), a few rooms (`u10` to `u16`), times the walk from the player's door to Floccesy Town's Pokémon Center, and last plays the chapter in a new game of its own (`Modes/UnovaStory.cs`, `us01` to `us30`, and part b's `us31` to `us46`, part c's `us47` to `us80`; plan 22 · U6's places `u21` to `u73` and story `us80` to `us149`). **Seasons** (plan 22 · U3; style guide, "Seasons") are Black 2's, a month each (`GameClock.SeasonOf`, `GameClock.Season`, `FixedSeason` from the options' `Season` row), and only a map of a region with seasons has one: **whatever is seasonal asks `GameEngine.SeasonHere` or `SeasonOn(map)`, never the clock**, which is null everywhere but Unova. The engine holds the field's season and moves it only where the picture may change (a game entered, an option, behind a fade, where a calendar's turn is told on the toast). The look is `Graphics/Seasons.cs`'s `SeasonPalette` (`Default` is the field's own and spring's, texel for texel), handed to `PixelGround.Bake`, `TreeModels`, `NatureArt` and `SceneTextures.TallGrassIn`/`LawnTuftIn`/`FlowersIn` through `MapScene.Prepare(map, chunk, season)`; `WorldRenderer.SeasonOf` decides each map's and drops scenes baked in another; `ArtLook.Seasoned` grades the open's light after the weather; the location sign gains a second line. The harness fixes spring for every mode; its `seasons` mode shoots the four. 

**Fly between regions** (plan 18 · W9; rulings, "Fly between regions"; style guide, "The Town Map", "Fly between regions") is the one way back to a region left. `Crossing.CanFly` allows every other region entered (`Crossing.HasEntered`: its Pokédex entered, a badge or its Hall of Fame, as the Trainer Card counts), built and with a `townmap.json`; `Crossing.FlyRegions` is the tabs in the chain's order. Fly's choice is `TownMapScreen.Open(tabs, home, mode, flier)` (`Tabs`, `Tab`, `HomeTab`, `ChangeTab` on the shoulders; the one-region `Open` stays for every other caller), built by `GameEngine.FlyChoiceNow` and `TownMapOf(region)`. A town of another region is a crossing, through `GameEngine.CrossTo(region, spot)`, which `TravelTo` (the vessel) is one line of: **whatever crosses into a region goes through `CrossTo`** (the sweep, `Crossing.Depart`, `Crossing.Arrive`, the music, the sign), and the headless host's `Fly` and `travel go` share its own `CrossTo`. A script crosses by no vessel with `travel to "<map>" X Y [facing]` (`IScriptHost.CrossInto`, the same `CrossTo`; plan 20 · J13, part d: Route 40's guide into Sinnoh's Battle Frontier and the Johto guide on its grounds back, one set of Frontier records from either door; rulings, "Route 40's way to the Battle Frontier"). Each region keeps its own place to come back to (`SpawnLocations.VariableOf`: Sinnoh's `VAR_SPAWN_LOCATION`, the others `VAR_SPAWN_LOCATION_<ID>`; `OfRoomAnywhere`, `Respawn(story, region)`), which Teleport reads in both hosts; nothing new is saved.

**The content check** (plan 16 · T8; `tools/ContentCheck`, whose README lists every check). The files written by hand are held to their rules by one tool, and **a session that adds or changes an overlay, a room or a script runs it** (`dotnet run --project tools/ContentCheck`, two seconds) before it runs anything else: `ContentTests.TheGamesOwnFilesPass` fails on a finding. What it finds is, above all, what the readers pass over without a word, because `WorldMapBuilder.PlaceEvents` goes through the area's own lists and only asks the overlay about each: someone the overlay names who is no object of the area (or is a signpost, an item's ball, the other half of a route's); a door, a locked warp or a switch numbered past the area's warps; a door or an exit to a name that is no map (`MapDatabase.Get` would answer with the map of Sinnoh) or onto a blocked tile; a field the format doesn't have (`"dialogue"`), which the JSON reader skips; a script, a counter, a look (`npcType`) or an evolution site that isn't there; a flag someone is hidden or shown by, or a way opened or switched by, that no script sets; and for a room everything `DataFileTests` held (it loads, reads back as written, its warps land on open ground) with the same of its people, signs and triggers. It also measures **every line said** (a person's `dialog`, a trainer's two lines, a sign, a script's `say`, `text`, `ask` and `choose`) against the text box: a line is written whole into the box and nothing pages, the box holds three rows (`UI/Kit/DialogueBox`, whose numbers `ModernUi.DrawDialogue` draws by), and the line is measured as the game measures it (`UI/Kit/TextMetrics`, the typeface's widths read with no window) with `{player}` and `{rival}` at the widest name the keyboard takes; and it names a character the typeface can't draw. `--rows "<line>"` shows how a line breaks while it is being written. **A finding is fixed in the file**; if the check is wrong the check is fixed, with a case in `ContentTests`' own world. The one list of exceptions is `Checks.Flags.NeverSetOnPurpose` (a flag named on purpose though nothing sets it, each with its reason; one that something comes to set is itself a finding). Where a rule lived only in a test it is the tool's now and the test asks the tool (`WorldTests.EveryOverlayEntryPointsAtSomethingThatIsThere`, three of `DataFileTests`): a new rule for these files goes into the tool, not into a test of its own. `--plan` draws a room or an open area from above on the CPU (`PixelGround`, an area from its own chunks) with everything on it tagged and named, which is how a room is looked at without the harness and how what a file says is held against where it stands.

### Walking the field

A tile's `TileType` is what it looks like; what it does is its `TileBehavior` (`Map.BehaviourAt`), which keeps Platinum's own numbers. A map of the world carries the original's value for every tile; on a hand-made map it follows from the type. `docs/tile-behaviours.md`, which the importer rewrites, lists the 94 in use with what the game does with each (`TileBehaviors.InGame`; a test keeps that table true).

- **Rules** (`Overworld/FieldMovement.cs`, no drawing or input, following the original's `src/player_move.c`): `FieldMovement.Step(map, x, y, direction, walker)` says what a step comes to (`Walk`, `Hop` over a ledge, `Jump` off a Bicycle's ramp, `Land` from the water, `Climb` up a waterfall or a rock face, or `Blocked` with the `Obstacle` that stopped it), where it ends, at what height and at what `Pace`. `Carries` says where ice or a moving floor takes someone next. A `Walker` is how they travel (`TravelMode`: on foot, surfing, cycling), the height they stand at and what the party can do (`FieldMoves`).
- **Heights**: `Map.HeightAt`, slopes for stairs, `DeckAt` for a bridge over the ground. A step of 1.25 tiles or more is a cliff. Whoever comes onto a bridge's tile stands on the deck or the ground under it, whichever is nearer their own height (`SurfaceAt`).
- **The player** (`Overworld/Player.cs`) plays the rules out: `Mode`, the height underfoot eased from tile to tile, hops, slides, `StartSurf`. `Update` reads the keys and calls `Advance`, which takes the steering as arguments, so tests and the harness walk the player without input.
- **Water is not solid**: open water is blocked for walkers by its behaviour, and `Map.IsWalkable` says so. Only a rock standing in it is a solid tile of water.
- **Wild Pokémon**: `Map.RollWildEncounter(x, y, steps, water, thick, lead, moment)` rolls the place's land or water table by Platinum's odds (`EncounterSteps`, which the player carries and resets after a battle or a map change). The ability of the Pokémon at the head of the party (`WildLead`, which `Player.Lead` carries and the game keeps up to date) has its say in each step of it, by the original's `wild_encounters.c` (`Overworld/WildEncounterRules.cs`, no drawing or input: the rate, the row of the table, the level, whether the Pokémon is scared off, its nature, its gender). What comes back is a row of its own with the level decided and the nature and gender chosen, from which `GameEngine.StartWildBattle` makes the Pokémon.
- **Beyond the tables** (plan 06 · R13; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "Wild encounters"): what the moment brings is an `EncounterMoment` (`GameEngine.EncounterMomentNow`, which the player's steps, a rod, Sweet Scent and Honey ask), and the table it makes is `EncounterSlots.Grass` (the time of day's, a swarm's, the Trophy Garden's and the Great Marsh's species in the original's slots, from the area files' `day`, `night`, `swarm` and `radar` lists and `encounters.json`, `SpecialEncounterTables`). What the game remembers is `Models/SpecialEncounters.cs` (the day's number and the dailies drawn from it, swarms begun, the Trophy Garden's two, the 21 honey trees, the radar's battery, the six roamers, the poison's steps; saved as `SaveData.Encounters`, its clock moved on by `KeepTheEncounterClock`). The rules are GPU-free, one file each: `Overworld/HoneyTrees.cs`, `PokeRadar.cs` (`RadarChain`, which the engine keeps and doesn't save, as the original doesn't), `Roamers.cs`, `FieldPoison.cs` (with `Feebas`); `GameEngine.Encounters.cs` hands them the game's state, gives wild Pokémon their held items (`WildEncounterRules.HeldItem`) and tells the roamers and the radar how a battle met in the field ended (`fieldEncounter`). A roamer met is a `WildEncounterEntry` with its `Roamer` slot, a radar patch's sparkle its `Shiny`. The radar's patches part the grass through the shader's walkers (`WorldRenderer.Stirring`) and a honey tree with Pokémon at it sheds leaves (`FieldLife.ShakeTree`).
- **Every species with nobody else** (plan 03 · D12a; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "Every species in Sinnoh with nobody else"): `Data/SpeciesSources.cs` (GPU-free) is every way the region's data and scripts give a species (the open areas' and rooms' tables, the tables of no area, `givepokemon`, `giveegg`, `wildbattle`, `roamer`, the trades, the fossils, and to a fixed point evolution, Eggs and wild held items, a trade's evolution by a Linking Cord), and `SinnohSpeciesTests` holds all 493 of Diamond, Pearl and Platinum to it, with what only Pal Park gives (`PalParkPool`) written in the test. **A session that adds a way to a species, or takes one away, sees it there** and moves the species on or off `OnlyInPalPark`. An overlay's `national` puts our own species in the grass's slots 8 and 9 and the Super Rod's slot 3 once the National Pokédex is had (`EncounterSlots.Grass`, `SuperRod`), at those slots' own levels, in the places Diamond and Pearl have the species (never the Battle Zone for its own sake), and `palpark check` lets the day's six visitors of the pool come (`PalParkState.Welcome`): a fixed rotation by the date (`VisitorSpecies`), never a draw. **The dual slot** (plan 08 · P23; rulings, "The dual slot"): each area with grass carries the two species each of the five GBA games puts in its slots 8 and 9 (`WorldAreaFile.DualSlot`, `MapArea.DualSlot`, a `DualSlotTables`, written by the importer's `--data` from the encounter files' `ruby`, `sapphire`, `emerald`, `firered` and `leafgreen`); the game in the console's second slot is the options' SLOT 2 GAME (`GameSettings.SlotTwo`, `SlotTwoGame`, None unless chosen: not `Foreign.GbaGame`, which tells who wrote a save file), handed on by `EncounterMoment.SlotTwo`, and with the National Pokédex `EncounterSlots.Grass` puts its two in slots 8 and 9 after our own D12a species (which they take the place of) and the Pokégear's sounds, so steps, Sweet Scent, Honey and the radar all meet them. The Pokédex's area page leaves them out, as the original does.
- **Field moves** (plan 02 · S2; `Overworld/FieldMoveRules.cs`, no drawing or input): `FieldMoveRules.Check(move, spot, story)` is the original's check of a field move where the player stands (a `FieldSpot`, made by `SpotOf`): the badge Platinum asks for (`BadgeFor`: Relic for Defog and Fen for Surf, not Diamond and Pearl's), the place, a partner, what is in force already. The party menu lists each Pokémon's field moves in its own move order (`PartyScreen.ActionsFor`, with Soft-Boiled and Milk Drink's sharing of HP) and runs `common.Use<Move>`; facing an obstacle or a tile runs `CutTree`, `Rock`, `Boulder`, `RockFace`, `Waterfall` or `Water` (`GameEngine.TryInteract`), which ask the move and the badge themselves. The movement rules ask only that the move is known: going up a waterfall or a rock face also needs `Walker.Climbing`, which only a yes to the question gives (`Player.Climb`), and the way down a waterfall needs the move and no badge, as in the original. A script's `usemove` plays the move's cut-in (`UI/FieldCutIn.cs`) and makes the obstacle give way as the band closes.
- **Obstacles** are things of the map (`NPC.Obstacle`, `IsThing`; `Map.AddObstacle`), not props: a cut tree, a cracked rock or a Strength boulder stands in the way like a person and is drawn as a card (`ThingCards`, `WorldRenderer.DrawThings`). Each is hidden by a local flag of its area (below), and `Map.ResetObstacles` puts pushed boulders back. Strength, Flash and Defog are story flags (`FieldMoveRules.StrengthFlag`, `FlashFlag`, `DefogFlag`) that `GameEngine.KeepFieldMovesInForce` turns every frame into `Player.PushesBoulders`, `Map.Lit` and `Map.FogLifted`.
- **Coming to another place** (`GameEngine.ChangePlace`, on every warp and every step into another area) forgets what lasted only there, as the original does when the map's header changes: the local flags (`FLAG_MAP_LOCAL_...`, `StoryState.ClearLocal`; an obstacle's is made its area's own by `WorldMapBuilder.LocalFlag`, because one map holds many areas), Strength, Flash and Defog outside the caves, the pushed boulders, and the Bicycle where the place doesn't allow it. A warp out of the map of Sinnoh notes the way back out for Dig and an Escape Rope (`exitSpot`, saved), a Pokémon Center notes the town Teleport goes back to, and a first arrival in a town lets Fly go there (`Data/SpawnLocations.cs`, the original's twenty fly spots, by their `FLAG_FIRST_ARRIVAL_<AREA>` flags; Fly's map is the Town Map in its `Fly` mode: "The map of the region", below).
- **Key items** (plan 02 · S2): the bag's USE runs an item that works in the field (`BagScreen.TakeFieldUse`: the Bicycle, the three rods, an Escape Rope, the Town Map), and REGISTER puts a key item on the item button (C; `SaveData.RegisteredItem`). The Bicycle's rules are `Overworld/Bicycle.cs` (where one may ride or get off: not on the Cycling Road, whose gates let only riders through, `Warp.CyclistsOnly`, nor between a gate's keepers and its door to the road, `BicycleRules.ForcedInGateFlag`; the two gates are the only rooms ridden through, a map file's `bike`), and the run button changes its gear. A rod's cast is `Overworld/Fishing.cs` (`FishingAttempt`: the original's timings, and the area's rod tables, `MapArea.RodEncounters`), which `GameEngine.UpdateFishing` plays over the field. The Pokétch is `Models/Poketch.cs` (given by `poketch on`, its apps by `poketchapp`; saved), shown over the field by `UI/PoketchView.cs` (P puts it on the screen, O changes the app, I takes it in hand: the player stands still and the arrows move a cursor over the app's buttons, the stylus's stand-in). Every app Platinum gives runs (plan 06 · R14b; the Stopwatch and the Alarm Clock, which nobody gives, don't, `Poketch.Runs`): each is a `PoketchAppState` in `Models/PoketchApps/` (GPU-free: its buttons in the screen's blocks, `Press`, `Update`, made afresh each time it comes up as the original's task is, keeping what the save keeps through `Poketch.Recall` and `Keep`), reading the game through a `PoketchContext` the engine fills each frame (`GameEngine.PoketchNow`), and drawn by `ModernUi.Poketch*.cs`. `if poketchapp X` asks whether one has been given.
- **Boxes anywhere and a sorted bag** (plan 12 · Q13): the options' `BoxesAnywhere` row (`RulesDefault`, read through `FieldHelp.On` as `GameEngine.BoxLinkOn`, so Platinum's preset keeps its menu and bag) gives the start menu a BOXES entry after POKéMON (`StartMenu.HasBoxes`, set as the menu opens; `GameEngine.OpenBoxesFromMenu` in `GameEngine.BoxLink.cs`, refused by `CanReachBoxes`: a map with `Map.NoBoxLink` (a map file's `noBoxLink`), a Safari Game, the Bug-Catching Contest) and the bag its orders: `Inventory.OrderOf`/`SetOrder` per pocket (`BagOrder`, saved as `SaveData.BagOrder`, forgotten by `Clear`), `BagScreen.Sorting` (set every frame by `KeepTheHelpersInView`) turning the item button into the order and PageLeft/PageRight into the letter jump. `GetPocketItems` is the ordered list, so anything that reads a pocket by index reads it in the order shown.
- **REMEMBER and RENAME in the party menu** (plan 12 · Q19): the options' `PartyTools` row (`RulesDefault.Rules`, read through `FieldHelp.On`, so Platinum's preset keeps today's menu) puts `PartyActionKind.Remember` and `Rename` in `PartyScreen.ActionsFor(pokemon, region, tools)` (`PartyScreen.PartyTools`, set each frame by `KeepTheHelpersInView`; none for an Egg, RENAME only for `OriginalTrainer` null). REMEMBER is `Models/Relearning.cs` (`MovesFor`, `Teach`; plan 06's Move Relearner will call it) and the screen's own list (`Remembering`, `RememberMoves`, `ForgetFor`, `ModernUi.RememberPanel`, which uses the evolution scene's `MoveChoice` for a full set). RENAME is `UI/NicknameScreen.cs` (`GameState.Nickname`, `GameFlow.NicknameScreen`, `GameEngine.PartyTools.cs`): the introduction's keyboard at ten letters (`NameEntry`'s `maxLength` now decides what `PlayerIdentity.Clean(name, max)` keeps; a trainer's stays seven), and the one naming prompt plan 13 · V4, plan 06's catch question and the Name Rater ask. The harness's `menus` ends with `20r0` to `20u`.
- **Field move helpers** (plan 12 · Q18, first piece; the plan's Q18 outcome lists what is left): the options' `FieldHelpers` row (`RulesDefault.Rules` by default and read as it is, not turned into On, so a Platinum-preset game keeps its HMs) lets Cut, Fly, Surf, Strength, Defog, Rock Smash, Waterfall and Rock Climb be done with no knower once the region's Badge (`FieldMoveRules.BadgeFor`) is held and the HM is in the bag. `Overworld/FieldHelp.cs` (GPU-free) works the grants out whenever asked (`Granted`) and stores nothing; `GameEngine.GrantedMoves` / `IScriptHost.Granted` hand them to `player.Moves` (`MovesOf(party) | FieldHelp.Moves(...)`, in `GameEngine` and `HeadlessScriptHost`), to the script question `granted "<move>"` (asked beside `knows` by the obstacle scripts: a script that asks `knows` for a helped move asks `granted` too) and to `usemove`, which falls back to `FieldHelp.HelperFor` (a seeded wild Pokémon, nothing from `Dice`). Part b (wave 44): the party menu's strip (`PartyScreen.Helpers`, `OnStrip`, kept by `GameEngine.KeepTheHelpersInView`; a pill chosen gives `TakeFieldMove` the place `PartyScreen.HelperUser`, -1, and `UseFieldMoveFromMenu` makes the user with `FieldHelp.HelperFor`), `TownMapScreen.Flier` as a `Pokemon?` (a helper may fly), and the Pokétch's `PoketchApp.HiddenMoves` (`Poketch.ShowsHiddenMoves` from the row, never saved, refused by `Register`; `PoketchContext.Granted`); the harness's `fieldmoves` ends with `fm30` to `fm34`.
- **Gyms** (plan 01 · M9, part 1): a Gym is a room on the original's plan (`interior: Gym`) whose map file names a `puzzle` (`Overworld/GymPuzzle.cs`, GPU-free): `EternaClock` (the flower clock: its five times, the tiles each opens and the hour hand's tip, hopped over through `GymPuzzle.HopsOver` in `FieldMovement.Step`), `VeilstoneBags` (the bags' track table, the kick and the stacks it knocks down) `HearthomeDoors` (the door chosen and its sign's tile, the wrong doors' warps, the fog) and `PastoriaWater` (plan 01 · M9 1b: the floating floor at nought, two or four tiles, set by a button's `pressbutton`, moving at the original's pace and carrying walkers by the original's rule for heights through `GymPuzzle.FloorAt` and `Refuses` in `FieldMovement.Step`) and `CanalaveLifts` (plan 01 · M9 2a: four floors ten tiles apart, each with the original's map of what is walked, and twenty-four platforms that carry the player from one end to the other when stepped onto, `GameEngine.TryRideLift`; a floor and its people show only once the viewer has risen toward it, `GymPuzzle.Hides`, and trainers look along their own floor, `Apart`) and `SunyshoreGears1` to `3` (plan 01 · M9 2c, `Overworld/SunyshoreGears.cs`: the Sunyshore Gym's three rooms of gears, each room in one of four states a quarter turn apart; the original's table of the walkways each state closes, refused through `GymPuzzle.Refuses`; a button on a hub, the rooms' own triggers running `gearbutton`, turns every gear of the room each its own way, played by `GameEngine.PlayGears` while the field waits; laid out by the door the player comes in by, through `GymPuzzle.ArriveAt`, which is told where they stand). The Snowpoint Gym (plan 01 · M9 2b) has no puzzle: its ice is the field's own (`Overworld/IceSlide.cs`, the original's `PlayerAvatar_TileMove_Ice`: a speed of nought to three gathered down a slope and lost up one, a slip back with none left, the original's frames a tile; `Player.IceSpeed`), and its snowballs are things of the map (`npcType` `Snowball`, drawn by `GymPieces` as cards of the scenery, lit as the ice is) that a slide with speed breaks (`IceSlide.Breaks`, `Player.TakeBroken`, `GameEngine.ShatterSnowball`) and that are back the next time the player comes in (`Map.ForgetForced`, as everything a script or a slide took off the map is). A room's map file may give the original's height plates, behaviours and people's heights (`heights`, `behaviours`, `level`), and is then walked and drawn with relief as the field is (the Oreburgh Gym's tiers); it may also give its own `weather` (the Lost Tower's fog, which Defog lifts) and `encounterRate` (its wild Pokémon live on the Old Chateau's floor behaviour). `Map.Puzzle` is applied every frame from the story (`GameEngine.KeepPuzzleInForce`) and laid out afresh as the player comes in (`ArrivePuzzle`, from `ArriveOnMap`); `GameEngine.Gyms.cs` plays what moves (a clock's turn, which the script waits for through `FieldHost.Busy`; a bag's run, which holds the field) and `Graphics/GymPieces.cs` draws the pieces the room's scene can't hold (the hands, the water, the doors and signs, the gears turned by the puzzle's angle). Where a Gym's piece stands over the dark, the room's tile is void (`V`) at the piece's walking height: a room draws no ground and no faces of its own there, and a floor's face beside it runs down to the room's lowest ground (`MapScene.AddFaces`); the Electric hall's floors stand on faces of steel (`SceneTextures.PowerFace`). A Leader's script marks the Gym's trainers beaten with `defeat`.
- **The League's rooms** (plan 01 · M9, part 2d; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "The League's rooms"): twelve rooms on the original's plans behind the door of the League's Pokémon Center (`PokemonLeagueNorthPokemonCenter`, itself rebuilt to the original's plan), each leading on by one door and none back. They are halls as the Gyms are: the Four's rooms take their look from `arenaType`, a room with no type from the map file's `hall` (`HallStyle`). A passage's, and the Champion's room's, `puzzle` is a platform lift (`Overworld/LeagueLift.cs`, GPU-free, the original's `platform_lift.c`: three tiles by two, up once at 3.75 tiles a second, never down; `platformlift` and `platformlift check` in a script; `GameEngine.PlayLeagueLift` carries the player while the field waits; laid out by the height the player stands at, so `ArrivePuzzle` asks the player's own). A door that shuts is a thing of the map (`npcType` `LeagueDoor`, `NPC.IsLeagueDoor`) behind its flag, drawn by `GymPieces` in its wall: shut or open in the back wall, a gate across the front wall's kerb. The Pokémon Center's `OnEnter` puts every door and `FLAG_DEFEATED_<WHO>` back, so the rooms' own arrival scripts go by the door's flag, never by a local variable (a save made in a room would lose it). Cynthia's room, the hallway and the Hall of Fame are each one `OnEnter` scene (the Hall of Fame's is plan 02 · S14's: "The Hall of Fame's end", below). `LeagueArt` paints the rooms' pieces; the faces their floors stand on are `SceneTextures.LeagueFace` (warmed with the rest), chosen by `MapScene.HallFace`.
- **Warp panels and Team Galactic's headquarters** (plan 02 · S11, part b; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "The eighth chapter, second half"): `Overworld/WarpPanels.cs` (GPU-free) is the original's rule for a tile of the behaviour `WarpPanel` with a warp on it: whoever ends a step on one is carried to the tile its warp names, which is the far panel, and stands there facing as they did (`WarpPanels.Arrival`; a door or a flight of stairs turns its traveller, a panel doesn't), turning on the spot before the picture closes and after it opens (`Spin`; `GameEngine.RideThePanel` holds the field meanwhile, `HandleWarp` starts it). Arriving is no step, so the far panel carries nobody back until they step off it and on again, and a pad with a panel's look and no behaviour carries nobody. The headquarters are nine rooms on the original's plans (`GalacticHQ1F` to `4F`, `B1F`, `B2F`, `Hall`, `Laboratory`, `ControlRoom`; `interior` `Lab`), each cut into parts that only stairs and panels join, entered from the warehouse once Looker has unlocked its door. A shutter is a thing of the map (`npcType` `GalacticDoor`, `NPC.GalacticDoorType`: two leaves, drawn as cards by `ThingCards`) behind its floor's flag, which the Galactic Key opens for good; the panels, the tanks and the doorway are `PropType.WarpPanel`, `WarpPanelOut` and `Tank` and a warp on the row behind a room's floor, all painted by `Graphics/GalacticArt.cs` (style guide, "Team Galactic's rooms"). The building's own flag, `FLAG_HIDE_GALACTIC_HQ_TEAM_GALACTIC`, hides every member on its floors and the fourth floor's bollards once the control room's button has been pressed.
- **Spear Pillar and the Distortion World** (plan 02 · S12; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "The ninth chapter"): the Distortion World's scenes are scripts, a file to a floor, where the original runs them from its own code, and all go by its one count, `VAR_DISTORTION_WORLD_PROGRESS` (0 to 14; `distortion_world_1f.txt` says what each value is): a floor's `OnEnter` stands its people by it, and they are the overlay's `npcs` at the original's tiles. A **pit** (an overlay's `pits`, `Map.BoulderPits`) is a tile a pushed boulder may always be pushed onto (`FieldMovement.CanPush`) and whose script starts as the boulder comes to rest there, the boulder its `self` (`FieldScripts.PitUnder`, `GameEngine.SlideBoulders`); on a map of nothing the builder makes the tile nothing too, and the boulder is drawn sinking into its mouth (`Relief.InPit`). The puzzle of the three boulders is those scripts and flags of our own (`FLAG_DISTORTION_WORLD_<WHO>_BOULDER_FELL`, `..._BOULDER_IN_PIT`, `FLAG_HIDE_DISTORTION_WORLD_B6F_<WHO>_BOULDER`); a boulder at rest in its guardian's pit is a boulder of its own behind `shownBy`. A tile read where nothing stands is an overlay's `readAt` (`Map.TileScripts`). Someone the story hangs where nobody walks has a `level` (`NPC.Level`): spoken to from beside them at that height (`TryInteract` asks at the footing ahead and then at the player's own), drawn there over nothing and on the rim over a rim (`Relief.Under`). `seen "Dialga"` tells the Pokédex of a Pokémon a scene shows, and `wildbattle` takes a form's name (`"Giratina-Origin"`); a Pokémon caught in a form keeps it only as the form's rule lets it (`FormRules.ByHeldItem` in `AfterBattle`). Two things of the map came with it, cards like an obstacle's (`ThingCards`): `Rubble` and `CavePainting` (`NPC.RubbleType`, `CavePaintingType`), the painting a slab on the tile of the way it shuts, tall enough to cover the dark behind it. Cynthia stands before Turnback Cave's mouth at Sendoff Spring until the Hall of Fame (`FLAG_HIDE_SENDOFF_SPRING_CYNTHIA`): she, not a flag of Giratina's, is what keeps the cave shut, so the Hall of Fame's script must go on setting it.
- **The houses' rooms** (plan 01 · M11, part a; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "The rooms of the south-west and the centre"): the houses of Twinleaf Town, Sandgem Town, Jubilife City, Oreburgh City, Floaroma Town, Eterna City, Hearthome City and Solaceon Town and of the routes between, the flower shop, the herb shop, the Pokémon News Press, the Berry Master's house, the Foreign Building and the Old Chateau's ten rooms are map files on the original's plans (`interior` `House`; `docs/data-files.md`, "A house on the original's plan"), each with a script file of its own where its people do more than talk. They were laid out from the decompilation's land data and events by a tool kept outside the repository and finished by hand (the plan's "Ready from M11a" has its rules, for the parts to come): the original's tiles are kept, so its people stand where its events put them (`RoomPlanTests`), a seat is a `PropType.Cushion` one walks onto, a flight of stairs stands beside its warp, and a lift is a warp in the back wall on a tile of the behaviour `Door`. A script's die is `random N` (the original's `GetRandom`; `IScriptHost.Random`, on `Dice` in the game and on `HeadlessScriptHost.Rolls` in a test): the flower shop's berry of the day, the Berry Master's, and the Old Chateau's two ghosts, each shown by its room's `OnEnter` on the original's roll and hidden by its flag the rest of the time. The Old Chateau's wild Pokémon live on its floor's own behaviour (`OldChateauFloor`); Rotom's television is a `Television` prop on (11, 3) and (12, 3) of `OldChateauBackMiddleWestRoom`, read by the room's `TV` script since plan 02 · S15, part c (below). In a house `MapScene` draws a way through the back wall as a doorway framed in wood or as a lift's doors (`PropModels.PaintDoorway`, `PaintLiftDoors`; a `Lab` keeps Team Galactic's), side stairs of wood, a block of wall that reaches the back wall at the back wall's height (`MapScene.BackWallJog`: a staircase's flank; a partition with floor on both sides stays cut to the wainscot) and an upper level on a panelled face (`SceneTextures.RoomFace`, through `HallFace`). `InteriorTests` holds the doors, the floors joined both ways, every gift once and the flower shop's prices.
- **The lakes' guardians and the Vista Lighthouse** (plan 02 · S13; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "The tenth chapter"): the three caverns (`VerityCavern`, `ValorCavern`, `AcuityCavern`) are maps of the imported world on the islands of their lakes, and their guardians Pokémon of the map behind the original's flags, which the scene in the professor's lab clears (`RowanLab.txt`: it is that scene, too, that opens the road to Sunyshore City) and the Hall of Fame's script clears again for whichever wasn't caught. Azelf and Uxie are `wildbattle`s that may be run from; Mesprit is never battled there: its script sets it roaming (`roamer start`; `VAR_ROAMING_MESPRIT_STATE` says what became of it). A cavern's floor is the original's wet, mirroring stone, which is no cave floor to the field, so its overlay names the battle's stage (`battleArena`), or a battle there is fought in a meadow. The lighthouse is two rooms on the original's plans: `VistaLighthouseElevator`, the lift's car, whose own `OnEnter` walks its rider in, rides and warps them to the other end (`VAR_VISTA_LIGHTHOUSE_FLOOR`, a variable of our own that Sunyshore City's arrival script and the gallery's each set, says which), and `VistaLighthouse`, the gallery, whose shaft is an inner wall with the car standing open in its south face (`PropType.LiftCar`: a plate on an open tile that is a warp; `LiftIndicator` hangs in the car's own room). `WorldWalk` follows a place's own arrival script as it follows a person's, which is how a walk of the whole game finds the gallery.
- **The rooms of the east and the sea** (plan 01 · M11, part b; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "The rooms of the east and the sea"): twenty-seven more map files made the same way, from the Café Cabin and Veilstone's houses to the Hotel Grand Lake's lobby and cottages, the Seven Stars Restaurant, Pastoria's houses and the Great Marsh's observatory on two floors, the Pokémon Mansion (its hall, the maids' three rooms, Mr. Backlot's office), Route 221's house, Canalave's houses and Iron Island's cabin. What their people ask of the team is one script command, `team` (`docs/scripts.md`, "What a room's people ask of the team": `befriend`, `level`, `combee`, `condition`, `massage`), beside two built-in variables, `TURNS` (the turns of the script's battles as the original counts them: `IScriptHost.BattleTurns`, which the engine takes from `BattleCore.Turn` as a battle ends) and `SAFARI_CAUGHT` (`SafariGame.Caught`, saved), and the placeholder `{marsh:N}`. **The Safari Game starts at the observatory's counter** (`PastoriaCityObservatoryGate1F`'s trigger `Counter`, before the attendant) and ends there: the room's `OnEnter` asks whoever comes back out of the marsh with a game on, `common.SafariTimeUp` and `SafariOutOfBalls` bring the player to the same tile (`VAR_SAFARI_GAME_STATE` 2), and `great_marsh_6.OnEnter` shows back whoever is in the marsh with no game on. The restaurant seats five of its nine couples a day (`SevenStarsRestaurant.OnEnter`, by `random 9`), each a pair of the map's trainers battled with `battle a and b`; a trainer battled again once beaten gets their team afresh (`ScriptRunner.Refresh`). The mansion's maids are one person of the map battled five times as five trainers (`battle … as "maid_…"`), in the day's number of turns. Someone behind a table is spoken to across it where the room's `behaviours` give the table's tile the original's `Counter`. In a house `MapScene` also draws a doorway for a warp on a wall that has come forward (the mansion's corridors: the warp's own tile no longer unmakes `BackWallJog`), and nothing where a house's plan has nothing (style guide, "Rooms"): void tiles (`V`) beyond the wall round the floor, the wall before them cut to the front wall's kerb with no floor under it, the side walls ending where the void begins. `EastInteriorTests` holds the doors, the gifts, each rule above and what is still shut.
- **The Hall of Fame's end** (plan 02 · S14; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "The eleventh chapter"): the room's scene is one script (`PokemonLeagueHallOfFame.txt`), in the original's order: the words, the balls on the machine (`anim halloffame`, the machine's own set piece since plan 09 · L12: `GameEngine.KeepTheMachinesBalls` hands what it has laid to `Map.MachineBalls` and `MachineLit`, and `GymPieces` draws them in the machine's sockets), `Victory` (the original's `SetHallOfFameVictoryFlagsAndVars`: it sets flags and variables and nothing else, because `StoryMigration` runs it with no screen), `halloffame`, the scene of the team's entry, `heal`, a `warp` to the player's room, `save` and `credits`. The script is still running in the room it warped to, so that room's own `OnEnter` waits for its end; and the screen stays dark from its `fade out` until then, whatever opens and closes over it. `HallOfFameCeremony` (`GameState.HallOfFameCeremony`, `halloffame show`) and `CreditsScreen` (`GameState.Credits`, `credits`) are functions of time and one button like the evolution and hatching scenes (`Advance`, `PressConfirm`; the credits also `Held`), their 3D figures rendered in `Render` before the 2D pass and drawn by `ModernUi.Ceremony.cs` (style guide, "The Hall of Fame's ceremony" and "The credits"); both close to black by themselves, and the engine goes back to the field without a fade of its own. The credits' words are `Data/credits.json` (`CreditsDatabase`; `CreditsRoll.Lay` is the column, GPU-free: whoever can measure text hands it a wrapper), and a test holds them to the sources `docs/art/CREDITS.md` and `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` name. `if nationaldex` asks `Pokedex.NationalUnlocked` (`IScriptHost.NationalDex`), and `save` writes the game where the player stands (`IScriptHost.Save`). A flag a script asks about before anything sets it is a failed test (`StoryTests.AFlagOrAVariableAskedAboutIsSetSomewhere`) unless `StoryTests.AskedAheadOfTheirScenes` names it, and a flag named there that something sets fails too: the session that writes the scene takes it off the list.
- **The rooms of the north and of the Battle Zone** (plan 01 · M11, part c; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "The rooms of the north and of the Battle Zone"): twenty-one more map files made the same way: Celestic Town's shop (its north-west house: the town's two counters) and two houses, the Snowbound Lodge and Route 217's two houses, Route 222's fisherman and the Pikachu Fan Club, Sunyshore City's three houses, the Fight Area's two, the houses of Routes 225 to 228, of the Survival Area and of the Resort Area, and the Ribbon Syndicate's lift and spa. Three more words of `team` (`first`, `nature`, `size`; `docs/scripts.md`), the last for **the size of a Pokémon** (`Models/SizeContest.cs`, GPU-free, the original's `overlay005/size_contest.c`: a factor out of the personality's low half and the IVs' low four bits, bent into a bell by the original's table; the record is the story's `VAR_SIZE_CONTEST_RECORD`, and a record never kept reads as the original's first, so no save needs it set; `{size}` and `{record}` say them in centimetres). **A lift with an attendant** (the Syndicate's): the lift's doors in each floor's back wall lead into the car (`ResortAreaRibbonSyndicateElevator`), whose own door is two ways on one tile, to the ground floor `until` `FLAG_RIBBON_SYNDICATE_LIFT_UPSTAIRS` and to the floor above `from` it: a room's map file may give a warp `until` and `from` as an overlay's `switches` do, and the attendant's ride sets or clears the flag. `PropType` gained `BookPile` at its end (the original's `book01` on a blocked tile; it blocks nothing by itself). Someone of a room drawn as a Pokémon who is a trainer (the Pikachu Fan Club's Poké Kid) names `"script": "Trainer"`, or talking to her would run the Pokémon's cry. The three move tutors' houses (Snowpoint City's east, the Survival Area's north, Route 228's north) stay shut for part d. And one rule of the builder's came with the doors: **a house that shuts a pocket in is one block** (`WorldMapBuilder.PlaceModels`; `PocketTiles`, `HouseWidth`, `HouseDepth`). The original leaves a tile or two open in the middle of some houses, shut in by their own walls; cut into rectangles round the pocket, Route 225's house was a small block, a ring of fence and a door under the fence, and the Survival Area's south house and its Battleground two halves with a gap between. A building no bigger than a house whose blocked tiles shut such a pocket in is the box of the tiles it blocks. A house whose door has been shut may never have been seen whole: look at it from outside as the door is opened (`battlezone Sinnoh,x,y`). `NorthInteriorTests` holds the doors, the gifts, each rule above and what is still shut.
- **After the Hall of Fame** (plan 02 · S15, part a; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "After the Hall of Fame, part a"): `nationaldex` in a script makes the Pokédex the National one (`IScriptHost.GiveNationalDex`, `Pokedex.UnlockNational`); the lab's script asks first what `Pokedex.CanUnlockNational` asks (the Hall of Fame entered and all 210 of Sinnoh seen: plan 03's decision 5, where the original asks only that Eterna City has been reached), and a test holds the two together. Whoever the original keeps for the National Pokédex asks `if nationaldex`, whoever it keeps for a Champion `flag FLAG_GAME_COMPLETED`, and some ask both (Victory Road's collector, the assistant's sister). The Fight Area's two blockades (`FLAG_HIDE_FIGHT_AREA_BLOCKADE`: a pair across the way north, a pair on Route 230's shore) go once the tag battle before the Frontier's gate is won **and** the National Pokédex is had, whichever comes second: each of the two scenes asks for the other. A Gym Leader a scene battles is named by the script (`battle volkner as "leader_volkner_fight_area" and flint with "<the rival's team>"`), never made a trainer of the map, whose class would want an eye theme (`MusicTests`). Someone who is on a map only for a scene and comes in by its door has the door's own tile for theirs (Professor Oak in the lab, as the rival at the League): the tile a warp puts the player on must be nobody's, with everyone present. `LEAGUE_VICTORIES` is one of the game's own variables (`IScriptHost.LeagueVictories`), and `Driver.PartnerAlong` tells a tool who travels with the player. `common.ChapterTwelve` hides parts b and c's people too, so those sessions need no story version to hide them. `WorldWalk.StoryArrivals` is down to the two islands and Snowpoint Temple's hall (part b).
- **The gate houses and the last rooms** (plan 01 · M11, part d; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "The gate houses, the tutors, the Department Store, the Pokémon Centers' floors and the Global Terminal"): no gate house is walked through any more. Fifteen more are rooms on the original's five plans (seven on the plan of the gate to Route 209, three on a road that runs north and south, the Cycling Road's two, Hearthome's two to Amity Square, and the hall between the Fight Area and the Battle Frontier), entered by `doors` of the overlays and left by warps of their own; an overlay's `through` and `WorldMapBuilder.Join`'s branch for it are gone, and a test that asks what lies beyond a gate walks its room (`GateWalk` in the test project). **The Cycling Road's gates** are the only rooms the Bicycle is ridden through (a map file's `bike`, `Map.BikeAllowed`); their doors onto the road are `cyclistsOnly`, a row of each room's lane turns back anyone on foot (the rooms' own triggers, which ask the built-in variable `CYCLING`, `IScriptHost.Cycling`), and from that row to the door a rider can't get off (`BicycleRules.ForcedInGateFlag`, the original's `FLAG_FORCE_BIKING_IN_GATE`, which goes with the next warp). **The move tutors** teach: `Models/MoveTutors.cs` (GPU-free) is the original's table of the three shard tutors' moves and prices, the two tutors of one move each (Draco Meteor for a Dragon type, the starters' three strongest moves for the twelve of them fully grown, both at full friendship) and the Move Deleter's rule, and the script command `tutor` (`Story/ScriptRunner.Tutors.cs`; `docs/scripts.md`, "The move tutors and the Move Deleter") is how their rooms reach it, through `MoveTeaching.Learn` as a TM does. The Move Relearner and the Name Rater say what they do and change nothing (plan 12 · Q19, plan 13 · V4). **Veilstone's Department Store** is six floors and a lift's car (`VeilstoneStore1F` to `5F`, `B1F`, `Elevator`; `interior` `PokeMart`): its counters sell `marts.json`'s specialties, its fourth floor the Underground's goods for money, its roof's `PropType.VendingMachine` three drinks, its basement Poffins (`poffin give`) and Lava Cookies; its floors are joined by escalators (a warp with its flight beside it, `SideStairsUp` and `SideStairsDown`, in the middle of each floor), and the car's door is six ways on one tile, each `from` a flag the floor's own arrival script sets. **Every Pokémon Center has a floor above and a floor below** (`<Center>2F`, `<Center>B1F`: the original's two plans, a map of each to a Center as the original has a header of each to a town), reached by escalators where the hand-made ground floors had their benches; their attendants and the three floors of the **Global Terminal** (`GlobalTerminal1F` to `3F`, joined by warp panels) say that the wireless link is down (decision 06 · 7). **Pal Park's viewing gallery**, the lobby's own upper level in the original, is a map of its own (`PalParkGallery`), come to by the lobby's escalator. A stairwell one tile wide (the Centers' way down) leaves the shade lines off its treads, which would be single texels there (`PropModels.SideStairsDown`).
- **The hand-made rooms rebuilt** (plan 01 · M11, the last rooms; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "The hand-made rooms of the first chapters"): the rooms made by hand before the world was imported are laid out again on the original's plans, each only once everything that stood on its tiles was listed and carried across. **Every Pokémon Center's ground floor is one plan** (`pokecenter_1f`, 17 by 14: the nurse at (8, 4) behind her counter, the PC's own tile at (12, 3), the escalators in the south wall's alcoves at (2, 10) and (14, 10), the door's mat at (8, 12)) and **every Poké Mart another** (`mart`, 12 by 13: the clerks at (3, 5) and (2, 5), the mat at (3, 11)), each town's a copy with its own people on the original's tiles; so are the Trainers' School, the Pokétch Company on three floors and with two doors, Jubilife TV's lobby (its stairs and lift roped off until plan 08 · P8 builds the floors) and the rival's house. Nothing names a tile of these rooms but their own files, the towns' overlays and the tests: the white-out finds its tile from the nurse (two before her) and the harness its spots from the people. Two wall props came with them at the end of `PropType`, on the wall's row like a painting and blocking nothing: `WallShelf` (a Mart's back wall) and `Blackboard`. **A save made in a room laid out again wakes inside its door**: `SaveData.PlaceOn(maps)`, which `ApplySaveData` asks in place of `Place`, puts a save older than its room's rebuilding (`SaveData.RebuiltAfter`: the rooms listed under each `WorldVersion`, `RebuiltRooms` being 3), or one standing on a tile of any room where nobody can stand, on the tile the room is come into by (`SaveData.ArrivalIn`: where the way back in from the room's first way out leads, so the mat inside a door or the head of a flight of stairs). A room rebuilt later is added to that list under a new version, with its tests in `RebuiltRoomTests`.
- **The player's house and the professor's lab** (plan 01 · M11, the last step; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "The player's house and Professor Rowan's lab"): the last two hand-made places are on the original's plans too (`PlayerHouse` 13 by 12, `PlayerHouse2F` 12 by 12, `RowanLab` 22 by 17; `WorldVersion` 4, `SaveData.RebuiltHome`), and their scenes walk on the original's tiles by the original's movements. **A new game begins on 4, 6 of the bedroom** (`RegionDatabase`'s `Start`, the original's `sPlayerStartLocation`), with the original's four triggers round it; the rival's scene there, the rival's mother's visit downstairs and Professor Oak's in the lab each go the original's four ways, told apart by `PLAYER_X` and `PLAYER_Y` (a script that turns the player loses `facing`). Mom sits on her cushion before the television at 7, 8, and **a white-out at home wakes on the tile east of whoever heals** (`GameEngine.HomeSpot`: 8, 8, the original's spawn tile). Someone who comes in for a scene by a way the player uses stands on the way's own tile, never on the tile it puts the player on: the rival's mother on the door's tile, the rival at the head of the bedroom's stairs. **The room a region's game begins in is come into by the tile it begins on** (`SaveData.PlaceOn`): a save moved in the bedroom wakes on the start tile, not beside the stairs, where a game saved before its first step could walk round the rival's scene. **A laboratory's room whose plan is no rectangle has nothing beyond its walls**, as a house has (`MapScene.OutsideThePlan`; style guide, "Rooms"): void that the room's own walls shut off from its floor is outside the plan, and void the floor runs up to is a well inside the room, drawn as it was (Pal Park's gallery). What is still made by hand in Sinnoh is the Jubilife boutique (plan 11 · C10) and nothing else.
- **After the Hall of Fame, part b** (plan 02 · S15, part b; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "After the Hall of Fame, part b"): Stark Mountain, Snowpoint Temple, the sailor's son and the three legendary Pokémon met there are scripts in the language as it stood. **A legendary Pokémon's room decides for itself**: Heatran is hidden or shown by its room's `OnEnter` on the original's whole condition (`stark_mountain_room_3.HeatranOrNot`: not caught, a Champion, the National Pokédex, the room's state 1 and `FLAG_TALKED_TO_BATTLEGROUND_BUCK`, which Buck sets in the Battleground: part c, below), Regigigas wakes only with the three Regis in the team (`if not has`), and both come back with the Hall of Fame's `Victory` unless caught; Cresselia is never battled where it stands: its script sets it roaming and clears the flag that hides the Lunar Wing's ball. **A ball a scene reveals is shown by its flag alone** (`clearflag`): a `show` would hold it on the map after it is picked up, until the place is come to again. **A thing of the story that looks like an obstacle** (the Magma Stone, which the original draws as a Strength boulder, and the cracked rock that is Looker) is not laid out from the area file, where it would be pushed or smashed: its object is `heldBack` and the overlay's `npcs` stand a `MagmaStone` or a `FalseRock` on its tile (`NPC.MagmaStoneType`, `FalseRockType`; cards by `ThingCards`, `OutdoorProps.PaintMagmaStone`), in the way like a person and cleared by no field move. **Someone lying on furniture** is a second person of the room on the furniture's own tile with a `level` (the sailor's boy in his bed, behind a flag of ours that the room's `OnEnter` keeps to the original's variable); the headless host takes a `level` for standing on something, and a room without relief draws him inside the furniture's box. Snowpoint Temple's west door is open (the trigger before it and Candice's scene keep it, `VAR_SNOWPOINT_CITY_STATE`); its east door, before which the guard stands for good, stays `locked`, as do the three Regis' ruins and the Harbor Inn (the rulings say what would open each). The League's five battle as `<id>_rematch` once `FLAG_ARRESTED_CHARON_STARK_MOUNTAIN` is set. `WorldWalk.StoryArrivals` is down to Newmoon Island's landing: the walk of the whole game finds Fullmoon Island by Canalave's sailor and the temple by its door.
- **After the Hall of Fame, part c** (plan 02 · S15, part c; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "After the Hall of Fame, part c"): the last of Platinum's story is scripts in the language as it stood but for two words. **The Battleground** (`Battleground`, the Survival Area's door 0, shut until `FLAG_ARRESTED_CHARON_STARK_MOUNTAIN` by the overlay's `openedBy`) is a room on the original's plan whose **guests change with the day**: thirteen people of the room (the eight Gym Leaders and the five who battled beside the player), each shown by a flag of the room's own (`FLAG_BATTLEGROUND_HAS_<WHO>`), of whom the room's `OnEnter` shows up to four and puts each on the original's place, by a draw kept in `VAR_BATTLEGROUND_TRAINER_1` to `_4` and made once a day as the original's `GetRandomBattlegroundTrainers` makes it (`random`, and `setvar VAR_X RESULT`, which copies a variable or one of the game's own). Nobody there is a trainer of the map: each is battled as a team of Platinum's data (`battle self as "leader_roark_rematch"`), counted in `VAR_TIMES_BATTLED_AT_BATTLEGROUND` (the Villa's catalogue reads it) and beaten for the day by `FLAG_DAILY_DEFEATED_BATTLEGROUND_<WHO>`. Buck, spoken to inside, sets `FLAG_TALKED_TO_BATTLEGROUND_BUCK` (Heatran's room asks it) and `FLAG_BUCK_LEFT_BATTLEGROUND` (the Battle Salon's). **Rotom** is the `TV` script of `OldChateauBackMiddleWestRoom`, read on the set's two tiles: a Rotom of level 20 after dark, once a day until caught. **Mt. Coronet's way out onto its summit is four ways on one tile**: its sixth floor's `OnEnter` decides as the original's does (the torn pillar; Dialga's pillar with the Adamant Orb in the bag or held, which `team holds "Adamant Orb"` asks; Palkia's with the Lustrous Orb; the pillar as it stands) and sets one of two flags of our own, and `Map.GetWarpAt` lets a way that is there `from` a flag take the tile from one that is only there `until` another. `SpearPillarDialga` and `SpearPillarPalkia` are maps of the imported world with one rift each, read from below (`Rift`): level 70, quiet once battled until the Hall of Fame's `Victory` puts the state back, gone for good once caught. The elder's book (`CelesticTownNorthHouse.Elder`, for a Champion) sets what the mountain asks, and Cynthia's talk before the painting (`celestic_town_cave.CynthiaReads`, once Charon is caught) is heard once for each entry into the Hall of Fame. `StoryTests.AskedAheadOfTheirScenes` is empty, and a test holds every flag the original's new game sets against what `common.NewGame` sets. Plan 02's last section, "What the story still owes", lists what of Platinum's story is not in the game, each with the plan and session that owns it.
- **Poffins and the contest condition** (plan 06 · R14c; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "Poffins and the contest condition"): every Pokémon has the original's condition (`Pokemon.Cool`, `Beauty`, `Cute`, `Smart`, `Tough`, `Sheen`, 0 to 255; saved, copied by `CopyStateFrom`), which Feebas's evolution reads and the summary shows on a page of its own once the Contest Hall has been visited (`GameEngine.ContestHallVisitedFlag`; `PartyScreen.ShowCondition`, `TurnSummaryPage`). The rules are GPU-free: `Models/Poffins.cs` (a `Poffin`, its kind as `Poffin_MakePoffin` decides it, its level and name, the natures' tastes and `Feed`, by the original's single-precision arithmetic), `Models/PoffinCase.cs` (a hundred, saved as `SaveData.Poffins`) and `Models/PoffinPot.cs`, the pot frame by frame at thirty a second in the console's fixed point (`PotMath`), for one to four cooks: the stroke, push and drag, the turns, burning and spilling, the stages, the arrow, stirring together, and `Result`, `ov83_0223FFD4`'s Poffin. A `PotTouch` is what one cook's stylus did that frame (`PotStylus` turns a stylus into one); `PoffinCookingScreen` (`GameState.PoffinCooking`, opened by `poffin cook`) stirs with a spoon the left and right keys turn (`Turn`, `Held` for a tool, `Touch` for a stylus of its own), choosing each berry through the bag (`BagScreen.OpenToPick("berries")`), and `PoffinCaseScreen` (`GameState.PoffinCase`, the bag's OPEN on the Poffin Case or the item button) lists, throws away and feeds them; both keep their logic in methods that take no input, drawn by `ModernUi.Poffins.cs`. The Poffin House and the Pokémon Fan Club are rooms on the original's plans (`PoffinHouse`, `HearthomeFanClub`; Hearthome's doors 10 and 12), and the cooking tables a prop of their own (`PropType.CookingTable`).
- **The Super Contests** (plan 06 · R17a; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "Super Contests"): the rules are GPU-free in `Models/SuperContests/`, ported from the original's `src/contest.c`, `src/unk_02094EDC.c` and `overlay017`: `ContestRules` (the tables that hold whoever is on stage: what an effect is worth, the voltage, the hearts' steps, the condition's score, `WhyNot`, the ribbons' names, the prizes), `SuperContest` (the theme, the judges and the three opponents drawn in the original's order, the Visual Competition, each round's shares, the board's bars and the places), `SuperContestDance` (a frame each thirtieth of a second, as `PoffinPot`: `Press` a step for the frame to come, `Advance` a frame; the opponents dance by generators of their own seeded from the contest's), `SuperContestActing` (`Choose` gives the opponents' choices by the original's table, `ActingAiTable`; `Perform` plays a whole turn at once and returns a log of `ActingEvent`s for whoever shows it, as a battle's core does) and `FashionCase` (the accessories, saved as `SaveData.FashionCase`). Every draw is a 16-bit one from the `Random` the contest is given (`SuperContest.Next16`), in the original's order: nothing there may draw from anywhere else. A move on stage is `MoveData.Contest` (`MoveContest`: its quality and its effect; Platinum's 467, from the importer; null for later moves; plan 21 · H11 adds Hoenn's beside it), and the tables are `Data/contests.json` (`SuperContestDatabase`: the contestants, judges, dressings, themes and accessories of the original's `contest_data.narc`, generated by `tools/DataImporter`, never edited by hand).
