A Pokémon's ribbons are `Pokemon.Ribbons`, a set of the original's names (`RIBBON_COOL_GREAT`; saved, and copied by `CopyStateFrom`); only a Super Contest gives any yet (R17b). `SuperContestScreen` (`GameState.SuperContest`, opened by `supercontest enter`, which the script waits for) is the rules' clock and buttons: its logic is `PressConfirm`, `PressCancel`, `PressStart`, `Move`, `PressStep` and `Advance`, drawn by `ModernUi.SuperContest.cs`; it keeps what the stage shows apart from the rules, which are a turn ahead while a turn of acting plays (`ShownTurn`, `Usable`, `ShownVoltage`). What a contest leaves is `ContestAwards.Conclude` (the ribbon, the prize into the Fashion Case, the friendship), which both script hosts call; the engine adds the Trainer Card's score, the Pokédex and the Master Rank's flag (`GameEngine.SuperContests.cs`). The Contest Hall's receptionists are scripts of `ContestHallLobby.txt`: every way through a script is played by `StoryTests`, where a question asked a second time is answered with its first answer, so a menu must never lead back to a question whose first answer leads back to it (the explanations end the talk, and "practise again?" has NO first).
- **Ball Capsules, Seals and ribbons** (plan 06 · R17b; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "Ball Capsules, Seals and ribbons"): the rules are GPU-free. `Models/SealCase.cs` is the original's `ball_seal_info.c` and capsule screen: a `BallSeal` (a kind and where it is stuck, in the original's dots round the ball's middle at 190, 70), a `BallCapsule` (up to eight, immutable), the `SealCase` (eighty kinds, ninety-nine of a kind counted with those stuck on capsules, twelve capsules; saved as `SaveData.SealCase`), a `CapsuleEdit` (a capsule being changed: a Seal taken, lifted, moved, dropped on the ball or back into the case, kept or given up) and `CapsuleRules` (attach, detach, swap, keep, the burst's delay and place, what an Unown and the case's giver hand over). A Pokémon's capsule is `Pokemon.CapsuleId` (the capsule's number from 1) and `Pokemon.Capsule` (its own copy of the Seals, which a `Keep` renews); both saved and in `CopyStateFrom`, and a trainer's Pokémon gets the one `trainers.json`'s `ballSeal` names (`SealDatabase.TrainerCapsule`). A battle's rules never read either: the log's `Entered` carries the Pokémon, and `BattleEngine.Show` starts `BattleAnimator.SealBurst` (`CueKind.Seals`, `EffectCue.Capsule`) beside the send-out, which `Graphics/SealFx.cs` turns into pieces as a function of the capsule, the cue's age and its seed (`SealFx.Motes` is the flat list the capsules' screen draws too; a Seal's kind is its `SealFamily`). Something new a Seal shows is a case there, never a draw from the battle's generator. The ribbons are `Data/ribbons.json` (`RibbonDatabase`: Platinum's eighty in its order) and `Models/Ribbons.cs` (`RibbonRules`: what each is for and whether anything gives it yet, `WaitsFor`; the day's ribbon, the Effort and Footprint Ribbons' conditions, the Syndicate's offer, the Champion Ribbon, which the script's `halloffame` gives). Seals and backdrops are data too (`Data/seals.json`, `SealDatabase`; `backdrops` in `contests.json`), all from `tools/DataImporter` (`Importer.Capsules.cs`), never edited by hand. `ContestRecords` (`SaveData.ContestRecords`) counts the contests entered and won and keeps the winner's photograph for each of the five frames; `FashionCase` holds the backdrops (`SaveData.Backdrops`). The screens keep their logic in methods that take no input: `SealCaseScreen` (`GameState.SealCase`; the bag's OPEN on the Seal Case, a PC's BALL CAPSULES, `seal capsules`), `SealShopScreen` (`GameState.SealShop`, `seal shop`), `FashionCaseScreen` (`GameState.FashionCase`; the bag's OPEN on the Fashion Case, and a photograph through `supercontest photo`) and the summary's third page (`PartyScreen.RibbonsPage`, `SelectRibbons`, `MoveRibbon`), drawn by `ModernUi.Capsules.cs` and `ModernUi.Ribbons.cs`. A Pokémon with a capsule doesn't go into a box (`PcBoxes.WhyNotDeposit`). The Ribbon Syndicate's ground floor is a room on the original's plan (`ResortAreaRibbonSyndicate1F`, plan 01 · M10, part b): its `OnEnter` calls `common.RibbonSyndicateEntry` and shows out whoever it refuses, and its cashier runs `common.RibbonSyndicateCashier`; the lift and the spa upstairs are plan 01 · M11, part c's (below). The Fashion Case is owed to a save past Jubilife City's scene with Team Galactic as it loads (`GameEngine.OweTheFashionCase`), with no story version of its own.
- **The Underground** (plan 06 · R16a; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "The Underground"): the rules are GPU-free in `Models/Underground/`, ported from the original's `src/underground/`: `UndergroundState` (the save's own, `SaveData.Underground`: the bag below ground, the base and its PC, the buried Spheres, the day's walls and traps, the records; `Rand16` and `Rand32` are the console kit's generators, and `DaysPass`, which `Models/DailyEvents.cs` calls, grows the Spheres and moves the day's number on), `MiningGame` (the wall: what is set in it by the original's weights, the dirt, the pick's and the hammer's blows, the wall's strength, what lies bare), `UndergroundField` (where digging down lands, `Below`; the day's spread, `SpreadIfDue`; burying, digging up, setting and stepping; the radar's `Pulse`), `TrapEffect` (a trap at work frame by frame at thirty a second, told what the keys did), `SecretBaseRules` (the drill, the room, the flags' ranks, `Decorating` and `Keep`), `UndergroundVendors` (the hikers' day's wares and prices) and `Diggers`, the single-player stand-in for other players' flags (up to seven bases a day drawn from the day's number). Every draw there is on the generator the rule is handed. The tables are `Data/underground.json` (`UndergroundDatabase`, from `tools/DataImporter`'s `Importer.Underground.cs`, never edited by hand). The map is `Underground`, matrix 2 of the imported world, a cave no warp leads into: its `world.json` entry names the tiles it is come to by (`arrivals`, `arrivalsInEveryChunk`: its tunnels are six systems that don't join), its hikers stand by the overlay's `"at"` on the original's table's tiles, and `WorldWalk` digs down from every chunk of Sinnoh it walks. `GameEngine.Underground.cs` is where the rules meet the map: the Explorer Kit (`UseExplorerKit`: down above ground, the bag below), `ArriveUnderground` (the spread, the day's diggers, the ways into the bases as `CaveMouth` tiles, the base's room laid out as props whose models are `ug:` names, which `UndergroundArt` builds; a map changed while it is shown is rebuilt with `WorldRenderer.Forget`), `UndergroundStep` from `OnStep`, `TryInteractUnderground`, the radar on the Run button, and `UpdateTrap`, which holds the field while a trap has the player. The screens keep their logic in methods that take no input: `MiningScreen` (`GameState.Mining`), `UndergroundScreen` (`GameState.Underground`: the bag below ground, a hiker's trade, a base's PC; what it asks of the tunnels comes back as a request, `TakeRequest`) and `DecorationScreen` (`GameState.Decoration`), drawn by `ModernUi.Underground.cs`. **Decorating is a seam for other rooms** (plan 21 · H10, Hoenn's secret bases): `Models/Decorating.cs` knows nothing of the Underground (a `DecorationRoom` of `RoomPiece`s with its own rules as functions, a store of `StoredDecoration`s, a `DecoratingSession` of the four things done), and `DecorationScreen.Open` takes any session, a title and how a piece looks on the plan. Scripts reach all of it through `underground …` (`docs/scripts.md`, "The Underground"); the game itself starts `Wall`, `BasePC`, `DiggerPC` and `GoUp` of `underground.txt` (`GameEngine.UndergroundScripts`) and sets the flags the Underground Man's missions wait for (`UndergroundMissionFlags`).
- **Fossils, the Game Corner, the Villa and Pal Park** (plan 06 · R16b; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, under the same name): the rules are GPU-free. `Models/SlotMachine.cs` is the original's `overlay101` frame by frame at thirty a second (`SlotMachine.Update` takes the frame's `SlotKeys`; the draw, the reels' slip, the five lines, the replay, the bonus that waits for three sevens, the bonus game with its pointed order, moons and stamina; a machine's mood is the day's deal, `MoodOf`), with `CoinCase` (50,000 at most) and `GameCornerPrizes`; every draw there is on the `Random` the machine is given. `Models/Pastimes.cs` holds what the save keeps beside the story (`Pastimes`, `SaveData.Pastimes`: the coins, the six Pokémon waiting in Pal Park, and the steps, berries planted, Eggs hatched and Fossils revived the original keeps as game records), `Fossils`, `PalParkState` (the six, the show under way and its clock, `Stock`, which plan 08 · P21's migration calls: with nothing brought over the lobby says so and the day's visitors stand in, and never in place of six that were brought, `IsMigrant`; its `Ledger`, `PalParkLedger`, is the twenty sources and the Pokémon that came) and `VillaRules` (the twenty pieces with their prices, places and the tiles they block, the catalogue's conditions read from a `VillaRecords`, the day's visitor, `NewDay` from `Models/DailyEvents.cs`). The Villa remembers everything in the story's own flags and variables (`FLAG_VILLA_FURNITURE_…`, `VAR_RESORT_VILLA_VISITOR`), so a script can ask them. Scripts reach all of it through `fossil`, `gamecorner`, `villa` and `palpark` (`Story/ScriptRunner.Pastimes.cs`; `docs/scripts.md`), and the hosts through `IScriptHost.Pastimes`, `VillaRecords` and `PastimeAct`. `GameEngine.Pastimes.cs` is where they meet the game: a machine opened (`GameState.SlotMachine`, `SlotMachineScreen`, whose logic is `Press`, `Advance` and `Held`; drawn by `ModernUi.Pastimes.cs`) and closed with the coins back in the case; the Villa's furniture laid out as props as the player comes in or a piece is delivered (`FurnishVilla`: a room whose props change is built again, `WorldRenderer.Forget`), each piece's tiles running its `Look…` script (`VillaLookScript`); the Catching Show's clock, its step in the park (`PalParkStep`, from `OnStep`) and the sixth catch, which starts `pal_park.CaughtAll`. The rooms are on the original's plans (`OreburghMiningMuseum`, `VeilstoneGameCorner`, `VeilstonePrizeExchange`, `VeilstoneSoutheastHouse`, `ResortAreaVilla`, `PalParkLobby`; `interior` `Museum` and `GameHall` are the two new looks), the park is `PalPark`, matrix 49 of the imported world, and their furniture is `Graphics/PastimeArt.cs`'s (twelve props at the end of `PropType`, none of which blocks by itself: the room's `solid` does). A question's answers scroll past eight rows (`ModernUi.DrawChoices`), so a script may ask with a long list.
- **The Battle Frontier's frame and the Battle Tower** (plan 06 · R18a; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, under the same name): the rules are GPU-free in `Models/Frontier/`. `FrontierRecords` (the save's own, `SaveData.Frontier`) is the original's hundred stats in its order (`FrontierStat`), a streak running or not for each facility and way of taking it (`FrontierFacility`, `FrontierChallenge`), the Battle Points, the victories at each facility and the Tower's own record; `FrontierBrains` is each facility's print (the story's `VAR_BATTLE_<FACILITY>_PRINT_STATE`, 0 to 4), when its Brain comes and who that is; `FrontierRules` who may go in (no Egg, none of the eighteen banned, no two of a species or holding the same item) and a Pokémon as a battle takes it (`ForBattle`, `TeamFor`: a copy at level 50, or its own level if lower); `FrontierTrainers` how a trainer's Pokémon are made (IVs by the trainer's number, a set built with its effort shared, a personality drawn for its nature, `ForTrainers`, the draw every facility but the Tower shares). The tables are `Data/frontier.json` (`FrontierDatabase`: the 315 trainers, the 950 sets, the Tower's rows by room, the Exchange Service Corner's prizes, the banlist; from `tools/DataImporter`'s `Importer.Frontier.cs`, never edited by hand). **A Frontier battle** is `BattleSetup.Frontier` (`CoreSetup.Frontier`, `FieldState.Frontier`): no EXP, effort or money, nothing from the Bag (`BattleCore.FrontierBagRefusal`), RUN a question that loses the match (`LearnStep.Forfeit`, on the move-learning question's box), no Pickup, the Pokédex told nothing, every Pokémon obedient, a Soul Dew without effect. The game fights one on a team of copies (`GameEngine.StartFrontierBattle`, `EndFrontierBattle`), so nothing a battle does reaches the player's own Pokémon and a rule that must outlast the battle is the facility's to carry. `BattleTowerChallenge` (`Models/Frontier/BattleTower.cs`) is the Tower: its own generator, seeded from a number kept with the records (a room is the same however often it is come to; a new streak and a new day move the number on), the set's trainers by room with the Tower Tycoon at battles 21 and 49 of the Single Battle Room, each team as it is come to, the five partners of the Battle Salon, the records at a win, a loss, a pause and a walk-out, the Battle Points, ribbons and trophies. Every draw there is on that generator: nothing in a challenge may draw from `Dice`. A challenge lives between scripts as the hosts' `Tower` (the engine's `tower`) and is saved only when put down (`TowerRecord.Paused`). Scripts reach all of it through `frontier …` and `tower …` (`Story/ScriptRunner.Frontier.cs`; `docs/scripts.md`, "The Battle Frontier and the Battle Tower"), and the hosts through `Frontier`, `Tower`, `FrontierAct`, `Entered` and `FrontierBattle`. `GameEngine.Frontier.cs` is where they meet the game: the team opened to be entered (`PartyScreen.OpenToEnter`, whose `Entered` is the order chosen), a facility's board (`GameState.FrontierRecords`, `FrontierRecordsScreen`, `FrontierBoard`; drawn by `ModernUi.Frontier.cs`), the Battle Points over the field while a script speaks of them, whoever stands as `opponent`, `opponent_2` and `partner` in a room taking the look of who was drawn. The Tower's seven rooms are on the original's plans (`BattleTower`, `BattleTowerElevator`, `BattleTowerCorridor`, `BattleTowerBattleRoom`, `BattleTowerCorridorMulti`, `BattleTowerMultiBattleRoom`, `BattleTowerBattleSalon`), each one arrival script that plays its scene and warps on; a room's `OnEnter` puts back whoever a script walked from their post (`place`), since coming in again doesn't. All five facilities are open since R18d, each built on the plan's "Ready from R18a" and what came after it.
- **The Battle Factory** (plan 06 · R18b; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, under the same name): `BattleFactoryChallenge` (`Models/Frontier/BattleFactory.cs`, GPU-free) is the original's `overlay104/battle_factory.c` and its helpers. Nothing of the player's goes in: a rental is a `FactoryRental` (a set of `frontier.json`, the IV of its row, a personality and the number it carries, which is what the original's save keeps; `Build` makes the Pokémon, whole, as often as it is asked), six are offered and three taken (`Offer`, `Team`), a trainer's three are `Foe` in the battle room and `NextFoe` while they are only told of (`Hint`: less with every round), and after a win one in hand may be traded for one just beaten (`Trade`), which comes over as it was. The tables are `frontier.json`'s `factory` (`FrontierDatabase.Factory`: each round's trainers, and the rows rentals are drawn from, by the round for the player and by the trainer's number for a trainer; a streak's trades lift some of the six into the next row, `Lifted`). The original draws on the game's one generator, so **every draw is on the sixteen-bit draw the caller hands over** (`host.Chance` in a script), in the original's order, and what was drawn is kept: a challenge put down is `FrontierRecords.Factory.Paused` (the trainers, the three in hand, the three just beaten). The records are the frame's stats, four to a way and a level (`RecordStreakStat` and its fellows), a streak running under `Factory` or `FactoryOpen`, and the wins counted under `Factory` for both levels. The trainers think by the round (`Mind`: no routine in the first two rounds, the basic one in the next two), which `FrontierOpponent.AsTrainer(mind)` hands the battle; the Factory Head is the 21st and the 49th battle of a Single streak. Scripts reach it through `factory …` (`Story/ScriptRunner.Factory.cs`; `docs/scripts.md`, "The Battle Factory") and the hosts through `Factory` and two more words of `FrontierAct` (`rent`, `trade`, answered in `Entered`). `GameEngine.Factory.cs` opens `FactoryScreen` (`GameState.FactoryRentals`; `OpenToRent`, `OpenToTrade`, `Move`, `Confirm`, `Cancel`, `Outcome`: logic that takes no input, on the original's rental application; drawn by `ModernUi.Factory.cs`, a rental's summary by `ModernUi.DrawSummary(..., rental: true)`) and asks for the round's models and menu sprites as the round is drawn (`AskForRentalPictures`), so the six are there to see. The lobby is on the original's plan with its floor level (`BattleFactory`; the original's flights of stairs are a ruling), and the corridor and the battle room (`BattleFactoryCorridor`, `BattleFactoryBattleRoom`) are laid out from the original's scene, which is a picture and no map: each is one arrival script, and `VAR_BATTLE_FACTORY_SCENE`, a variable of ours, tells the corridor what the battle room sent the player back with. The trigger under the Factory's porch on the grounds walks the player in (`battle_frontier.EnterBattleFactory`).
- **The Battle Hall** (plan 06 · R18c; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "The Battle Hall and the Battle Castle"): `BattleHallChallenge` (`Models/Frontier/BattleHall.cs`, GPU-free) is the original's `overlay104/battle_hall.c` and its helpers. One Pokémon of level 30 or more goes in at its own level (two of one species for a Double challenge) and meets ten trainers with one Pokémon each, healed before every battle; before each a type is chosen on a board (`Board`: the seventeen types in the board's own order, which is what a rank is kept under and what the trainers' classes go by, not the original's numbering of types). A type's rank, 0 to 10 wins, is four bits of a stat it shares with its neighbour (`LoadRank`, `SaveRank`) and lasts as long as the streak; with it go the opponent's level (`CalcOpponentsLevel`, in the console's fixed-point root, `FixedSqrt`, and single precision), the group its Pokémon is drawn from, its IVs and how its trainer thinks (`Mind`). The tables are `frontier.json`'s `hall` (`FrontierDatabase.Hall`: the Hall's own 477 sets with their types and groups, the classes of each rank and of each type, the class of every trainer), and **every draw is on the sixteen-bit draw the caller hands over**, in the original's order (`Choose`: the classes, the trainers, the Pokémon; `MakeOpponent`: its number and personality). A streak belongs to the species it was begun with (`StreakSpecies`, `Forfeit`), each species keeps a record of its own (`FrontierRecords.Hall`, a `HallRecord`: `Singles`, `Doubles`, a challenge put down), and the lobby's people read them (`AskKeeper` and its milestones, `FanStage`, `FanTalk`, `Visitors`). The Hall Matron is the 50th and the 170th battle of a Single streak, whatever type is chosen. Scripts reach it through `hall …` (`Story/ScriptRunner.Hall.cs`; `docs/scripts.md`, "The Battle Hall") and the hosts through `Hall` and two more words of `FrontierAct` (`enterhall`, `board`, answered in `Entered`). `GameEngine.Hall.cs` opens `HallBoardScreen` (`GameState.HallBoard`; `Open`, `Move`, `Confirm`, `Cancel`, `Outcome`: logic that takes no input, on the original's board, cursor and steps; drawn by `ModernUi.Hall.cs`). The lobby is on the original's plan with its floor level (`BattleHall`), the passage and the stage (`BattleHallCorridor`, `BattleHallStage`) are laid out from the original's scene, and **a whole round is the stage's one arrival script**, which loops. The records' board shows the species too (`FrontierBoard.Species`).
- **The Battle Castle** (plan 06 · R18c; the same rulings): `BattleCastleChallenge` (`Models/Frontier/BattleCastle.cs`, GPU-free) is the original's `overlay104/battle_castle.c`, its helpers and the rules of the valets' two applications. Three Pokémon of different species go in as copies the challenge carries (`Team`: level 50 at most, nothing held) and meet seven trainers; **nothing is restored between battles**, so a battle is fought on `Team` itself and what it did stays (after a win whoever fainted has one HP and every status is gone, `Won`). Castle Points are a stat of the way the challenge is taken (`Points`, `Receive`, `Spend`): ten as a round begins, what a battle earns by how it was won (`PointsFromBattle`: who stands, their HP and status, the PP used since `StartingPp`, the foes raised), and they buy what the counters sell, each asked in the original's order and answered with a `CastleAnswer`: `Heal`, `Rent` (what is lent at the rank, `Lent`), `RankUp`; `Identify`, `AdjustLevel`, `ShowStats`, `ShowMoves`; and `Pass`, from the fourth round. The tables are `frontier.json`'s `castle` (`FrontierDatabase.Castle`), the draws on the caller's sixteen-bit draw as the Factory's are, the trainers' minds by the round (`Mind`), the Castle Valet the 21st and the 49th battle of a Single streak. The points and the three counters' ranks stay with a running streak; a challenge put down is `FrontierRecords.Castle.Paused` (the three's HP, PP and items). Scripts reach it through `castle …` (`Story/ScriptRunner.Castle.cs`; `docs/scripts.md`, "The Battle Castle"; `castle begin` only once the save is agreed to, since it hands out the round's points) and the hosts through `Castle` and three more words of `FrontierAct` (`castleself`, `castlefoe`, `castlepoints`). `GameEngine.Castle.cs` opens `CastleScreen` (`GameState.CastleCounter`; `Open` for one's own three or for the trainer's, `Move`, `Confirm`, `Cancel`: logic that takes no input, on the original's two applications; drawn by `ModernUi.Castle.cs`) and keeps the Castle Points over the field while the valet speaks of them. The lobby is on the original's plan with its floor level (`BattleCastle`); the valet's room and the throne room (`BattleCastleCorridor`, `BattleCastleBattleRoom`) are laid out from the original's scene, each one arrival script, and `VAR_BATTLE_CASTLE_SCENE`, a variable of ours, tells the valet's room what the throne room sent the player back with. The triggers under the Hall's and the Castle's porches on the grounds walk the player in (`battle_frontier.EnterBattleHall`, `EnterBattleCastle`).
- **The Battle Arcade** (plan 06 · R18d; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, under the same name): `Models/Frontier/BattleArcade.cs` (GPU-free) is the original's `overlay104/battle_arcade.c`, its helpers and the game board's application. `ArcadeBoard` is the board: as it opens it draws a number for its events to go by, the panel its cursor starts on and then each of sixteen panels (`AvailableAt` by the round and the battle of the round, `TierOf` the row of weights by the last battle's score, `Assign` two draws a panel), and `Advance(press)` is one of its frames, thirty to the second: the opening, the count from three, the cursor a panel every so many frames by its speed (or jumping), stopped by the press or by its thirty seconds, the event shown. `BattleArcadeChallenge` is the challenge: three Pokémon of different species go in as copies holding nothing (`Team`) and meet seven trainers whose three hold nothing either (`Foe`); before each battle `OpenBoard` brings a board up and `TakeBoard` carries out the event it stopped on (`Apply`, on the challenge's own two threes: HP a fifth down, a status for all three or for one, a berry or an item lent, three levels; `Conditions` for the battle's weather or Trick Room; `Speed` and `Randomized` for the boards after); `ForBattle` hands the battle **copies** of both threes, each other's where the board swapped them, and makes both threes whole, so nothing of a battle outlasts it but what the challenger's own copies still hold (`AfterBattle`, which also scores the battle for the next board, `Performance`, and takes a raised level back). A win or a battle the board passed counts for the round (`Won`, `Skipped`: only a win counts as a victory), the Arcade Star is the 21st and the 49th battle of a Single streak, and the trainers think by the round (`Mind`). The tables are `frontier.json`'s `arcade` (`FrontierDatabase.Arcade`), **every draw is on the sixteen-bit draw the caller hands over**, in the original's order, and a challenge put down is `FrontierRecords.Arcade.Paused` (the game it had come to, the cursor's speed, the round's trainers, what the three hold). A battle's conditions go in through the frame, never into a running battle: `IScriptHost.FrontierBattle` takes a `FrontierConditions`, which `GameEngine.StartFrontierBattle` hands on as `BattleConditions.Weather` and `TrickRoom`, as a place's weather goes in. Scripts reach it through `arcade …` (`Story/ScriptRunner.Arcade.cs`; `docs/scripts.md`, "The Battle Arcade") and the hosts through `Arcade` and one more word of `FrontierAct` (`arcadeboard`; `HeadlessScriptHost.AtArcadeBoard` is a test's hand on a board, and `ArcadeBoard.Rig` makes its panels show what a test or the harness wants). `GameEngine.Arcade.cs` opens `ArcadeBoardScreen` (`GameState.ArcadeBoard`; `Open`, `Advance(dt)`, `Frames`, `PressStop`: a clock and one button, logic that takes no input; drawn by `ModernUi.Arcade.cs`, a panel's colour saying whom its event falls on). The lobby is on the original's plan with its floor level (`BattleArcade`); the corridor and the game room (`BattleArcadeCorridor`, `BattleArcadeBattleRoom`) are laid out from the original's scene, and **a whole round is the game room's one arrival script**, which loops. The trigger under the Arcade's porch on the grounds walks the player in (`battle_frontier.EnterBattleArcade`). With it all five facilities are built: what the Battle Frontier still lacks as a whole is listed under R18 in plan 06.
- **The Battle Subway and the World Tournament's first tournament** (plan 22 · U6, part d; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "Unova's second chapter, part d"): not a sixth facility, but the Frontier's rules lent to Unova by `Models/Frontier/BattleSubway.cs` (GPU-free) and the script command `subway` (`Story/ScriptRunner.Subway.cs`; `docs/scripts.md`): who may go in (`FrontierRules`), copies at level 50 (`FrontierRules.TeamFor`), a Frontier battle through `host.FrontierBattle`, the Frontier's trainers (`FrontierTrainers`) drawn by the Tower's rooms, the Frontier's Battle Points. **A run is the story's own variables** (`VAR_UNOVA_SUBWAY_*`, `BattleSubway.Vars`), never a field of a host or of a save, so a game saved in a car resumes at its battle; the Subway has no `FrontierFacility`, no stats and no print. The Gear Station's desks (`GearStation.txt`) begin a Single, Double or Multi run and `BattleSubwayTrain.txt`'s arrival script loops the cars; Ingo and Emmet are our own teams (`our-trainers.json`, `subway_ingo` and `subway_emmet`) at the 21st and 49th battle. `subway foe "<id>"` stands any trainer of the data at level 50 in the car's place, which is how the World Tournament's hall (`PokemonWorldTournament.txt`, a map of ours: the Driftveil Tournament of three rounds, once, a story event; plan 14 · B11 and B12 own the real formats) plays Cheren, Hugh and Clay. A new script command that asks for a team of three of different species needs its given in `ScriptWalk.Givens` (`subway enough`), or the scenes behind it are never played by `SceneSkipTests`; and conditions added to `common.UnovaCatchUp` become givens of every Unova arrival that starts a scene, so a catch-up for a later part goes in its own place's `OnEnter` (`pwt.txt`'s) or as a flag-only script (`UnovaFrigate`).
- **Amity Square's walk** (plan 06 · R16c; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, under the same name): the rules are GPU-free in `Overworld/AmitySquare.cs`, from the original's `scripts_amity_square.s` and `scrcmd_amity_square.c`: the twenty species the receptionists let out (four of them with the National Pokédex; `MayWalk`, `HasPermitted`, `Next`), what the Pokémon turns up after two hundred steps (`DrawNext`, decided ahead by three dice; `Find`: the six rows of accessories by family, the nine berries, the kind decided tried first and the other when it doesn't fit), the man of the park's sixteen gifts and five places (`Arrive`), and the ruins' nine huts (`Huts`, `Leading`: a step north, west or east of a hut's middle leads to another's; the two of the west lawn lead only to one another, the seven of the east about among themselves). **What a walk remembers is the story's own variables**, under the original's names (`VAR_FOLLOWER_MON_ACTIVE`, `_PARTY_ID`, `_SPECIES`, the three of the next find, `VAR_AMITY_SQUARE_STEP_COUNT`), so a game saved on a walk needs no field of its own: `GameEngine.ResumeStroll` puts the Pokémon back behind the player as the save loads, and the square's `OnEnter` leaves a walk under way alone. Scripts reach it through `amity …` (`Story/ScriptRunner.Amity.cs`; `docs/scripts.md`, "Amity Square's walk") and the hosts through `WalkWith`; the square's own script (`amity_square.txt`) is the original's: the two gates' tiles that ask and say goodbye, the Pokémon's talk, the receptionists, the man, and the huts' twenty-seven tiles, each a `place player` behind a fade (a Pokémon out of its ball is behind the player wherever a script puts them, `FieldHost.Place`, and follows a walk a script sends the player on, `GameEngine.TrailBehind`). The Pokémon is the square's own object `follower_mon` (the overlay makes it a Pokémon and names a `species` for it to stand as until dressed), off the map behind `FLAG_HIDE_AMITY_SQUARE_FOLLOWER_MON`; `GameEngine.AmitySquare.cs` lets it out (the script waits for its model behind the dark screen, `AwaitingStroll`), keeps the place of the team the story remembers with the Pokémon when the team is put in another order (`KeepTheStroll`), and ends the walk however the square is left (`LeaveStrollBehind`). **A tall Pokémon keeps two tiles behind** (`Follower.Gap`, `GameEngine.TallStroller`: Torterra and Empoleon): a tile south of the player it hid them to the shoulders from the field's steep camera; it makes way like any follower when walked into and is spoken to from the tile between. A hut is a thing built and walked into (`PropType.RuinHut`, `Landmarks.Amity.cs`: whoever steps through its doorway is under its slab and unseen) and each receptionist stands behind a `ReceptionDesk`. `WorldWalk` follows a tile whose script puts the player down elsewhere on its map, which is how a walk of the whole game finds the square's raised ground.
- **The game with every species in it** (plan 03 · D13; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, under the same name): what the number 1,025 changes. **The PC** has Platinum's eighteen boxes until the Pokédex is the National one and a hundred from then on (the user's number; `PcBoxes.BoxCount`, `NationalBoxCount`, `Grow`, `Capacity`, `Free`): the script's `nationaldex` gives them (both hosts), a save keeps as many boxes as its PC has (`SavedBoxes.ToBoxes`), one that had the National Pokédex before is given them as it loads, and whatever asks how many boxes there are asks the PC (`PcBoxes.Boxes.Count`, `PCScreen.BoxCount`, an instance's), never a constant. **A saved Pokémon** carries its species' name and its National Pokédex number (`SavedPokemonData.DexNumber`), and is found by the number where the data no longer has the name. **What the game keeps of the species it has shown is bounded**: the menu sprites of `PokemonSprites.Kept` species (`Graphics/SpriteLedger.cs`, GPU-free: the longest unasked for go first, never one asked for in the last `FreshFrames`, nor the start's own), no model for good but the stand-in's, `Cries.Kept` cries. `FullDexTests` makes a Pokémon of every species and form at four levels, saves and reads each back, and holds the bounds; the megabytes are the harness's `fulldex` mode's.
- **The Pokégear** (plan 20 · J3; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "The Pokégear"; style guide, "Menu screens"): Johto's alone, an entry of the start menu once `FLAG_GOT_POKEGEAR` is set. The rules are `Models/Pokegear.cs` (GPU-free): the cards by their flags, a number registered by a flag of ours (`Pokegear.ContactFlag`, the script command `addcontact`), the phone book (`PhoneBook`, Johto's `phone.json`), `PhoneRematch` (a registered trainer asks for a rematch on their day and hour and waits, spinning, until fought; the teams are their row of `trainers.json`, plan 06 · R12's `VsSeeker.RematchTeam`, opened by the Johto Badges), `PhoneRinger` (a contact rings on a step once ten minutes have passed, one time in two) and the radio (`RadioDial`, HeartGold's round dial and stations; `RadioShows`, Johto's `radio.json`; the songs, which go into `EncounterMoment.Radio`: the March and the Lullaby move `EncounterSteps.Meets`' share by 25, the Sounds fill slots 2 to 5). `GameEngine.Pokegear.cs` meets the field: `UI/PokegearScreen.cs` (`GameState.Pokegear`, drawn by `ModernUi.Pokegear.cs`), a call is the contact's script of `phone.txt` with `VAR_PHONE_CALL` set (`GameEngine.PhoneScripts`), the map card is the Town Map's screen in `TownMapMode.Card` on Johto's `townmap.json`, and the radio's song plays over the field (`PlayFieldMusic`) until tuned away, a warp or a battle.
- **Clothes** (plan 11 · C9, C10, done early): what the player wears is an `Outfit` (`Core/Outfit.cs`: a garment of `Data/clothes.json` in each slot, `ClothingDatabase`), kept in `PlayerIdentity.Outfit` and the save (`SaveData.Outfit`, `SaveData.Wardrobe` for what was bought; `Models/Wardrobe.cs`, GPU-free). Only drawing reads it: `PlayerIdentity.Character` is the look's name dressed (`PLAYER@red_cap.-.-.-.-`), which `CharacterStyle.For` reads back through `CharacterStyle.Dressed`, so an outfit with nothing chosen is plain `PLAYER` or `DAWN`, drawn as before. `wardrobe` in a script opens `WardrobeScreen` (`GameState.Wardrobe`): at home the player's own clothes, with a boutique's key its stock for sale (the Jubilife boutique, `JubilifeBoutique`, until plan 01 · M11's Department Store). The screen tries outfits on through `CharacterModels.TryGet`, which never blocks, and frees the rigs it tried (`Forget`).

### The story and its scripts

Plan 02 · S1. `docs/scripts.md` is the language's reference; read it before writing a scene.

- **What the story remembers** is `Story/StoryState.cs` (GPU-free, owned by `GameEngine` as `story`): flags, integer variables, the trainers beaten, the items taken, each region's badges (plan 18 · W2: a mask by the region's id, its bits in the order of the names its file lists; `GiveBadge(region, name)`, `HasBadge`, `BadgeCount(region)`, `TotalBadges`; what reads "the badges" reads the region the player is in, `GameEngine.Here`, and a script's `givebadge`, `if badge`, `if badges` and `BADGE_COUNT` the host's `Region`), the two starters and the people spoken to (`Greeted`, each once, filled by `TryInteract` for Spiritomb's tower: `GREETINGS`, `greetings clear`). Flags and variables are named after the original's (`FLAG_...`, `VAR_...`). `Revision` counts every change. It is saved in `SaveData` (`StoryFlags`, `StoryVariables`, `DefeatedTrainers`, `TakenItems`, `RegionBadges`, the starters, and `StoryVersion`), and `Story/StoryMigration.cs` brings a save from an older game up to date as it loads: a chapter that changes what an existing save must already know raises `StoryState.CurrentVersion` and adds its step there (version 2: a save from before the first chapter, whose game began with a Turtwig and a Pokédex, is taken past it by `common.OpeningDone`; version 3: a save from before the second is given `common.ChapterTwo`, which hides that chapter's people until their scenes and which `NewGame` calls too; version 5: a save that won the Coal Badge before Oreburgh Gate's hiker gave out HM06 is handed it, into the bag `Upgrade` is given, or the Ravaged Path's rocks shut it out of the north; version 7: `common.TimedPeople` puts out the doubles of the people who battle only at some hours, below; version 8: `common.CampaignOver` takes the Pokétch campaign's clowns away once the north gate's battle is won, the third from Jubilife TV's door; version 9: `common.ChapterFour` hides the fourth chapter's people until their scenes, and with the Relic Badge won takes Fantina out of the Contest Hall and the guide from her Gym's door; version 10: `common.ChapterFive`, which `ChapterFour` calls, hides the fifth chapter's, and a save with Maylene's or Crasher Wake's Badge, or that has been to Pastoria City, is taken past what they brought, plan 02 · S8; version 11: `common.ChapterSix`, which `ChapterFive` calls, hides the sixth chapter's, a save that cured Route 210's Psyduck before Cynthia came after them is given her Old Charm, one that has been to Canalave City has Route 218's blockade gone, and one with the Vs. Seeker its first level of rematches, plan 02 · S9; version 12: `common.ChapterSeven`, which `ChapterSix` calls, hides the seventh chapter's, a save with the Mine Badge keeps the library's people as Byron's script left them, and one that has been to Snowpoint City, or has its Icicle Badge, finds Team Galactic's grunts gone from Mt. Coronet's northern hall and Maylene past on Route 217, plan 02 · S10; version 13: `common.ChapterEight`, which `ChapterSeven` calls, hides the eighth chapter's, and a save that has been to Snowpoint City, or has its Icicle Badge, finds the rival gone from the head of Acuity Lakefront's rock face, where he waits to call down to whoever first comes up from Route 217, plan 02 · S11; version 14: `common.Headquarters`, which `ChapterEight` calls, hides the people of that chapter's second half (the grunt who lets the Storage Key fall in Veilstone City, Cyrus on the dais of Team Galactic's hall, Commander Saturn in its lobby), and a save that has already played Lake Acuity's scene keeps the grunt on the terrace, as that scene shows him, plan 02 · S11, part b; version 15: `common.ChapterNine`, which `Headquarters` calls, hides the ninth chapter's (Looker on Mt. Coronet, the rival at Spear Pillar, Cynthia and Mesprit at the torn pillar, the painting's shards until the button, and whoever flags of our own keep off Spear Pillar and the Distortion World until their scenes), lets Giratina stand in Turnback Cave unless it was caught, where a game of ours had hidden it until the Hall of Fame, and takes Cynthia from Sendoff Spring for a save already past the Hall of Fame, plan 02 · S12; version 16: `common.ChapterTen`, which `ChapterNine` calls, hides the tenth chapter's (the professor in Verity Cavern, the rival in Sunyshore City), a guardian of the lakes the player already has counts as caught, and a save that has been to Sunyshore City, or holds its Beacon Badge, finds Flint gone from the gate and the Gym's door, Volkner back in his Gym and the road from Valor Lakefront open, plan 02 · S13; version 17: `common.ChapterEleven`, which `ChapterTen` calls, hides the eleventh chapter's (the rival in the League's Pokémon Center, and the Day-Care couple's fan unless a Pokémon has been left with them), and a save that entered the Hall of Fame before its scene was written is given what a victory sets by the scene's own `Victory` script, with the Vs. Seeker's fourth level and the rival's battle before the League's door counted as had, plan 02 · S14; version 18: `common.ChapterTwelve`, which `ChapterEleven` calls, hides the people of the time after the Hall of Fame (Palmer in the Fight Area, Buck and the rival in the Survival Area, Byron in Iron Island's cabin, Route 224's two, and whoever parts b and c bring on), and a save that has already been to the Battle Zone, which it can have reached only while nobody stood there, has the Fight Area's and Route 227's first scenes counted as played, their people gone and the blockades with them, plan 02 · S15, part a; version 19: the badges by region, plan 18 · W2: an older save's one mask, `SaveData.Badges`, is given to the region it was made in by `SaveData.RegionalBadges` as the story is read, before the steps above, which ask Sinnoh's badges). The original's `RemoveObject` sets the hiding flag of whoever it takes off the map (`MapObject_SetFlagAndDeleteObject`): a scene ported from it that sends someone away hides them by their flag too (`hide` and `setflag`), or they are back on the next visit. `common.NewGame` is the script that sets what every game starts with; it runs with no screen (for a new game and for the oldest saves), so it may only set flags and variables, and it ends in `return`, because `OpeningDone` calls it and `end` would end that too. What the game gives as the story goes are flags as well: the Pokédex (`StoryState.PokedexFlag`, which the start menu asks before it offers POKéDEX) and the Running Shoes (`RunningShoesFlag`, `Player.HasRunningShoes`).
- **Scripts** are text files, one to a place (`Data/scripts/<area key or map name>.txt`) plus `common.txt`. `ScriptParser` reads a file into `Script`s of checked `Instruction`s and tells anything wrong with its file and line; `ScriptLibrary` holds them all (`Default` is the game's), finds a name in the place's file first and in `common` second, and lists what is wrong beyond grammar (`Problems`: an item, a species, a song, a map that doesn't exist). The lines are our own, on the original's beats.
- **The runner and its host**: `ScriptRunner` decides what happens and in what order (jumps, calls, conditions, the batch of lines that makes one talk, what a battle's outcome does to the script) and an `IScriptHost` is where it happens. The game's host is `GameEngine.FieldHost`; `HeadlessScriptHost` is a game with no screen, where everything happens at once and is written down (`Transcript`, `Asked`, `Log`, `Problems`), which is how tests play a script, or a chapter's, to its end. A new command is an `Op`, a case in the parser, a case in the runner, perhaps a method of the host (both hosts), a line in `ScriptTests.EveryCommand`, and its row in `docs/scripts.md`.
- **In the game** a script runs over the field in `GameState.Overworld` (`UpdateOverworld` hands the frame to `UpdateScript` while one is running, so the keys do nothing) and **goes on only in that state**. Whatever it starts that the player must see through changes the state (text is `GameState.Dialogue`, a battle, a screen, a warp's fade) or is something `FieldHost.Busy` reads (the script's own fade), and the runner waits for `Busy` to clear. Text closing hands the frame straight back to the script (`UpdateDialogue`), so the box doesn't blink between two talks. A question is a line of the text box that stays (`DialogueManager.ShowQuestion`) with its answers beside it in a `ChoiceBox` (`UI/Kit`, GPU-free; `ModernUi.DrawChoices`). People a script sends walking are `NpcWalk`s, the player a `PlayerWalk` (the player's own steps, with no wild Pokémon and no doors; a step that can't be taken is given up after three quarters of a second); a script's black screen is a `ScreenFade` drawn between the field and the text box.
- **What starts one** (`GameEngine.TryInteract`, `OnStep`, `EnterArea`, `FieldScripts`): talking to someone (their `Script`, or the common one for what they are; a trainer who walked up runs the same), a signboard (`common.Sign`, or `Map.SignScripts`), something read in the ground (`Map.TileScripts`, an overlay's `read`: a door, a statue), a step onto a `StepTrigger` whose variable has its value (`Map.Triggers`; an overlay gives the original's triggers their scripts by number; one in front of a warp also starts as the player comes out of it, `steppedOutOfWarp`, and one on a warp's own tile goes before the warp), and arriving somewhere (`OnEnter` in the place's file, on a warp, a loaded save or a walk across an area's border). A step across a border onto a trigger runs the place's `OnEnter` first and the trigger once it has ended (`triggerAfterEnter`), as the original runs a map's own script before its coordinate events.
- **Furniture and the programmes** (plan 08 · P8; `docs/scripts.md`, "Looking at furniture"): a television, a bookshelf, a bin and a map on a wall with nothing of their own to say run `common.Television`, `Bookshelf`, `TrashCan` and `TownMap` (`FieldScripts.ForFurniture`: a room's prop that covers the tile, else the tile's behaviour, read as the GBA games read it, a bin from any side and the rest facing north), after a sign, a tile script (a room file's `tileScripts`, an overlay's `read`) and a hidden item. A television's programme is the script's `broadcast`: `Story/Broadcasts.cs` (GPU-free) makes it of `BroadcastFacts` (`IScriptHost.Broadcast`, which the game's host fills with the clock, the play time and the whole Pokédex) in `ui.json`'s `broadcast.*` words, drawn on the script's die; a programme says only what is true of the game. `townmap` opens the Town Map in its wall mode. The harness's `story` ends on them (`st90` to `st93`), and `Driver.ScriptNow` names the script running.
- **Phrases and the interview** (plan 08 · P9; style guide, "Phrases"): `phrase NAME` opens the phrase picker over the field (`PhraseScreen`, `GameState.Phrases`, the GPU-free `UI/Kit/PhraseEntry.cs` on `Data/PhraseGroups.cs`: the Pokémon seen, the team's moves, the types and five lists of our own in `ui.json`'s `phrases.words`) and keeps the two words by key (`feelings:happy`, never the shown word) in `StoryState.Phrases`, saved as `SaveData.StoryPhrases`; `{phrase:NAME}` says them in the language chosen. Jubilife TV's reporter (`jubilife_city.Interview`, once a day) keeps `INTERVIEW` and sets `FLAG_JUBILIFE_TV_INTERVIEW_ON_AIR`, which `common.Television` and `PlayerHouse.TV` answer with `broadcast interview` (a programme by name, `Broadcasts.Names`) before clearing it; after that `Programme.Interview` is among those a die draws. A later picker (mail, the Trainer Card's greeting) opens the same screen under a name of its own; the harness's `story` shoots it last (`st94` to `st9a`).
- **The text box's markup and backlog** (plan 09 · L3; style guide, "Text box markup"): a line of a script, overlay or room may carry `{red:…}`, `{blue:…}`, `{name:…}`, `{money N}`, `{pause S}`, `{speed N}`, `{br}` and `{sfx name}`, parsed by `UI/Kit/RichText` (GPU-free; anything else in braces is left alone, since `{money}`, `{var:X}` and the rest are other parts' placeholders). `DialogueManager` holds a `RichLine` (`CurrentLine` and `VisibleText` are the plain text, `CurrentSpans` its colours) and fills `{player}` as a blue (boy) or red (girl) run in `Prepare`; `PlayerIdentity.Fill` stays plain. Wrapping and `ContentCheck`'s measuring go by the stripped text (`RichText.Plain`), and `HeadlessScriptHost` strips it from its transcript; a new place that shows a line outside the box strips it too. Up over a whole plain line opens the backlog (`DialogueLog`, fifty lines). `ScriptLibrary.Problems` and `ContentCheck` report a bad code or an unknown `sfx`.
- **The day and the hour** (plan 06 · R14a): a new day runs `Models/DailyEvents.cs` (the original's `FieldSystem_HandleDailyEvents`, for the parts this game has: the `FLAG_DAILY_...` flags cleared, the day's number moved on, `VAR_DAILY_RANDOM_LEVEL`, the hidden items that come back) from `GameEngine.KeepTheEncounterClock`. Scripts ask `if time morning` and `if weekday friday`; someone who is there only at some hours is a pair of the original's objects on one tile, the one who battles and a double who only talks, swapped by the place's `OnEnter` (Routes 209, 210 and 215's joggers, 212 and 222's policemen). The Lottery Corner is `Models/Lottery.cs`, `lottery draw` and `JubilifeTV1F`, the station's lobby (on the original's plan since plan 01 · M11's last session). The berry patches are `Overworld/BerryPatches.cs` (GPU-free, the original's `berry_patches.c`: 128 patches, their stages, water, mulch, yield and regrowth, counted in minutes by `KeepTheEncounterClock` and saved as `SaveData.Berries`); each patch of soil in the world is a thing of the map (`NPC.IsBerrySoil`, `BerryPatch`: its number from the area file's `patch`) that grows only once seen (`GameEngine.KeepBerriesInView`), is drawn by `BerryArt` and runs `common.BerryPatch` (`berry ...`, `chooseitem`, which opens the bag to pick, `BagScreen.OpenToPick`). A berry's growing is the item's `Berry` (`items.json`'s `berry`). The harness's `berries` mode (not part of `all`) shoots the stages, a patch picked and the Lottery Corner (`b*`). Its `poketch` mode (not part of `all`) shows every app of the Pokétch close up, as it comes up and taken in hand and touched (`pk*`).
- **Who moves about** (plan 02 · S6): a person of the world takes the original's movement type and range from the area file (`NPC.Movement`, `PersonMovement.Parse`), and `Overworld/Wandering.cs` (GPU-free) moves them while the field is the player's: looking about, wandering within their box, walking a loop. Nobody starts a move while a script, a trainer's walk or a menu has the field; coming to a map again puts everyone back where they first stood (`Wandering.SendHome`). The harness keeps everyone still (`GameEngine.PeopleStayPut`) but where a mode shows them moving. Someone a scene counts on finding where the import stands them is kept still by their overlay (`"still": true`).
- **Someone travelling with the player** (plan 02 · S6, `partner <who> "<trainer>"`): `Overworld/Follower.cs` walks them a step behind, onto each tile the player leaves, and `Map.Follower` keeps them out of the player's way. **There is one kind of follower and the game has one at a time** (`GameEngine.along`, plan 06 · R16c): a person with a `TrainerId`, who battles beside the player (`GameEngine.partner` is that kind alone, which is what everything that changes for a partner asks), or a Pokémon of the team out of its ball (`Follower.Pokemon`, dressed onto a thing of the map by `Follower.Dress`), which only walks: in Amity Square now, and wherever plan 10 · F3 lets the lead follow. A partner stands on the battle's platform beside the player until the Pokémon are sent out (`BattleAnimator.PartnerTrainer`, their look the person's they are in the field). While they travel along, the engine heals the team after every battle not lost (`EndBattle`), meets two wild Pokémon at a time in the grass (`StartWildBattle`, `Map.MeetAnother`), brings two trainers who see the player at once together for a tag battle (`pairApproach`, `common.TrainerPair`, `pair` in a script), keeps the Bicycle, the rods, an Escape Rope and the field moves that leave the place in the bag (`FieldSpot.Partner`), and drops them on a white-out (`SetPartner`). Saved (`SaveData.Partner`, `PartnerPerson`).
- **Who is on the map**: a person may carry `HiddenBy` (a flag that takes them off the map; the world's people get the original's own from the area file) or `ShownBy`. `Map.ApplyPresence` moves people between `Map.NPCs` and `Map.Absent`, so everything that asks `NPCs` (drawing, walking, trainers' eyes) goes on as if the absent weren't there; the engine does it for every map whenever `story.Revision` has moved (`RefreshPresence`). A script's own `hide` and `show` set `NPC.Forced`, which holds until the map is come to again: someone a scene brings on for good is cleared off their flag as well (`clearflag`, then `show`, as the original's `ClearFlag` and `AddObject`), or they are gone again after the next door (the briefcase on Route 201 was, and the game went on with no Pokémon).
- **Items on the ground** (plan 02, after S1): an item to pick up is an `NPC` whose `NpcType` is `ItemBall` (`IsItemBall`), with its `Item`, `ItemCount` and a `HiddenBy` flag of its own. It is drawn as a Poké Ball on a card (`OutdoorProps.PaintItemBall`, `WorldRenderer.DrawItemBalls`), is in the way like a person, and runs `common.ItemBall`: `setflag own` (the flag that hides it, so it is gone for good, in this game and every save of it) and `find own`. Something hidden is a `HiddenItem` in `Map.HiddenItems`, found by looking at its tile (`FieldScripts.HiddenAt`, `common.HiddenItem`). What a script was started with is its "own": lines (`sayown`), an item (`find own`), a flag (`setflag own`); `ScriptRunner.Start` takes them, and takes an item ball's from the ball. In the imported world none of this is written by hand: `tools/MapImporter` reads what each ball and each hidden place holds from the original's item scripts and tables and writes it into the area's file (`AreaObject.Item`, `AreaSign.Item`), and `WorldMapBuilder.PlaceEvents` lays them out for every open area.
- **Local flags**: a flag named `FLAG_MAP_LOCAL_...` lasts only while the player stays in its place (`StoryState.ClearLocal`, run by `ChangePlace`), as the original's do. An obstacle's flag is one; a scene that should be undone once the player leaves can use one too.
- **Battles from scripts**: `battle self` goes through the same `StartTrainerBattle` as ever; the engine notes the outcome (`scriptOutcome`) as the battle ends and the script goes on from it once the field is back. A battle lost ends the script (the player wakes up at home) unless the script said `canlose`. No battle is ever started without a Pokémon able to fight: the field meets no wild Pokémon and no trainer's eyes then (`StartWildBattle`, `CheckTrainerSight`), and a script's battle says why and ends the script (`IScriptHost.CanBattle`; `HeadlessScriptHost.NeedsPokemon` turns the check on in tests, as `OpeningTests.Game` does for every chapter).
- **Scenes that may be skipped** (plan 12 · Q20; `docs/scripts.md`, "Scenes that may be skipped"; style guide, "Field menus and notices", "A scene skipped"). A script marks a scene with `scene "Name"` and `endscene`: a stretch that takes the field from the player to show something, never a person who says two lines. `scene` goes after the story's leading questions (`if var … end`), `endscene` before the `end` or `return` of every way out, neither behind an `if`; a scene's battle, question and warp are inside it, what it `call`s is inside it too, and a called script that ends the whole script is called after the `endscene` (`ScriptLibrary.Problems` names a scene left open, an `endscene` with none, a scene inside a scene and two of one name in a file). **Seen**: a scene that reaches its `endscene`, played or skipped, is seen by the game (`StoryState.ScenesSeen`, saved as `SaveData.ScenesSeen`, empty in an older save with no version raised) and by the machine (`GameSettings.ScenesSeen`, written with the options once for each scene, whatever the row says); a scene that ends any other way (the script's `end`, a battle lost, a script stopped) is not. **The row** is `OptionRow.SkipScenes` (`Off` unless chosen, `Seen`, `Any`), and `SceneSkip.Allowed` alone decides. **The hold** (`SkipHold`, GPU-free; `GameEngine.Skipping.cs`): the `Menu` action held for a second in `UpdateScript` or `UpdateDialogue` while a scene that may be skipped runs fills `ModernUi.SkipRing`, the picture goes dark (0.2 s), the runner hurries in one frame behind it (`ScriptRunner.Hurry`) and the dark opens (0.3 s) on what the scene leaves. **The hurry stops before** whatever waits for the player or can't be ended at once (`ScriptRunner.StopsAHurry`: `ask`, `choose`, a battle, a screen, `save`, a field move, a puzzle's pieces), which is then carried out at the scene's own pace, and a host that turns `Busy` all the same ends it; **`StopsAHurry` names every `Op` and throws for one it hasn't been told of**, so a new command says which it is. **A host method that starts something the player sees through says what it does while `Skipping`**, one of three things (`IScriptHost.Skipping` has the rule): what is only shown or heard is dropped (lines, a bubble, a gesture, a sound, a shake, a passing effect, slow motion; `AudioManager.Hushed` drops whatever would be heard); what leaves something behind is ended at once in the state it leaves (a warp arrives, a fade, the camera, a held effect, a machine's frame, the music); and **a walk is walked, unseen**: the time the scene would have waited passes for whoever is walking (`IScriptHost.Elapse`, a `wait`, a bubble, a camera's move, a fade, a gesture, a set piece) and where the scene waits for its walks they all walk to the end together (`Arrive`), because the player's scripted steps are their own and where they end can hang on who stood where when (someone sent ahead with `move` before a `wait`, and the player's `walk` after it). Every flag, variable, item, Pokémon, `hide`, `show`, `place` and battle happens as when the scene is played. **The test that holds it** is `SceneSkipTests.EverySceneOfTheGamePlayedAndSkippedLeavesTheSame` (`ScriptWalk.PlayEveryScene`): every script that marks a scene, on every way through it, played and then played by someone who holds the button the whole time (`ScriptRunner.Impatient`), must leave the same people, tiles, flags, items, team and questions, and every scene must be played to its end on some way (it finds the story a scene needs among the conditions its script asks); a scene written later is taken by itself. The Hall of Fame's scene is a scene only for its opening words: the machine, the team's entry, the save and the credits are never skipped.
- **Who speaks a line, for the voices** (plan 26 · V1; `docs/plans/26-voice.md`, "V1 as built"): `Data/voice/lines.json` is the catalogue of every line a character says (key, voice, English and its hash, spoken text, variants), `Data/voice/cast.json` the cast, `docs/voice/coverage.md` the report; `tools/VoiceTool catalogue` writes all three from `Data/VoiceCatalogue.cs`, and `VoiceTests` fails when they are not what the game gives, when a speaker has no cast entry or when a spoken text holds a free placeholder. **A new script's speaker reaches the cast by its display name** (`speaker "Roark"` finds `names["Roark"]` in the cast) or by an id written before it (`speaker roark "Roark"`, `Instruction.VoiceId`); a new person of an overlay or a map reaches it by their name, trainer's name, class or look (`VoiceCast.ForPerson`). **After adding or rewriting any line, script, speaker or person, run `dotnet run --project tools/VoiceTool -- catalogue --add-missing`**: a name nobody has cast gets a provisional voice, a rewritten line is listed as stale (its audio, once there is any, is not played), and a spoken text or direction written by hand in `lines.json` (`manual`) is kept while the line's hash holds. Keys are plan 17 · N4's, so inserting a `say` before others in a script moves the later keys on to other lines: the report shows it as stale.

- **What to do next, and rumours** (plan 12 · Q14; `docs/plans/12-quality-of-life.md`, its outcome): `Story/Objectives.cs` (GPU-free) lists each region's objectives in play order (`Objectives.All`, lines under `objectives.*` in `ui.json`). **Sinnoh's are derived from the flags, variables and Badges its chapters set** (the first whose `Done` isn't yet satisfied; a `Done` may only be something that never clears, and `ObjectiveTests` sets everything a script writes and asks for `free`), so they need no field, version or migration. **A chapter of another region adds its objectives there** (ids in a block of its own) **and ends its scenes with `objective <name>`**, which stores the number in `VAR_OBJECTIVE`; a stored objective further along than the derived one wins, in its own region only. The line (`Objectives.Line`, `GameEngine.NextObjectiveLine`, the options' `NextObjective` row, `RulesDefault`, on unless turned off) is drawn by the start menu, the Trainer Card and the save panel (`ModernUi.NextLine`), and spoken by `{objective}` / `if objective` in `common.NextHint` (the nurse and Mom ask first). Townsfolk opt in to `common.Rumour` with `"script": "Rumour"`.

### Evolution and friendship

`docs/mechanics/evolution.md` lists every evolution method of the main games and what this game does for each; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md` records the stand-ins where the original needs hardware or a mode this game lacks. Read both before touching evolution.

- **Rules** (`Models/Evolution.cs`, no drawing or input): `Evolution.Find(pokemon, trigger, context)` says which evolution happens now and `Evolution.Evolve` carries it out. A trigger is a level-up, an item used on the Pokémon, a trade, the player spinning or the end of a battle (Sirfetch'd's critical hits); `EvolutionContext` carries what the Pokémon can't know itself (party, bag, night, dusk, rain, the map's `EvolutionSites`, the item, the species traded for, the region). The first of a species' evolutions whose condition holds wins, so their order in `species.json` matters (the importer sets it). An evolution can start from a form, lead into one or happen in one region (`fromForm`, `targetForm`, `region`); a regional form evolves only by its own (docs/mechanics/evolution.md, "Forms").
- **Never in the middle of things**: `Pokemon.GainExp` only levels up. A battle records who grew (`BattleEngine.LeveledUp`) and `GameEngine.EndBattle` asks the rules once it is over; the bag hands its evolutions over through `BagScreen.TakeEvolution`. Both end in `GameEngine.PlayEvolutions`, which queues them and fades into `GameState.Evolution`.
- **The scene** (`UI/EvolutionScreen.cs`): a function of time and three buttons (`Advance`, `PressConfirm`, `PressCancel`, `MoveCursor`), so tests drive it without a window. The species changes at the start of its `Burst` phase. Its Pokémon are rendered offscreen in `Render` (before `BeginTextureMode(virtualScreen)`, like every 3D pass) with `PokemonSprites.Render(..., flash:)`; pass the flash there, because `SetStudio` clears one set beforehand.
- **Friendship** (`Models/Friendship.cs`): Platinum's table. `GainExp` applies the level-up bonus, `GameEngine.OnStep` the walking one every 128 steps, the battle the faint penalty. `OnStep` also counts steps for the Pokémon at the head of the party, and the battle counts move uses, knock-outs, critical hits (one battle at a time), damage taken and recoil (until a faint), but only for species and forms whose evolution asks for them (`Pokemon.EvolutionProgress`).
- **Trades**: `GameEngine.ReceiveTradedPokemon(received, given)` is the single entry point for a Pokémon arriving by trade (online or from a character): it swaps it in and plays its trade evolution. Nothing calls it yet.

### The personality and breeding

Plan 06 · R15; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "Breeding and what a personality decides".

- **What a personality decides** (`Models/Personality.cs`, GPU-free): every Pokémon the game makes draws its personality first (two 16-bit draws) and its IVs after (two more), and reads its nature (`% 25`), gender (its low byte against the species' share), ability (bit 0) and shininess (against `PlayerIdentity.Number`, the player's 32-bit trainer number, under the `Ruleset`'s odds) from it. A trainer's Pokémon keep the personality the trainer data gives and are never shiny. Something that wants a nature, a gender or a shiny Pokémon asks for a personality that gives it (`Personality.Choose`, as Synchronize and Cute Charm do; `ShinyWith`, as the Poké Radar does), never sets the fields afterwards. Nature, gender and shininess are still saved beside the personality, so an older save loads as it was. Every seeded draw after a Pokémon is made moved with R15.
- **Breeding** (GPU-free): `Models/Breeding.cs` is the original's `daycare.c` and `egg_hatch.c` (how well two get on, the Egg's species, form, IVs, moves, nature and Masuda, the cycles and Flame Body, hatching), `Models/DayCare.cs` the Day Care (two places, the steps each has walked, the fee, the Egg the couple find). `GameEngine.Breeding.cs` walks them on every step (`DayCareStep`, from `OnStep`) and plays a hatching (`common.HatchEgg`, then `GameState.Hatch` and `UI/HatchScreen.cs`, a function of time and one button like the evolution scene). Scripts reach them through `daycare`, `giveegg` and `hatch` (`docs/scripts.md`, "The Day Care and Eggs"). Saved as `SaveData.DayCare`.
- **An Egg** is a `Pokemon` with `IsEgg` (its cycles in `EggCycles`, which is the friendship field, as in the original). It is drawn as an Egg wherever a Pokémon is (`PixelArtGenerator.IconOf`, `SpriteOf`, never `GetPokemonSprite` by species; `DisplayName` says "Egg") and counts as fainted (`IsFainted`), so whatever asks who can fight passes it by without a check of its own; what would heal, feed, teach, evolve or befriend a Pokémon asks `IsEgg` first.
- **A Pokémon's own id** (plan 07 · O2; plan 07's decisions 5 to 9): `Pokemon.Id` is a GUID of the trainer's number and a count (`Models/PokemonIds.cs`, `SaveData.PokemonIdsIssued`), drawn from nothing, so it moves no seeded draw. Only the player's Pokémon have one (the team, the PC, the Day Care: `GameEngine.OwnedPokemon`), given by one sweep, `GameEngine.GiveIds`, as a game begins, loads and saves and as one is received; whatever keeps Pokémon by id while the game is played runs it first. The same Pokémon keeps it (`Clone`, `CopyStateFrom`, an evolution, a hatching, a trade); what is another is made without one (`FrontierRules.ForBattle` clears its copy's). `ReceiveTradedPokemon` refuses an id already held. An older save is given its ids as it loads by `SaveMigration`'s first step (`SaveData.SaveVersion` 1).

### Rendering

The field and battles are 3D scenes; almost every asset is generated in code at start-up (meshes, textures, character rigs, Pokémon models, sound).

- **`RenderContext`** holds the GPU resources shared by both 3D renderers: the shaders (`FieldShaders`, GLSL embedded as strings), the shadow map, the scene target with a depth texture, and the half-resolution blur, bloom and ambient-occlusion buffers. Only one 3D scene renders per frame.
- **Frame order in `GameEngine.Draw`**: the active renderer's `Render(...)` runs first (shadow pass → scene pass → `PreparePost`), then `BeginTextureMode(virtualScreen)` opens and `DrawToScreen` / `DrawField` composites the scene through the post shader, with the 2D interface drawn on top.
- **Field**: `MapStructures.BuildingsOf` gives a map's buildings: found from the tile grid on a hand-made map (their kind from where the door leads, or from the map's `buildings` list), placed by the world's models on a map of the world; `MapScene.Build` turns a `Map` into cached meshes (ground baked by `PixelGround`, water, trees, tall grass, buildings, street furniture, furnished rooms). Water is a flat surface over the ground whose shader reads a mask `PixelGround` bakes (alpha = water, red = texels from the shore). `WorldRenderer` draws it with Platinum's field camera (pitch 59.05°, FOV 16.18°, taken from the decompilation), or with the original's camera for caves or its zoomed-in one where the map says so (`MapScene.ViewOf`: every place that needs the pitch, the lens or what is in view asks it, never the outdoor constants). Characters are baked to pixel sprites (`CharacterSprites`, with pixel faces stamped by `CharacterFaces`) and drawn as lit, shadow-casting billboards. One world unit is one tile, +Z is south, and pixel art is 32 texels per unit.
- **Reflections** (plan 09 · L10; style guide, "Water", "Puddles", "Quality presets"): what is mirrored where is `Graphics/Reflections.cs` (GPU-free: the water's plane in view, `PlaneIn`; whether the pass runs, `Wanted`; the mirrored camera; the straightening's numbers; the walker's tint on a puddle or a polished floor). On a preset that reflects (`QualityProfile.Reflections`: High and Medium), outdoors with water in view, `WorldRenderer.DrawMirror` draws the standing meshes (`SceneMeshes.DrawStanding`), things, Pokémon and people through the camera mirrored about the plane into `RenderContext.Mirror` (1920×1080) before the scene (`FrameSection.Reflections`), and the water program reads it per field texel as its material's second map (`MapScene.MirrorWater`, `texture1`). Something new that stands by water and should mirror is in `MeshPass.Opaque` or drawn by `DrawThings`, `DrawPokemon` or `DrawActors`; the scenery's vertex shader's `uprightBase` and the fragments' `mirrorClip` are nought outside the pass.
- **Streaming**: a streamed map is drawn a chunk at a time. `WorldRenderer.ScenesFor` keeps the block of three chunks by three round the camera, each a `MapScene` of its own 32×32 tiles. `MapScene.Prepare(map, chunk)` bakes and packs a chunk on a worker thread; `MapScene.Uploads` hands back its GPU steps, which the renderer runs on the main thread within 2.5 ms a frame (all at once for a chunk already in view, which only happens behind a fade); chunks more than 40 tiles behind the camera are freed. `WorldRenderer.Streaming` counts the chunks a frame had to wait for.
- **Relief** (`Graphics/Relief.cs`, GPU-free; style guide, "Relief"): the heights the ground is *drawn* at, which are not the heights it is walked at. The map's `GroundLevel` is drawn at zero, so the lowlands are exactly as flat as a map without heights; water is drawn at the whole tile above its own height, level with its banks; neighbouring tiles less than 0.75 apart are joined by a slope; anything more is a face (`MapScene.AddFaces`: earth under grass, rock under rock, a deck's beams). Everything that stands on the ground asks `Relief.At` for its height: trees, grass, props, buildings, people, the camera.
- **Life** (plan 04 · G9; style guide, "Life"): what moves in the field. `FieldLife` (GPU-free; owned by `WorldRenderer`, its clock run by `GameEngine` only while the field is on screen) is told what happens (`Footstep`, `Landing`, `Splash`, `Rainfall`, `OpenDoor`, `LeaveDoor`) and gives back the quads to draw (cells of the `LifeArt` atlas, tinted) and the frame of the one door that is moving. `GameEngine.OnStep` tells it of the player's steps (`Player.JustLanded`, `JustRodeOut`); footprints follow the tile's behaviour (`TileBehaviors.KeepsFootprints`), not its look. An open door is a card (`BuildingArt.OpenDoor`) over the shut one painted on the wall, placed by `WorldRenderer.DoorPlace`. Weather is a `FieldWeather` per area (`Map.WeatherAt`, read from the area's header by `Weathers.Of`): `WeatherFx` gives what falls and the layers of mist over the picture as functions of the time, `ArtLook.Weathered` changes the light, `Weathers.Wind` the sway, and `Weathers.IsRain` is what an evolution that waits for rain asks. Art that moves in place is redrawn frame by frame: `SceneTextures.AnimateWaterfall` for the waterfalls' shared sheet, `ArtSheet.PaintFrames` and `MapScene.Animate` for a face of a map's own sheet (a fountain, a turbine). `SceneTransition` holds the shapes that close over the field and open on a battle; `ForBattle` picks among them as Platinum does (who is met, and whether their first Pokémon is of a higher level than the player's first). Three entry points are there for plan 02's scenes: `GameEngine.ShowEmote` (a bubble over anyone's head), `PanCamera` / `ReleaseCamera`, and `Steering`, which walks the player in place of the keys.
- **Scene effects** (plan 09 · L11; style guide, "Life", "Scene effects"): what a story's scene lights on a tile with `effect <kind> X Y [seconds] [way] [wait|hold]` and `effect stop X Y` (`docs/scripts.md`): a burst of light (with a white flash over the picture), a beam, a rift, a shadow crossing the ground, a ripple of the whole picture, sparkles. `SceneEffect` and its lengths and sounds are `Overworld/SceneEffects.cs`; what each draws is `Graphics/SceneFx.cs` (GPU-free: `Quads` as a function of age, length, end, seed and way, `Flash`, `Ripple` and `RippleOffset`), from cells of 32 texels of their own atlas (`LifeArt.Scene`, `SceneTextures.Effects`). `FieldLife` keeps the effects lit beside its pieces, timed by its `Now` and seeded by their tile (`Effect`, `StopEffect`, `ClearEffects`, `SceneQuads`, `Flash`, `Ripple`; a held effect ends at infinity and a stopped one as it would at its end, a burst or a shadow at the end of its round), and `ChangePlace` clears them. `WorldRenderer.DrawSceneEffects` draws them in the 3D pass with life's, undimmed; the flash is a white rectangle after the composite, and the ripple is a uniform of the composite's own pass (`RenderContext.Composite(..., ripple)`, `FieldShaders.SetPost`: rows of 1/360 of the picture pushed by whole texels of 1/640 on a sine of 24 rows), so it costs no pass of its own; the battle and the title pass none. The script's `wait` is the runner's own wait (as a camera pan's), not the host's `Busy`. The scenes that use them: Spear Pillar (rifts, bursts, a ripple and Giratina's shadow), the torn pillar (sparkles, a ripple), the Distortion World's shadows (1F, B4F, Giratina's room), Team Galactic's control room (a beam held on each machine until the button, sparkles and bursts as the three go) and the explosion felt in Canalave's library (a ripple).
- **Set pieces** (plan 09 · L12; style guide, "Rooms", "Machines at work", and "Life", "Set pieces"; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "The original's set pieces"): where the original's scripts call a set piece by name, ours say `anim heal`, `anim pc on|off`, `anim elevator up|down [hums]`, `anim halloffame`, `shake <who> [times]` and `flicker <who> [times]` (`docs/scripts.md`). The rules are `Overworld/SetPieces.cs` (GPU-free): `SetPieces` is what each piece shows as a function of the time since it began (a machine's balls and whether they are lit, a PC's frame, a lift's indicator and its shudder in whole texels, a shaken person's offset, a flickering person seen or not, and its `Cues`, the sounds at their moments), and `SetPieceStage` (`WorldRenderer.Pieces`) is what plays now, timed by `FieldLife.Now` and forgotten by `ChangePlace`. `GameEngine.SetPieces.cs` is where a script's line meets the place: `IScriptHost.Anim`, `Shake` and `Flicker` return how long the piece takes, which the runner itself waits, and nothing where the place can't play it (no machine, nobody of that name on the map), so no script is left waiting; `RunTheSetPieces`, beside the field's clock, plays each cue once. A machine that changes is a **held face**: `ArtSheet.PaintFrames` with no rate (`KitBuilder.Held`) paints every frame, `MapScene.Hold(key, frame)` asks for one, and `Animate` redraws the face only when its frame changes (`FaceFrames`, GPU-free); `WorldRenderer.HoldFaces` tells a room's scene each frame what the stage has come to (`PropModels.HealerTray`, `HealerFront`, `LiftLights`, and `PcScreen(x, y)` for a PC that is used: one an `IsPCTerminal` stands on, `PropModels.IsTerminal`, dark until switched on, where a computer nobody uses keeps its lit screen). Whoever is shaken or flickers is drawn off their tile or not at all by `GatherActors` asking the stage; their tile, and who can be spoken to, never change. The count on the nurse's tray is the Pokémon that are no Eggs (the original's `CountPartyNonEggs`), the Hall of Fame's the whole team's. The PC's two sounds are the piece's, not `PCScreen`'s: a script that opens a PC switches it on first and off afterwards, as `common.PC` does on every way out.
- **The ways into a battle** (plan 09 · L13; style guide, "Life", "Into battle"): a battle begun in the field says whom it is with and where in a `BattleMeeting` (`Battle/BattleMeeting.cs`, GPU-free: the trainers' classes and names or the wild Pokémon's species, two a side or not, the `BattleKind`, the Frontier's, a link battle, the battle's `BattleTerrain` and the two first levels), which `GameEngine.MeetWildPokemon`, `StartTrainerBattle` and `StartFrontierBattle` build. `BattleMeeting.Row` is the original's `EncEffects_GetEffectPair` (`MeetingRow`: its order and its quirks kept, the first trainer's class alone read; `ClassRow` is `EncEffects_TrainerClassEffect` over the class words of `trainers.json`), and `SceneTransition.ForBattle(meeting)` gives the row its way, or the way by place (`Local`, the original's `CutInEffects_ForBattle`: water, a cave, every other ground, each stronger when their first Pokémon's level is above the player's first). `IntoBattleTests` holds the choice to the original's lines row by row. The species' rows (a legendary's and a mythical's camera) are L14's and go into `Row` before its check for two at once. A way is a `TransitionKind` (added at the enum's end) with its lead and its closing in `SceneTransition.Pace` (`OutSeconds` is the flashes, the lead and the closing: the first four still take `CloseSeconds`, the twelve of L13 and the two bands about what the original's frames come to), its dark in `CoversByWay` and `DrawDark` (`UI/SceneTransition.Ways.cs`), which must answer alike, and whatever rides over the picture as the field closes in `Pieces` (a `TransitionPiece`: a Poké Ball, the Frontier's mark, the Galactic mark or one of its shards, each a function of the kind and the time, which `Draw` only draws), with `Ripple` (the water's waver, handed to `WorldRenderer.DrawToScreen` for the field's own composite) and `Veil` (the boss's dimming). The battle opens on the dark's own shape in reverse, or through the iris after a wild Pokémon, with nothing riding over it. A cave's ring closes on the player wherever the field shows them (`WorldRenderer.PlayerOnPicture`, the `focus` of `Covers` and `Draw`). The theories of `FieldLifeTests` over every `TransitionKind` take a new kind by themselves: it covers nothing at 0, everything at 1 and more as it closes.
- **The bands** (plan 09 · L15; style guide, "Life", "Into battle", "Bands"; `docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "The bands of the Gym Leaders, the Elite Four and the Champion"): `MeetingRow.Leader` is met on `TransitionKind.LeaderBand` and `EliteFour` and `Champion` on `EliteBand`, rematch teams included, since the row is the class's; in a double battle the band is lost, as in the original, and plan 04 · G9's diamonds (`TransitionKind.Leader`) are gone with decision 5. A band is one look, not pieces: `SceneTransition.BandAt(kind, seconds, width, height)` gives a `BandLook` (`UI/SceneTransition.Bands.cs`, GPU-free: how far the band has opened, its speed lines' run in whole units, where each portrait's foot is and whether they are lit, the dark round the band, the name's place and alpha, the mark's, the flash, the white) by the kind's `Beats` (a `BandBeats`, the style guide's table), and `DrawBand` only draws it. It closes to `SceneTransition.White` as a shape (`CoverOf`; a bar from the band's middle row for a Leader, a slash along the rising diagonal for the League), which `CoversByWay` and `DrawDark` answer for like any dark, and the battle opens on the same shape in reverse: no frame between is dark.
